# Changelog

## Unreleased

- Lightroom imports bring the whole keyword list too: keywords no photo carries are kept in the Keyword List with their hierarchy, and names holding `,`, `;`, `>`, `<` or `"` stay one keyword. The import check names what has no counterpart here instead of dropping it silently: keywords Lightroom leaves out of exports (Rawtonic exports them), keywords exported without their parents, person keywords (imported as keywords) and names holding `|` (imported as levels); synonyms are counted as not imported.

- HDR HEIF and AVIF photos (PQ or HLG, such as a Canon HDR PQ HIF) no longer clip everything brighter than diffuse white in the SDR edit: from 0.75 of diffuse white up to the HDR reference peak (1000 cd/m²) the highlights roll off smoothly into white, so clouds, lamps and speculars keep their detail. Diffuse white (203 cd/m²) now shows at 0.945 of white (241 of 255) instead of white, tones below the roll-off are unchanged, and the brighter values stay in the photo, so lowering Exposure, Highlights or Whites brings them back. On an iPhone HDR scene re-encoded as PQ and HLG, clipped pixels fall from 12 % to 0 %. New edits and never-edited photos get it (`renderVersion` 20); saved edits and all other photos render as before.

- The bulk Capture One crop action uses count-neutral English and Polish labels, including when only one photo needs the action.

- Capture One's Exposure on non-RAW photos (JPEG, TIFF, PNG) imports with Capture One's response instead of the same number of Rawtonic stops. Capture One scales each channel's file value by 2^(E/1.8) and clips at white. An Adobe RGB or ProPhoto photo gets Rawtonic's Exposure scaled to the same law (Adobe RGB +1 becomes +1.22, ProPhoto stays +1). An sRGB or untagged photo gets that law as its RGB curve, with Exposure at 0. Measured against Capture One 16.9.1 on 16-bit TIFF and 8-bit JPEG neutral ramps, with filled layers at −2…+2 and the photo's own Exposure at −1: ΔE00 median 0.00–0.02 (was 0.4–3.2), and colour charts at the two editors' base difference, except where Exposure clips. Saturated Adobe RGB colours at +2 still differ (Capture One clips in the file's primaries; median 4.7, max 41). The photo's own Exposure above 0 clips the brightest tones that Capture One rolls off (ramp max ΔE00 about 6). The import report does not list each such photo. It names the photos that keep Rawtonic's response: a LUT colour profile or one whose channels have different tone curves, an AVIF or HEIF file, or an sRGB photo with Capture One RGB or colour curves. A profile Rawtonic cannot read is treated as sRGB, as Rawtonic renders the photo. RAW photos are unchanged.

- Changing the crop ratio fits the largest crop of the new ratio into the photo, or into the crop you last drew, resized or moved by hand, around the crop's centre: the result no longer depends on the ratios chosen in between (16:9 → 1:1 → 3:2 keeps the whole 3:2 frame, 7360 × 4907 on a 7360 × 4912 photo, instead of the square's area, 6016 × 4011), and going back to the drawn crop's own ratio returns that crop. Single ratio changes from the full frame are unchanged. Undo and redo bring back the crop the next ratio change fits into.

- Faster Shadows and Highlights drags on photos with their own tone curve (DNG profiles and camera profiles with a curve): tone version 23's anchors read the curve's segments the CPU finds once per render instead of searching the 48-entry curve for every pixel. Metal, 2560 × 1707 preview with readback: a Shadows drag costs +0.1 ms p50 over version 22 (was +4.4–5.5 ms; p50 12.9–16.2 → 8.7–9.8 ms in four runs on a shared host), Highlights +0.0 ms (was +1.1–1.4 ms). Exports and previews are byte-identical on Metal (326 preservation exports, 90 tone version 23 exports, 192 GPU renders); Vulkan and DirectX were not compared.

- Windows with AMD graphics (Vulkan), first start after an install, an update or a new graphics driver: after the first photo is shown, the order of the shaders that are still being prepared changes. The detail pass the shown photo's frames read compiles first: its recipe's own pass when the photo is already on its way to the screen, otherwise the one a new RAW edit reads. Shaders prepared ahead of use (effect passes, other detail passes) give way to the ones a frame needs. A Subject mask computed ahead no longer starts while the start holds back the shader for the Basic-panel sliders, so that shader no longer waits behind the mask (the Radeon 680M logs show it waiting 12 s). The effect on the Radeon 680M has not been measured yet. Masks computed ahead are ready later on such a cold start, but a click on Subject still goes first. Other computers are unchanged.

- Rawtonic no longer hides other apps in Open With for RAW files, and on macOS installing it no longer replaces the default app of a file type that already has one: it is offered for camera RAWs through the system's camera RAW type, so Preview and the other RAW programs stay listed and the default stays the system's or yours, and it is an alternative for JPEG, PNG, GIF, BMP, TIFF and EXR. Formats macOS does not know (Sigma X3F, Kodak KDC, QOI, …) now list Rawtonic too; for a format no other installed app opens, it is the default. Opening a file Rawtonic cannot read yet this way (such as a Nikon NEFX pixel-shift merge or a Sony AXR) shows a message naming the file instead of trying to develop it.

- CLI exports find catalog photos through the app’s recent libraries or `--catalog`, retaining their saved edits, catalog metadata, corrected capture times and sticky AI-generated-content marking after an external editor strips XMP; ambiguous ownership requires an explicit choice.

- Colour-noise preview tests use an injected memory-pressure level, so frame-marker checks stay reliable on a busy host; warning and critical pressure still disable the production lookup.
- The second window at Fit, when it is larger than the main window's photograph, shows the edit sharp about 57–89 ms after a settled edit on macOS (p50 to its frame's submission to the window server, was about 350–370 ms): its own fit frame keeps its upload and colour noise pyramid from edit to edit and starts at once after the main window's frame. Pixels are unchanged.

- Edit With also renders photos with generated areas (object removal, generative fill). The render is marked as containing AI-generated content (IPTC Digital Source Type in its XMP, as any export of it), and the library marks the stacked render for good when it registers it: the mark stays when the other program saves the file without it, and a metadata edit or Use XMP does not remove it. The "Source type" search finds the render, and its exports are marked, also with metadata stripped (from the app, and from `rawtonic export` for photos of a session). Libraries are upgraded to schema 37 when opened.

- Virtual copies are photos of their own in the Library, as in Lightroom: a copy has its own pick or reject flag (a new copy starts with its photo's; existing copies keep the flag they showed), an ordinary album can hold a copy without its photo (adding or removing a copy leaves the photo as it is), smart albums have a "Is a virtual copy" rule (Lightroom's Copy Status rules now import), and the value lists in the filter bar count copies like the grid lists them. Lightroom imports bring each copy's own pick and put copies into their albums as copies (they used to become their photo).

- Camera rules with "contains", "starts with" or "ends with" also find a camera by the names its files state, so rules saved with the JPEG spelling (`contains NIKON`) keep matching that camera's photos after the one-camera-name change; Lightroom "Camera starts with / ends with" smart collections now import.

- The second window at 100 % shows the sharp region about 118 ms after a settled edit on macOS (p50 117.6 ms to the log line written just after its present, 96 edits on a shared host; was about 395 ms): it keeps its region's upload and colour noise pyramid from edit to edit, develops at once after a single edit, first only what it shows with a turn of its own at the GPU; once the window rests, the margin around it for short pans follows in the background (not on Linux). Each region is developed as from a fresh upload; its 8-bit display dither may differ by one code from before where the region now starts elsewhere. Exports are unchanged.

- Smart albums and the Library search list a virtual copy by its own rating, colour label and keywords, as Lightroom does: a five-star copy of a one-star photo is in "rating ≥ 4" without its photo, and a copy shows up in a smart album's grid and count.

- A camera's RAW and JPEG files are one camera in the Library: the Camera list, searches and smart albums use one make and model for both (e.g. `Nikon` / `D750`, not also `NIKON CORPORATION` / `NIKON D750` for the JPEG). Existing libraries are renamed when opened, so a smart album saved from the JPEG spelling ("Camera is NIKON D750") now also contains that camera's RAW files. Lightroom smart collections with "Camera is" (and "is not") now import, except for a camera Rawtonic names once for several models Lightroom tells apart (such as Olympus Stylus 1 and 1s), which the import report lists as not imported.

- Save As replaces the file you confirmed with the save dialog's Replace, in one atomic step; the photo, its sidecars and library photos stay refused.

- Canon CR3 catalog facts retain capture date and camera information when image data follows the metadata, so import preset `{year}` uses the capture year.

- Capture One catalog imports retain shifted capture times and the camera’s timezone offset, leave equal times uncorrected, and defer unknown camera facts until they can be read.

- Assigning a keyboard shortcut gives it to the new command, clears its previous owner, and names that owner in Preferences.

- Linux: an Arch Linux package recipe (`rawtonic-bin`) and a Flatpak manifest in `packaging/linux/`, both built from the release AppImage and checked in fresh containers (not published yet); inside a Flatpak Rawtonic makes no update request — Flatpak delivers updates — and an unpacked AppDir started through its AppRun resolves relative paths against the folder the command ran in.

### Highlights

- **B&W Toning: one place to tone a black & white photo.** The Black & white tool has a Toning part: a Highlights tone and a Shadows tone (hue, saturation, density), Balance (where the highlights tone starts) and Paper (how far it reaches into white). By default toning keeps the grey's lightness wherever sRGB can hold the tint (a strong warm tint of very light greys gives up a little lightness to stay in sRGB) and leaves black and white neutral; Density darkens the toned parts like a toner (the deepest shadows keep their order), Paper tints white like a toned paper base. Measured against Capture One 16.9.1's B&W split toning (saturation up to 60) on a grey ramp and two RAW photos, with the settings fitted to it: ΔE00 median 0.9–1.3 on the ramp, 0.8–1.2 and 1.5–2.0 on the photos (Capture One's own plain B&W against ours: 0.5, 0.7 and 1.4), where Color Grading reached 5.6–15; strong blue highlight toning (Capture One 240° at 60) reaches 1.3 only as a toning-only fit on Capture One's own plain ramp (about 1.8 with the same settings on ours, before output mapping). Capture One styles with B&W split toning now import into it (on the same ramp and photos ΔE00 median 1.3–3.3 instead of 1.6–15 through Color Grading; not yet as close as the tool can get). Lightroom presets keep toning with Color Grading; Camera Raw's B&W toning was not rendered for a side-by-side (the Lightroom-type behaviour here is Rawtonic's own Color Grading on greys).

- **Open iPhone HEIC photos on macOS.** HEIC and HEIF files (iPhone `.heic`, Canon, Sony and Fujifilm `.hif`) now open, import and export like JPEGs. macOS decodes them with its own HEVC decoder, so Rawtonic bundles no codec. 10-bit files are read above 8 bits (on test files written by macOS: within half a 10-bit step), Display P3 colour is kept (including colours outside sRGB), the photo is turned the way the file says, and the capture date and camera come from its EXIF. The import dialog previews reuse the file's embedded thumbnail when it is large enough, else they are made from the photo. HDR photos with a gain map show their standard (SDR) image; the HDR part is not used yet. On Windows and Linux such a file now says "can't be opened on this system yet" instead of being skipped. Not checked yet: real iPhone and camera files, and a side-by-side with Lightroom and Capture One.
- **Export: limit a JPEG's file size.** "Limit file size to … KB" in the Export panel's file settings (saved with each recipe; `rawtonic export --max-file-kb N`, 1 KB = 1000 bytes) writes each JPEG, mask images included, with a high quality whose whole file, metadata, colour profile and any signed Content Credentials included, fits the limit. A binary search of at most 7 encodes looks for it. JPEG file size does not always fall monotonically with the quality, so this is a best effort: in an unusual image a slightly higher quality that also fits can be skipped. The quality field then reads "auto". If no tried quality fits, the smallest file tried is written (nearly always the lowest quality), the picture is never resized, and the export says so: the queue row of the job and the Activity centre (a warning that stays until dismissed) list each file as "IMG_0001.jpg: could not reach 500 KB; saved the smallest file (690 KB)" (a photo's main file and each mask file separately), and the CLI prints the same line and lists the files under `sizeLimitMissed`. Without the option, exports are unchanged, byte for byte.
- **Export: "If a file exists" — Keep both, Skip or Replace if changed.** A new choice in the Export panel's Destination, saved with each export recipe (and `rawtonic export --on-conflict keep-both|skip|replace-if-changed`).
  - **Keep both** (the default) works as before: the new file gets a numbered name (`photo_1.jpg`) and nothing is written over.
  - **Skip** leaves out every photo whose file already exists. This is decided before the photo is decoded or rendered, so re-exporting a folder only renders the new photos. The queue and Activity say how many were skipped.
  - **Replace if changed** re-exports a photo only when its edit, its keywords or other metadata, the recipe's settings, its LUT or watermark image file, or the decoding and tone-mapper preferences changed since Rawtonic last exported it to that file (or when Rawtonic was updated); unchanged photos are skipped. If the photo still follows its Develop defaults, changes to the defaults it uses also count, including a change to the default LUT in effect or its file contents. Handy for a folder that syncs to a client gallery or the web: only changed photos are uploaded again. The old file is replaced in one step, so a crash or full disk never leaves half a file.
  - Rawtonic only ever replaces **its own earlier export of the same photo at the same path**, and only if that file is still exactly as Rawtonic wrote it. An original, a camera JPEG next to the RAW, a file any library Rawtonic knows lists as a photo (even an earlier export you added to a catalog), a file from another program, an export retouched elsewhere, or the export of another photo that later took the same file name is never replaced: the new file gets a numbered name and the queue says why. Rawtonic keeps that record in its own data folder, not next to your exports. Exports made before this version are not in the record, so the first Replace if changed run keeps both for them.
  - **Ask** asks once per export, before anything is rendered or written, when files with these names already exist: a short list says how many are your unchanged earlier exports, how many are earlier exports that have changed, and how many are other files (with a few names each), and you answer Keep both, Skip or Replace if changed for the whole export, or Cancel to export nothing. No file in the way, no question. Originals and files a library lists as photos are never offered for replacement. The command line cannot ask: `--on-conflict ask` is refused.
  - Not covered yet: with separate mask files on, a changed photo is kept both instead of replaced.

- **Export: Save As… for one photo.** With exactly one photo selected, the Export dialog has a Save As… button beside Export. It opens the system's save dialog with the name the naming template gives and the format's extension; change the name, the folder or (by typing another image extension, or where the dialog offers a file type) the format, and that one file is exported with the dialog's other settings. The form's folder, subfolder and folder preservation do not apply to it; When you click Replace in the save dialog, that file is replaced, whatever "If a file exists" says and without a second question: the new file is written beside it and swapped in in one step once it is complete, so an export that fails or is cancelled before the swap (a crash or a full disk while it is written included) leaves the old file as it was, and so does Cancel up to the swap; the swap itself either happens whole or not at all. (With separate mask files, the mask images are written after the main file is swapped in; one failing then leaves the new main file.) Should that file change after you confirmed it (a sync client, another program; told by its content) before the export starts, it is not replaced and "If a file exists" decides as for any export (Keep both writes a numbered file); should it change, or be removed, while the export renders, the export fails, names the file and leaves it as it is. With separate mask files, a file already at one of the new file's mask names was not part of the question: Keep both (and Ask's Keep both) numbers the whole set before anything is written, Skip writes nothing. The naming template only proposes the name: a template that cannot name the photo no longer stops a valid name typed in the dialog. The photo's original (also reached through a linked folder), its sidecars, any camera RAW (also one a camera wrote as `.TIF`, and a TIFF that cannot be read to tell), the camera JPEG a library keeps with a RAW and any photo a library lists are refused with a message, even after Replace, and nothing is written. The chosen folder is where the next export dialog opens; recipes are unchanged. With several photos selected or several recipes ticked, Save As is not offered (several photos) or disabled (several recipes).

- **Edit With: each program keeps its own settings.** "Settings for <program>…" in the Edit With menu (the gear at a program's row, or Settings for a program in the menu bar's Edit With) sets that program's file format (TIFF, JPEG, PNG), bit depth, colour space, size (and whether to enlarge), JPEG quality and metadata (creator contact, GPS coordinates) — for example 16-bit ProPhoto TIFFs for GIMP or Photoshop and 8-bit sRGB JPEGs for a quick retouch app. A program without its own settings uses the default recipe, as before; its first save starts from that recipe, and "Use default recipe" goes back to it. The menu shows a program's own settings in its row. Kept in Rawtonic's settings folder, not in recipes or libraries; existing settings stay as they are.

- **Hot folder and tethering apply an import metadata preset.** Next to the next-capture rating and keywords, the hot folder (and the tether panel) offer the same metadata preset as the import dialog: creator, copyright (with `{year}`), usage terms, credit, description, contact details and keywords, by the same rules (fill empty fields, or replace those marked Overwrite). It is applied to every capture in the same step that registers it, stored in the library and the XMP sidecar next to the copy; the watched file is never changed. The choice is kept with the hot folder and shown again when it is resumed. Orientation is not offered: cameras write it. Tested with generated files only (no real camera file); not compared side by side with Lightroom's Auto Import yet.

- **Canon CR3 photos decode faster.** Rawtonic now uses the current CR3 decoder of the open-source dnglab project. Decompressing the raw data of a CR3 takes less than half as long (five files of the EOS R5, R, R7, R6 Mark II and M6 Mark II on an M5 Pro: 34–63 ms instead of 75–144 ms), and the whole step from the file's bytes in memory to the developed linear image, demosaicing included, 11–21 % less (190–347 ms instead of 228–438 ms). Reading the file and showing the photo are not included in these times; how much sooner a CR3 appears in the app was not measured. The pixels are the same: all 110 raw.pixls.us CR3 samples (26 bodies, RAW and C-RAW) decode identically, and saved edits export as before. None of these samples is split into several rows of tiles, so the decoder's handling of such files is untested: it now places a lower tile row correctly and reports an error instead of returning wrong pixels where its wavelet step has no line ready.
- **New cameras: Canon EOS Ra, Nikon D810A and Samsung GX-1S.** Their camera data come from dnglab, with Adobe's colour matrices. No sample file of these cameras was available, so they are not checked on real photos yet. DNGs of these three cameras now carry the camera's own name ("Nikon D810A" instead of the file's "NIKON CORPORATION NIKON D810A"); a camera default you set for such DNGs before keeps applying.

### Fixes

- **First visit of a folder of never-edited RAWs on a network share makes their thumbnails faster (tested: macOS smbfs over Wi-Fi, headless tile jobs, 500 RAWs: CR3, NEF, RAF, ORF).** A never-edited RAW's thumbnail is the camera's embedded preview. It was read from the share page by page, each page waiting for the network. Rawtonic now first reads the file's structure, finds where the preview and the camera-crop data lie with the same parsers that then read them, and reads just those bytes in a few large reads (2–6 per file): the head, the JPEG, Canon's timed metadata, and the structure the decoder reads (an ORF's maker note, a RAF's tag block). Reading the tiles' bytes of 500 RAWs took 26.5–54.1 s and now 19.0–29.0 s, at the link's speed (interleaved runs; the link's speed varied between them). It reads 1.00–1.02 instead of 1.16–1.21 GB. Making the 500 thumbnails took 48.8–68.2 s and now 35.9–51.6 s (release build, busy machine). ARW, CR2, DNG, PEF, RW2 and SRW previews are read the same way (checked on 85 files of 10 formats on the share: at most three 16 KB pages read outside the read-ahead). Containers it does not know, a structure spread over the file, or a damaged length are read as before. On a local SSD the reads are not slower (500 RAWs: 1.4–1.8 s before, 0.7–1.1 s now). Thumbnails are byte-identical, and nothing else is changed: Develop, exports and previews of edited photos.

- **Thumbnails of never-edited RAWs open much faster from a network share (tested: macOS smbfs over Wi-Fi, headless lookups).** Looking up 500 never-edited RAWs whose thumbnails (the camera's embedded preview) were already made took 36.5–45.9 s on the development build before this change, now 0.29–0.65 s. The median per photo was 56–67 ms, now under 0.7 ms. Each lookup opened the RAW on the share three or four times to check whether it was available. That check only matters when an older thumbnail could stand in for a disconnected photo or a stored preview has to be checked. It is now asked only then, and at most once per lookup. A lookup also stats the RAW twice instead of 9.5 times. Measured on an earlier build (one open per lookup before this change), the lookups of a folder opened for the first time went from 18.9 s to 0.06 s, and making a thumbnail opens its RAW once instead of two or three times. Making the thumbnails still reads each camera preview from the share through page faults (500 RAWs in about 80–100 s over Wi-Fi); that is not changed here. Thumbnails and exports are unchanged.

- **Thumbnails of edited photos that were already made open much faster from a network share (tested: macOS smbfs over Wi-Fi).** Looking up 500 edited photos whose thumbnails were already made took 22.7–30.5 s, now 0.14–0.70 s; the median per photo was 36–46 ms per run, now under 1 ms. For each such thumbnail Rawtonic opened two files on the share. It opened the RAW, only to check that it was available, before it looked for the thumbnail on the local disk. It also read the photo's sidecar again right after the folder listing had read it. Now the local thumbnail is looked for first. Where the folder listing reads a photo's `.rrdata` sidecar file, the grid now reuses that read. This covers photos in a plain folder and virtual copies. The reuse lasts as long as the sidecar's size and modification time stay the same, and on macOS and Linux its file identity too. Opening a folder still reads those sidecars from the share. A sidecar whose size or time changes, or on macOS and Linux one replaced by another file, is read again. A photo whose listing comes from a session's or catalog's own records has its sidecar read first by the grid, as before. Thumbnails taken from the camera's embedded preview (never-edited RAWs): see the entry above. Thumbnails and exports are unchanged.
- **HEIC from Android phones keeps its metadata.** A HEIC whose EXIF block is larger than 64 KB (a Xiaomi photo in our tests) lost all of it: no capture time at import, no Make, Model or exposure data in the export. Large EXIF blocks are read now. Exporting a HEIC or other source with a tile layout in its metadata as a TIFF with metadata no longer writes a TIFF macOS and other readers cannot open.
- HDR merges with ghost removal (new merges; merged files you saved stay as they are): where a frame had pixels dropped because they disagreed with the reference (a sun glint or ripple that moved), the rest of that frame no longer counts in the coarse comparison that decides whether the next frame is a ghost there. So moving water follows the reference frame more closely. On hand-held sunrise brackets with glints on water, 29,301 pixels at Medium and 8,621 at High change by more than 0.1 EV, at least 99.7 % of them towards the reference. On a hand-held sea bracket, 525 pixels at Medium do, all of them towards the reference. The measured counts of dark holes in clipped glints and of dark rims around them did not increase on these brackets. On six synthetic brackets with a known answer (five without motion, one with a moving patch), the merged pixels at Low and Medium are identical to before; at High, up to 0.08 % of the pixels change by at most 0.008 EV, and the error against the known answer (99th and 99.9th percentile, relative RMS) agrees to four decimal places. With ghost removal off, nothing changes.
- After a lost graphics device on a computer whose shader compiles run one at a time (AMD Radeon 680M on Windows), the multi view sometimes stayed soft with "Preparing graphics for this computer" for 35 s and more. The photographs' display variant had to compile again on the new device, and that compile waited for a Subject mask Rawtonic was computing ahead of a click. A compile that the shown photographs wait for now starts beside masks computed ahead, and those computations are asked to stop. Masks you asked for (a click, a pasted or shown mask) still go first. An export started while such a mask's model loads still waits for that load, as before.
- Windows with AMD graphics (Vulkan): on the first start after an install, an update or a new graphics driver, the first photo no longer waits for shaders prepared ahead that it does not need. The Basic-panel shader that compiles in a helper process for later edits, and the detail passes, now start only once the first photo's frame has its shaders (or, with no photo open, once the first shader is ready). A first photo that already needs the Basic-panel shader, such as one with saved edits, still has it compiled at once. On every computer, the effect passes prepared ahead for Glow, Halation and Grain now start only after the frame's own effect passes are ready. On a Radeon 680M, two of those compiles had pushed the first frame from 11 s to 17 s in two of three cold starts. The gain on that laptop is not measured yet.
- **A saved version keeps its preview when Rawtonic decodes RAWs differently.** After an update that changes how RAWs decode for new edits (such as the dead-photosite repair of `renderVersion` 18), Build Previews or opening a never-edited photo made its Standard and 1:1 previews again for the new decode and replaced the old ones, so a virtual copy (or any saved edit) still using the older decode could no longer be opened while the RAW's drive was unplugged. Rawtonic now keeps the previews of every older decode that a saved edit of the photo still uses beside the new ones, and removes them once no saved edit uses that decode any more. They count towards the preview cache size and go with Discard 1:1 Previews, the Standard preview size limit and clearing the cache like other previews. Opening with the RAW available is unchanged. Previews already replaced before this fix cannot be brought back without the RAW.
- Automatic keyword suggestions respect the requested total count, including parent and colour keywords, and keep a stable order.

- Hold Shift while dragging a curve point for finer control; clicks within 12 pixels grab the nearest point, including endpoints.

- Headless exports and CLI progress finish normally when a downstream command closes its output pipe.

- RAW-only library filters keep matching photos visible when RAW+JPEG grouping prefers JPEG.

- After importing a Capture One session or a Lightroom catalog into an open catalog, the imported photographs' folder in the Folders panel and the Previous Import album ("Last import") now appear at once. Before, they stayed missing until the app was restarted. The folder list now also follows a hot-folder capture, a tethered shot or a folder synchronization that adds photographs outside the listed folders.
- Capture One catalog and session import reports name the photos whose Color Editor settings were approximated or partly left out.
- After a lost graphics device (driver reset), a collage cell whose render met the loss is rendered once more on the new device, as the export queue does, instead of failing the collage. In a multi view, a zoomed cell shows its cached thumbnail enlarged at once on the new device, no longer the whole photo at fit until its region develops again.
- **Capture One style imports: Color Editor uniformity and Skin Tone amounts come over.** Uniformity (Homogenität) on an Advanced range was dropped, and on a Skin Tone range it came over at a small fraction of Capture One's strength; both now even colours out towards the picked colour (an Advanced range takes the picked colour as the target of each uniformity it has; with hue uniformity and asymmetric hue edges the import report names the Colour Editor as partial). Skin Tone hue and lightness amounts came over about twice as strong as Capture One's and the Skin Tone selection was narrower; they now follow Capture One's renders. Measured at uniformity 100 and amounts ±5 only (ΔE00 median in the area Capture One changes): on one photo's Advanced styles, which the uniformity scale was fitted on, hue / saturation / lightness uniformity 1.05 / 0.42 / 1.28 (dropped: 2.49 / 1.31 / 2.69); on two portraits held out from that fit, Skin Tone uniformity hue 1.43–1.59 (was 1.66–1.85), saturation 0.50–0.51 (was 1.13–1.20), lightness 1.26–1.29 (was 1.72–2.55); Skin Tone hue and lightness amounts 0.21–0.36 on the portrait held out from the amount fit (was 0.61–1.38). Skin Tone saturation amounts ±5 change almost nothing in Capture One and could not be fitted. Not closed: hue and lightness uniformity stay above 1 ΔE00 median, other strengths are not measured. Only new imports change; saved edits render as before.
- **The Library grid fills faster when you scroll through a large folder, and uses less memory.** Making a grid tile is cheaper: the reduction to tile size now reads each row of the camera's preview once for a band of tile rows instead of once per filter tap, and turns the result into display colours on all cores. Tiles that were already made from a camera's preview JPEG are bit for bit the same as before. Olympus and OM System RAWs now show the preview JPEG the camera stores in the file (3200 × 2400 px on bodies from the E-30 to the OM-3), as other cameras' RAWs already did, instead of being developed from the sensor data for their tile; until you edit them their tiles therefore show the camera's colours, as for other cameras, and tiles of such photos made by an earlier version are made again once (an offline photo keeps its old tile). For a photo shot with another aspect ratio set in the camera (16:9, for example) the tile is cut from the camera's 4:3 preview to that frame, as Develop shows it. Where no aspect ratio set in the camera cuts it, the same preview now also appears first when such a photo is selected and in the import dialog's large view. Scrolling the whole grid of 1,000 RAWs (29 cameras, 14 % Olympus) until every tile is made: 36.2–36.9 s → 23.1–23.9 s on an M5 Pro (3 alternating runs each of this version and the one before), peak memory 1.50–1.52 → 1.24–1.27 GB. Not changed: DNGs without a preview are still developed for their tile; exports are unaffected.
- For a capture without an embedded date, a metadata preset gets its copyright year from the source file's verified modification time.
- Linux Edit With follows applications-directory precedence when reading MIME associations, so a system removal leaves a higher-priority user desktop entry available.
- When a hot folder retries registering a verified RAW copy, it uses the watched file's XMP sidecar. It verifies or copies that sidecar beside the RAW copy before applying the metadata preset.
- Start watching waits for the hot folder's metadata preset to finish saving so the watch keeps its saved preset ID.
- An automatic sidecar repair never overwrites a newer save.
- **The Library on a network share no longer reads each never-edited RAW's camera on every start while Develop defaults are set (tested: Linux, kio-fuse SMB, shaped link).** To pick a photo's camera default, each tile read the RAW's camera tags from the share once per app start: 45–90 ms per RAW at 110 MB/s, 80–190 ms at 40 MB/s. Rawtonic now keeps the names a file gives its camera in its own cache folder (never next to your photos), for the file as it is (path, size, modification time and file id); the next start reads them from there (12 test RAWs at 110 MB/s: 746 ms → 2 ms in all), and the camera's name is made from them as before, so the result is identical (2171 test files of 14 formats). Checking whether a tile may show the camera's embedded preview no longer reads the camera when its Standard preview is current (making a never-edited tile still reads it once, then it is kept). A missing or damaged store just means the files are read again. A Library tile also reads each sidecar once per lookup and render instead of up to three times (about 22 ms per read at 110 MB/s, 52 ms at 40 MB/s). Not measured in the running app (engine calls only); kernel CIFS, macOS and Windows shares were not measured.
- Restoring or replacing a RAW at the same path refreshes its base look the next time it is read. A damaged file's embedded preview no longer leaves a stale look that lasts until restart.
- **Merging the same photos again gives the same file.** Two merges of the same photos could differ in a few pixels (in a hand-held 5-frame HDR, 0.004 % of the pixels by up to one step of the file's precision; in an HDR panorama up to 4.6 %), because the alignment added its numbers in an order that changed with how the work was spread over the processor cores. That order is now fixed, and so is the order of the two colour matrices written into the file, which could swap with some cameras. Tested: repeated HDR, HDR panorama, panorama and focus-stack merges of real photos with 1 to 16 threads gave byte-identical files. Already merged files are not changed.
- **Develop opens photos from a network share up to 2–4× faster (tested: Linux, kio-fuse SMB, shaped link).** Opening a photo in Develop read the file 2–8 times over the share: the decode once, then each fact of its base look (camera, image shape, raw tags, a DNG's tags) the whole file again. The base look is now read from the decode's own buffer, and the decode reads the file ahead in parallel (not on a card). A recipe with diffraction correction or a Kelvin white balance still reads the file once more for those (once between them, no longer once each). Develop open of 12 test RAWs at 110 MB/s: 44.5 → 18.5 s in all (a 26 MB ARW 3.4 → 1.7 s, 103 → 29 MB read); at 40 MB/s, 4 of them 54 → 15 s. Library thumbnails of edited photos read their base look from the decode's buffer too (diffraction correction or a Kelvin white balance may still read the file once), and the import dialog's thumbnails of RAWs without a usable preview are read ahead with their facts from the dialog's buffer (import, 12 RAWs at 110 MB/s: 18.3 → 8.3 s). Everywhere else (export, Auto tools) these facts, a DNG's tags included, now read the file once between them instead of once each. Checking whether a Standard preview is current no longer reads the RAW's frames when the preview recorded them for the file as it is now (same size and time): 100–160 ms per CR3, ARW or RAF tile at 110 MB/s. Other steps of a tile (an edited photo's base look, for one) may still read the RAW. Local disk: no change within noise. The facts came out identical on 268 test RAWs. Not changed: kernel CIFS, macOS and Windows shares were not measured.
- **Graphics no longer freeze for good after a graphics driver error.** On integrated graphics (such as a Radeon 680M on Windows) a long render could make the driver reset the graphics chip, and every view then stayed frozen until Rawtonic was restarted. Integrated graphics now draw the Develop view in smaller pieces, so each piece is shorter. This covers edits whose recipe has render version 2 or later (every edit started since 20 October 2026); an edit started earlier keeps its render version and the larger pieces. In the display cases we tested (whole photo and regions, film grain, previews), the picture stays byte-for-byte the same. If the driver resets the chip anyway, Rawtonic starts the graphics again by itself, keeps your last picture on screen, redraws the photo, the multi view (zoomed cells stay at their zoom) and the Second Window, and says so once. During a batch export, a photo whose files were not yet written is exported once more on the restarted graphics. Your edits are never affected. After three resets within five minutes Rawtonic stops restarting the graphics and asks you to restart the app. Graphics work that takes long is now noted in the log.
- **Multi view after a graphics driver error: the active photo no longer goes blank.** While the graphics restart, the photo you are working on in Multi view shows its cached preview where it was on screen (same zoom and position) until its sharp picture is back, as the other cells already do. With the Crop tool open on it (it was blank for about 1.9 s in a Windows QA run on a Radeon 680M laptop with a simulated graphics error), the cached preview of the cropped photo is shown inside the crop frame, where those pixels were, until the uncropped picture is back, also while a crop edge or the straightening is being dragged; a preview made before the latest crop change keeps its shape there instead of being stretched (not yet checked on the Radeon 680M).
- **One camera, one name for its Develop defaults: RAW, DNG conversions and JPEG/HEIF of a body are the same camera.** A camera's JPEGs and HEIFs were named by their EXIF ("Canon EOS R5", "NIKON CORPORATION NIKON Z 6_2"), its RAWs by the decoder's camera catalogue ("EOS R5", "Nikon Z 6 2"), so a camera default set from one kind of file never reached the other, and a DNG's camera changed name when its camera joined the catalogue (its saved default stopped applying). Now every file of a camera the catalogue knows gets the catalogue's name, by exact name only (similar bodies such as the EOS R5 and R5 C stay apart; phones and other cameras without RAW support keep their EXIF names). "RAW files only" (on by default) still keeps JPEG/HEIF neutral; turned off, a camera's default applies to all its files. Camera defaults saved under the old names keep applying (Preferences keeps showing them under those names); if you saved one camera default from its JPEG and another from its RAW, each kind of file keeps its own.
- **Imported Lightroom smart collections on a lens now follow Lightroom's own lens names.** Lightroom matches a lens rule against its own name for the lens (`EF50mm f/1.8`, `TAMRON SP 17-50mm F/2.8 DiII A16E`, `6.1-30.5 mm`), which can differ from the name the file or the lens database gives, so these collections listed other photographs. The import now reads Lightroom's lens name for each photograph from its catalog and the imported smart album matches against it (shown as "Lens (as named in the imported catalog)"); photographs added later are matched by their file's lens, and the Lens filter and your own searches keep the file's lens name. Importing a catalog again that you imported before adds its lens smart albums once more beside the old ones; the old albums are never changed, so anything that refers to them keeps working, and you can remove them once nothing needs them.
- **Multi view: Zoom Lock keeps 100 % at 100 % when you switch the primary, and a zoomed cell you click stays zoomed.** With Zoom Lock on, about a second after clicking another cell every cell went from 100 % to a different zoom (127 % on a test set that mixed 4:3 and 3:2 photos of different resolutions), because the zoom was read back from the editor's zoom factor, which is relative to the fit of whichever photo it shows. The zoom now stays the same (1 image pixel per screen pixel at 100 %) whenever the primary's fit changes: another photo of another size or shape, the cells packed again, a crop, a window resize. A zoomed cell that becomes the primary (Zoom Lock off) no longer shows the whole photo for up to a second; its own zoomed frames stay until its live frame takes over, also when the other cells' zooms reach the display before the click's new layout (which still left it at fit in 2 of 10 switches on Windows). Checked with the native multi view tests on Metal and the multi view zoom tests; Windows check pending.
- **Multi view: the photo you leave as primary stays zoomed in its cell.** Clicking another cell while zoomed in (for example at 100 %) made the previous primary jump to fit for one to three seconds (Zoom Lock on or off) until its own render came in. The layout and every cell's zoom now reach the display in one step, and the previous primary keeps its last frame where it was on screen, its visible part as sharp as before (also nearest-neighbour at the maximum zoom), until its own render replaces it; no softer interim region goes over it. A layout that waited for its thumbnails no longer undoes a zoom or pan made meanwhile. Checked with the native multi view tests on Metal; Windows check pending.
- **Lightroom smart collections by aperture, focal length and shutter speed now import, and camera/lens "contains words" rules match whole words as Lightroom does.** Measured in Lightroom Classic 15.6: aperture values are APEX (f/8 = 6), shutter speeds are time values (greater = longer; a range runs from the shorter to the longer time), and "equal" means within 0.01 of that unit. A lens word such as `EF` no longer matches `EF50mm`. The smart-album editor has the operator "contains the whole words" for camera, maker, lens and file name. Smart collections that Lightroom evaluates per virtual copy (a copy with its own rating, label or keyword) still list the photograph, not the copy, and Lightroom's own lens names (older Canon lenses, compact cameras) can differ from the names read from the files.
- **Imported Lightroom shutter-speed rules match more closely.** Smart collections now use Lightroom's measured tolerance of 0.01 stop around a shutter speed. The smart-album editor shows the rule's time in seconds. Re-importing can add corrected smart albums alongside previously imported versions.
- **The import dialog's 100 % view of a 61–100 MP RAW over an SMB share is 2–4× faster (tested: Linux, kio-fuse, shaped link).** Besides the render's own read, the camera and diffraction facts of the base look each read the whole file again (4–6 times the file in all), and the render read it one page-fault round trip at a time. The facts now read the buffer the dialog already holds, and the render of a file that is not on a card reads it ahead in parallel. Until the whole 100 % image is on screen: at 110 MB/s a 61 MP ARW 15.4 → 6.1–7.6 s (its first visible region 14.5 → 5.1–6.6 s; a local NVMe disk: 5.2 s before and after), a 100 MP 3FR 27 → 11.5–12.4 s (local 8.7 s); at 40 MB/s 32 → 8.5 s and 59 → 14 s. The base look's adjustments came out identical on 362 test RAWs. Not changed: the Sigma fp DNG (still slow over a share), a share the system reports as removable media (its file is read into memory in one pass, without read-ahead), and the library's and Develop's own readers. Other disks, shares and platforms are not measured.
- **Importing a Lightroom catalog or Capture One session again never overwrites what you edited in Rawtonic.** Before, a repeated import replaced the edit of every photo it found with the other program's settings, and the photo's settings file next to it kept showing the old edit. Now Rawtonic remembers what each import brought in:
  - If you have not changed a photo (or virtual copy) in Rawtonic since the last import, it follows the new settings from Lightroom or Capture One. The import summary counts these, and one Undo puts all of them back.
  - If you changed it in Rawtonic and the settings in Lightroom or Capture One changed too, your edit stays as it is and their new settings come in as a new virtual copy beside it. The summary lists each one. Importing the same settings again does not add a second copy.
  - If you changed it in Rawtonic and nothing changed on the other side, nothing happens to it.
  - An edit saved next to the photo by another Rawtonic catalog counts as yours too. A photo whose settings in Lightroom or Capture One were reset to their defaults keeps its edit (Rawtonic cannot tell a reset from settings it could not read).
  - While an edit could not be saved yet, the import waits: it asks you to import again once the edit is saved. A photo open in Develop is closed, with its edit saved, before the import starts.
  - Photos imported with an earlier version count as changed by you once they differ from the incoming settings, so they get a copy rather than being overwritten. Ratings, keywords and other metadata still come from the other program on every import, as before. Your photo files are never written.
- **Grain without bright specks, and grain that reaches the shadows.** Grain added in new edits (`grainVersion` 4) is now about as dark as it is bright, so it no longer reads as a scatter of bright single-pixel specks at 100 %: measured midtone skew −0.05…+0.07 instead of +0.06…+0.45 (seven grain settings on a tone chart). Some bright skew remains at the ends of the tonal range: on the tone chart up to +0.5 in the darkest band (display 0.05; before up to +2.1) and up to +0.13 in the brightest (0.9); in the brightest areas of two test photos up to +0.23 (before −0.5…−1.5, i.e. dark specks there). Strength, grain size and clumping stay within about 5 % of before. It also keeps about a third of its midtone strength in dark areas instead of a sixth, so shadows no longer look clean next to grainy midtones. Saved edits keep their grain unchanged.
- **Automatic lens correction checks lens generations and model codes.** When the lens name fits several generations, or its model code conflicts with the available profile, the Lens tool offers candidates to choose from. The same check applies when an Auto edit made offline first resolves its lens at export.
- **Automatic lens correction finds more lenses and no longer guesses.** The lens is now also read from the camera's own lens data where the photo's standard lens field is empty (older Nikon and Canon bodies, e.g. a D800 with the 24-70 f/2.8G or an EOS-1Ds Mark III with the EF 50 f/1.2L, now get their profile). Where several lenses report the same identity (third-party 24-70 f/2.8 zooms on Canon bodies) or the lens name gives only a focal range, Rawtonic no longer applies a wrong profile: the Lens correction tool lists the lenses that fit and you choose yours. A profile whose focal range contradicts the photo (another camera's fixed lens, a lens named only "----") is no longer picked. Saved edits keep the lens they were saved with. Photos edited with automatic lens correction while they were offline get their lens looked up at export: an ambiguous lens is no longer guessed there either, so such exports may now come out without a lens profile instead of a wrong one.
- **Multi view (Windows and macOS): the photo that becomes the primary no longer flashes the viewer background.** Switching the primary showed a flat frame for up to about a second; the cell now keeps its own picture until the new frame is ready. A zoomed cell that stops being the primary stays at its zoom instead of jumping to fit for a moment.
- **Capture One style imports: curves follow Capture One's curve between their points, and Advanced Color Editor ranges now come over.** Capture One draws a curve through two points as a line, through three as a parabola and through four or more as a natural cubic spline (measured on one ramp with 3- and 4-point curves; the rule for five or more points is extrapolated). Rawtonic's curves bend differently between points, so an imported S curve or steep curve could be off by up to 4 L* in some tones. The import now gives the curve its own points plus samples of Capture One's curve (12 points in all for a curve with up to 12 points of its own, which leaves four of the 16 slots free for your own points; a curve with more points keeps all of them). On a multi-band test ramp, three RGB and channel-curve styles are within 0.01 ΔE00 median of Capture One's export (p95 0.04 to 0.33; before: median 0.13 to 1.24, p95 0.5 to 3.1). Ranges that Capture One's Advanced Color Editor tab writes (a colour range with hue, saturation and lightness shifts) were left out whole before; they are imported now with a wider saturation window and a smoother edge than the older fit, and the import report still names the Colour Editor as partial (saturation window and uniformity are not carried over). On 10 test styles of one photo, the change the range makes is 1.7 Lab units off Capture One's on the five styles held out when the two numbers were chosen (3.5 when the range is left out), and 2.2 against 2.8 on the five used to choose them. Only new imports change; saved edits render as before. Not closed: Capture One's Luma curve on saturated colours (1.8 ΔE00 median on the ramp) and its split toning in Black & White (5.6 to 8.2 ΔE00 median on the ramp) — see the parity notes.
- Export recipes saved with Super Resolution keep it after a restart (it was dropped when the settings were written).
- **Privacy: an Edit With render no longer keeps metadata you left out of its file.** When "Include metadata" was limited, or creator contact details were off, the render file itself was stripped correctly, but the library still recorded the photo's full metadata for it (contact details, place, caption, keywords), and a later XMP sync could write those into the render's sidecar. The library now records only what the file carries (a render also no longer takes over the photo's pick or reject flag), also for renders recovered after a quit or crash and for Try again. A render an earlier version registered this way loses those fields when Rawtonic recovers its unfinished Edit With; fields you changed on the render since stay.
- Opening an untouched photo in Develop no longer marks its earlier export as changed.
- **Lightroom imports: brushes with erasers, several brush groups and hidden groups now import as Lightroom renders them.** A Lightroom mask whose brush groups hold erasers was left out when a second erasing group existed (hidden or not), and a subtracted or intersected brush group was left out too; an eraser cut earlier components. Measured by rendering hand-written Lightroom sidecars in Lightroom Classic 15 itself (2 RAWs, 1500 px, coverage read back): each group is evaluated alone (its eraser cuts only its own strokes), groups combine like any other components, a hidden group adds nothing, and a group's own value is ignored (Lightroom resets it). The import now matches that law within 0.000–0.003 mean coverage error on both photos (two erasing groups, one or the other hidden, both hidden, subtracted group with an eraser, group at value 0.5), where the previous import left these layers out or applied the 0.5. The 0.000–0.003 figures are each editor's coverage against its own component masks. A luminance range alone still differs from Lightroom's rendered coverage by 0.14 and 0.16 (mean over the masked area, two photos): Lightroom's own equalisation of the photo's luminance is not reproducible from the rendering (open). A group whose eraser uses Auto Mask keeps its colour-gated erase as before (it goes first when only additive components precede it), otherwise it is left out and named. Only new imports change; saved edits render as before.
- **Capture One Generic Distortion now comes over.** A photo whose Capture One lens profile is "Generic" (no profile of the lens) with Distortion above 0 gets the same pure radial distortion without any lens profile and without cropping the frame, as Capture One renders it with Hide Distorted Areas off (measured on two cameras: 0.7–3.4 px rms on a 7360 px wide frame, 2.5–3.2 px on a 3648 px one, over interior patches). The Lens correction tool shows it as an imported correction whose Distortion amount scales it; the checkbox switches it off and on without losing it, Copy, Paste and Sync of the Lens group carry it, and choosing a lens from the database replaces it only when you ask. Like every repeated import, it never replaces an edit you made in Rawtonic (see below). Distortion at 0 keeps Rawtonic's lens correction for the lens.
- **Capture One imports: Generic Light Falloff, the "Generic pincushion distortion" profile and straightening now come over.** Light Falloff on the Generic profile becomes the imported correction's vignetting, fitted to the brightening Capture One renders (measured on four cameras at 50 and 100, fitted on one: 0.015–0.08 EV rms inside the frame's long-side circle on the other three, where it was dropped before, a miss of 1.5–2 EV at 100; the far corners at 100 differ more, up to 0.25 EV). The pincushion profile becomes the same polynomial distortion with the frame filled to its corners as Capture One crops it (0.2–0.6 px rms on a 7360 px frame, 1.6–2.8 px on a 3648 px one); before, the photo got its own lens's profile at Capture One's amount. A photo straightened in Capture One is straightened by the same angle and cropped as Capture One shows it, and its quarter turns come over (the EXIF orientation's own turns are not applied twice); Capture One's own crop is still not carried (the import report says so). The Lens correction tool's Vignetting amount scales the imported falloff. Only new imports change; saved edits render as before.
- **"Apply Capture One straightening and crop" for photos an earlier version imported.** Where Capture One shows such a photo straightened and the import kept your edit (it cannot tell whether the straightening is new), the import report now offers the choice: buttons for the first listed photos and one for all listed photos, and the same button in Develop's Crop tool for every one of those photos. It replaces only the straightening angle, quarter turns and crop with what the import makes of Capture One's: the angle, and an automatic crop of the frame's own aspect ratio without empty corners (a crop drawn in Capture One is not read, and a crop of your own is replaced); a photo Capture One no longer straightens, or whose edit develops an older frame of the file, is left as it is and named. Every other adjustment stays, drawn masks stay on their content, the photo file is never touched, one Undo puts it back, and the next import no longer names the photo. The angle the report shows is the angle applied: if Capture One's session shows another one by then, nothing is changed and the new value is named. A photo whose file is offline is not changed. Nothing is applied unless you click.
- The export queue and Activity count newly written photos alongside skips, replacements, new names and failures. Replace if changed skips an unchanged export when saving and reopening its edit only adds bookkeeping to the recipe.
- On Linux, Edit With also lists raster editors that support opening files but omit the selected export format from their desktop entry. Programs associated with the format are recommended.

- **Restart now for an update reopens a photo you opened on its own.** With no library open, a photo opened that way could come back as an empty start screen after the update's restart: on Windows always (the setup restarts Rawtonic without the file), on macOS and Linux when the photo was handed to Rawtonic while it was already running or opened from the Finder, and for a Linux AppImage started with a relative path. Rawtonic now notes what was open in its own settings folder before the update installs and opens it again at the next start; the photo file itself is never touched. A library reopens by itself as before, and a restart no longer brings back a library or photo from the first launch's command line after you switched to another one. The restarted AppImage also starts in the folder it was first launched from, so a file named relative to that folder is found again.

- Virtual copies use previews that match their framing. A copy can open from its matching preview while the original is offline, even if the master uses a different frame. The grid keeps the copy’s existing thumbnail when it can’t make a new one offline.
- **Lightroom imports: a negative Point Color Variance is converted closer to Camera Raw's result.** Variance below zero evens a swatch's colours out. The import gave hue, chroma and lightness uniformity the same amount; in a model of the import (Python, Camera Raw 18.7, 10 swatches on 4 photos, default windows, no shifts) its colour error against Camera Raw was 2.3–4× the size of Camera Raw's own change on 8 of 10 swatches, worse than ignoring the Variance (1×). It now sets them in the ratio measured against Camera Raw (100 : 30 : 15 at Variance −1); on the 6 swatches of 2 photos not used for the fit the modelled error is 0.34–1.04× (one swatch, a dark red-brown, no better than ignoring it). A positive Variance is still not converted. Only new imports change; saved edits render as before.
- Activity says when every photo in an export was skipped, including a skipped Save As. When export names are already taken, Ask shows up to five filenames in each group and says how many more are in that group.
- **A virtual copy saved with an older render version opens offline again after an update.** When an update changed how a never-edited master develops its RAW, its virtual copies saved before could no longer open while the original was offline ("reconnect the RAW"), although the stored previews were made for exactly their version. A stored preview now stands for the render version it was made for: such a copy opens on it offline, in the editor, the grid and the multi view alike. The master keeps showing its older previews offline, marked as offline previews, until the RAW is back; nothing is exported from them.
- **Capture One session imports now preserve nested keyword assignments.** The importer removes the counters that used to appear as extra levels and keeps all listed keyword paths, including parent assignments. This applies to new imports of keywords assigned to photos.
- **A virtual copy with another decode variant than its master (e.g. the multi-scale colour noise filter) now gets its refreshed grid tile at once.** Its tile is developed from the RAW, but kept its old look until the master's preview was renewed; and with the original offline and only the master's preview rebuilt, the copy's tile went blank. It now keeps its old tile offline and renews online. Exports are unchanged.

- **HDR merges with ghost removal: far fewer dark specks around moving sun glints, and High keeps more of the darkest frame** (new merges; merged files you saved stay as they are). Measured on real sunrise brackets with glints on water and on test brackets with a known answer, next to Capture One 16.9.1's HDR merge of the same brackets.
  - **Glints:** where a glint moved between frames, darker frames that saw water there still added to the bright reference and left dark specks and rims. Wherever the reference frame is bright, the other frames now have to agree with it pixel by pixel (with ghost removal on). On one set of sunrise brackets at Medium, in areas clipped in the reference, the pixels below 70 % of its brightness fell from 95 to 3, and in a 4-pixel rim around them from 70 to 0 (High now gives the same 3 and 0; Capture One: 1,264 and 25 — it leaves black holes in the glints). The test fades in with the reference's brightness, so smooth reflections get no edge, and pixels it drops show in Show ghost areas and the frame's ghost share.
  - **High ghost removal** treated sensor noise in the darkest frame's deep shadows as ghosts (13–19 % of that frame dropped on still scenes). Each colour channel's noise allowance now follows how much the camera's colour matrix amplifies noise in it: 0.2–0.4 % dropped, and the error 9 stops below white falls from 0.325 to 0.246 and from 0.170 to 0.155 (two test scenes). A moving coloured object is still removed.

- Slider edits made while a colour target is still being measured keep their values and Undo history.
- The export queue explains why a proof sheet could not start, even when all its photos have already rendered.
- "Replace if changed" can record exports again after an interrupted first export leaves an empty records file.
- **Replace if changed reuses numbered exports.** When another file occupies the requested name, Rawtonic skips its own unchanged numbered export of the same photo or atomically replaces it if the photo's edit or export settings changed. The file at the requested name stays untouched.
- **Before/After: a snapshot of an older edit is no longer shown through the wrong preview while a photo opens.** For DNGs with a lens warp per colour plane (e.g. some drone and action-camera files), the Before of a snapshot saved with an older render version could briefly show the new per-plane warp until the full decode arrived; it now waits for the decode.

- **Levels pickers keep sampling the right frame on Linux** while the next preview is prepared in the background after a drag: a pick made in that moment could take the reduced base of the frame being prepared instead of the shown one, which shifted the picked white and black points slightly. A pick samples the shown frame's base again, before and after that work.
- **Photo merges: fewer dropped panorama frames, sharper focus stacks, cleaner HDR ghosts** (new merges; merged files you saved stay as they are). Measured against RapidRAW 1.7.1's rewritten merge engine on test sets with a known answer and on real brackets and panoramas; each change takes the better of the two approaches.
  - **Panorama:** a frame with little texture (a wall, a grey card or sky filling most of the view) gave too few matching points, so an overlapping frame could not be joined to it and was left out of the panorama (the merge summary listed it as "no overlap"). The point search now lowers its contrast threshold step by step when a frame gives too few points. On a three-frame test panorama whose middle frame is mostly a grey card, all three frames are now stitched (the outer frame next to the card was left out before), with the same coverage as RapidRAW's and sharper detail. Frames that give enough points at the normal threshold keep exactly the same points.
  - **Focus stack:** a small, bright detail that is sharp in one frame only (lettering, a stamen, an edge) could come from a blurred frame. On a five-frame test stack, the share of detailed areas at least 30 % less sharp than RapidRAW's result fell from 8.0 % to 0.1 %. The 99th percentile of the smoothed brightness error against the known answer fell from 0.63 to 0.14 EV (RapidRAW: 0.35).
  - **HDR ghost removal:** a moving object of another colour but similar brightness (a red coat against grass) was only half removed. Each colour channel is now checked on its own, relative to the pixel's brightness. On a test set with a moving red patch, the 99th percentile of the smoothed brightness error fell from 0.12 to 0.02 EV. On real sunrise brackets with sun glints on water, inside the areas clipped in the reference frame the pixels below 70 % of the reference's brightness fell from 64 to 49, and in a 4-pixel rim around those areas from 96 to 73 (RapidRAW: 1,472 and 2,776). The cost on a still test scene at Medium is 1 % more relative RMS error 9 stops below white (0.216 → 0.218); at High that error was about the same with the old and the new rule (0.322 and 0.325).
- **Face retouch Iris spills less onto the eye white** (retouches made from now on; Eyes and Teeth saved before keep their look, also when their faces are found again after a flip, turn or straightening, even if a face left the frame in between). Face Mesh's iris circle often sits beside the real iris or is a little large, so Iris also lightened the eye white and the dark ring around the iris. The Iris mask's disk now reaches 0.9 of the circle's radius on retouches made from faces the eye and teeth model analysed (stored on the retouch as `irisDisk`). Measured on 36 eyes of 20 public portraits against marked irises (medians): the share of changed pixels farther from the iris than 4 % of its radius (at least 1 px) fell from 0.12 to 0.057, and the halo (mean absolute lightness change in the 3 px band beyond that distance ÷ the mean change inside) from 0.26 to 0.16; Capture One 16.9.1 at its maximum: 0.17 and 0.22. On the 10 hold-out portraits, measured once after the setting was frozen, the halo is 0.131 vs Capture One's 0.19. The lightening is unchanged (ΔL* 5.6; Capture One 5.0). Sclera is unchanged: its halo stays above Capture One's on the hold-out (0.165 vs 0.08), and none of the 24 mask settings tested (gate, inset, inner edge) reached Capture One's hold-out halo at its coverage.
- **Library thumbnails of never-edited CR3, ARW and RAF files on a network share read less of each file.** To decide whether a stored preview fits a photo, Rawtonic compares how its recipe and the stored one decode. That comparison read the RAW's frame data even when both recipes ask for the same frame version, which a never-edited photo's always do; it now reads it only when they differ. A photo that has a Standard preview still reads its frame data once to check that preview. The Library's grid read that data once in its tile lookup and again, after reopening the file, for the camera JPEG; on share clients that drop cached data per open (KDE's kio-fuse) the share served those parts twice. Measured for the grid's two steps (tile lookup, then making the thumbnail; the engine steps in the app's order, not the whole app) on 11 cold RAWs without a Standard preview over a simulated LAN / Wi-Fi SMB link on Linux: 3.35 → 3.12 s / 6.15 → 5.46 s, CR3, ARW and RAF 26–63 ms faster each on the LAN, other formats unchanged; local disk within noise. With develop defaults set for other cameras, the camera is still read by path in the lookup, as before. Also fixed: a never-edited RAF thumbnail could miss its camera crop (16:9, 5:4) when the file's time could not be read.
- **Sharp stars, lamps, glints and thin lines keep their brightest photosites** (new edits; saved ones keep their look). The automatic hot and dead pixel repair now uses RapidRAW 1.7.1's isolation test: a photosite is repaired only when it stands apart from its whole neighbourhood, not when the photosites around it share its light. The old test darkened the cores of sharp stars and lamps (on a 15 s night frame 9 of the 12 photosites only it changed; the other 3 are faint single points that may be stars or hot photosites and now stay), left a dark dot in the middle of small specular glints (35 photosites on a daylight frame, now none), changed photosites along 1 px dark lines, and at ISO 204800 lifted 7,620 dark noise photosites per frame (now none). On a synthetic star field with 600 stars, none is dimmed by more than 20 % (the old test dimmed up to 195), and on 1 px dark lines 7 photosites change on Bayer and 123 on X-Trans sensors instead of 14,075 and 3,184. Hot photosites are found as before (36 of 400 injected into a 33 MP frame left, were 37). Dead (black) photosites in busy texture are left more often (82 of 150 left, were 43), where they barely show: 10 more visible spots among 550 defects. The repair takes 1–3 % longer (at most about 1 ms on the 40–81 MP files measured).
- **Dead photosites in busy texture are repaired again** (new edits; saved ones keep their look). The isolation test of the previous fix left a dead photosite whose neighbours of its colour spread widely (grass, foliage, gravel) about twice as often as the old test. New edits also repair a dark photosite it leaves when it is far darker than every neighbour of its colour and no other photosite around it is dark (as on a thin dark line or between bright points). Measured on an ILCE-7M4 frame with 150 injected dead photosites: 35 left instead of 82 (the old test: 43), and 52 instead of 87 still visible in the export (a difference above 5 % of white within 3 pixels of the defect). On the synthetic test scenes no star peak is dimmed by more than 20 % (0 of 600), the thin dark lines have as many changed samples as with the previous fix (7 Bayer, 123 X-Trans), and 1 of 1.9 million samples of the defect-free high-ISO scene changes by more than 2 % (none with the previous fix); on the 12 test RAWs it repaired 0–5 more photosites per frame (5 on a 15 s night frame, none at ISO 204800).
- **Dead photosites in dark texture are repaired too, in the colour around them** (new edits; saved ones keep their look). A dead (black) photosite in dark, busy texture (foliage in shade, dark fabric, gravel) was often left because its neighbours vary as much as it differs from them. New edits also repair a photosite at black that is far darker than every neighbour of its colour when no other photosite around it is dark (as on a thin dark line), and a repaired dead photosite takes the colour the neighbouring green photosites imply instead of the plain median of its own colour. The new rule acts only where the neighbours stand well clear of the photo's own noise, measured on each photo; everything the previous rule repaired is still repaired. Measured on an ILCE-7M4 frame with 150 injected dead photosites: 29 instead of 52 still visible in the export (a difference above 5 % of white within 3 pixels of the defect; Capture One 16.9.1 with Single Pixel noise reduction at 100: 37, while that setting changes 1.28 million pixels of the defect-free frame). The defect-free frame and a 4 s ISO 204800 frame export unchanged; a 15 s night frame changes 1 pixel by more than 2 % (one more repaired photosite) and no star or lamp core by more than 20 %. On the synthetic test scenes no star peak is dimmed by more than 20 % (0 of 600), thin dark lines keep as many changed samples as before (7 Bayer, 123 X-Trans), and the defect-free high-ISO scene changes as with the previous fix (1 of 1.9 million samples by more than 2 %). On the ISO 204800 frame (21 MP), whose noise has many photosites at black, the repair step took 1.1–1.5 ms longer (measured on a busy machine).
- **Fewer dead photosites left in dark texture, and dead green photosites take the colour around them** (new edits; saved ones keep their look). A dead photosite at black is now also repaired when a photosite near it is fairly dark against its own colour (down to half its neighbours' level, as dark texture often is; before, 0.6), and a repaired green photosite takes its green neighbours' ratio to the red and blue around them, as red and blue ones already took green's, instead of the plain mean of the greens beside it. Measured on the same ILCE-7M4 frame with 150 injected dead photosites: 17 instead of 29 still visible in the export (difference above 5 % of white within 3 pixels; Capture One 16.9.1 Single Pixel 100: 37); on a second copy with 150 dead photosites at new places, not used for any choice: 20 instead of 28. The defect-free frame, a 15 s night frame and a 4 s ISO 204800 frame export with no pixel changed by more than 2 %; on the synthetic test scenes thin dark lines, star peaks and the defect-free high-ISO scene change as before.
- **Repaired dead photosites match their surroundings more often** (new edits; saved ones keep their look). A dead photosite now takes the middle of three estimates of its value instead of one: the colour ratio of its neighbours (as before), the ratio of the neighbours whose surrounding colours are closest to its own, and the colour difference along the direction in which the picture changes least. No other photosite is repaired or changed. Measured on the ILCE-7M4 frame with 150 injected dead photosites: 13 instead of 17 still visible in the export (difference above 5 % of white within 3 pixels; Capture One 16.9.1 Single Pixel 100: 37), and 15 instead of 20 on a second copy with 150 dead photosites at other places that was not used to choose the estimates. The defect-free copy and a 4 s ISO 204800 frame export byte-identically, a 15 s night frame changes no pixel by more than 0.2 %, and the repair step took between 0.6 % less and 0.9 % more time than before on the frames measured (within the 5 % budget).

- Capture One imports bring the whole keyword list: keywords no photo carries are kept in the Keyword List (with their hierarchy), names holding `;`, `,` or `>` stay one keyword instead of being split, and the import check shows how many keywords the list holds and how many keyword synonyms are not imported (Rawtonic has no synonyms). In the Keywords field a level in double quotes is one name (`Places > "Rome, Italy"`), and autocomplete offers keywords whose names hold `,`, `>` or `<` that way, so choosing one applies that keyword. Changed: a `;` in Capture One keyword text no longer separates keywords (older sessions' `a;b` imports as one keyword, was two).

## 0.1.0-alpha.19 — 2026-11-22

The nineteenth alpha. New: a Landscape mask (sky, mountains, water, vegetation, ground and architecture), Eyes and Teeth in the face retouch, a Culling panel in the Library, adaptive presets whose AI layers work on photos you never opened, target colours in the color editor, and signed provenance (C2PA) in exports, off by default. On new edits raised Shadows move at Camera Raw's pace and lowered Whites leave less of a light rim; saved edits keep their Shadows and Whites. Adding a folder to the Library needs about half the memory, the Library and the import dialog read far less of each RAW from a network share, and **Restart now** for an update on Windows no longer stops at "Failed to kill Rawtonic".

### Highlights

- **Color editor targets: a photo colour far beyond sRGB gets much closer to its target, and the target's measurement no longer waits 250 ms.**
  - When the model's solve and its measured corrections still miss by more than ΔE00 1, the sliders now follow the renderer's own measured response: one probe per slider, then measured steps. That is at most 8 probes, and no new probe starts later than 1 s after the first measurement.
  - The result is kept when it reaches the target (ΔE00 ≤ 1) or at least halves the miss. A target that cannot be reached therefore keeps its own direction instead of a drained colour that only shaves the distance.
  - **Measured (QA app, full-resolution exports of the saved recipes, laptop M5 Pro):** a blue toy robot whose colour lies far beyond sRGB, aimed at the sRGB blue #0057B8, now exports ΔE00 1.13 from it on the eyedropper's block (1.11 on a wider block). It was 3.3; Capture One 16.9.1 lands 1.37 / 1.69. It is still short of the target, and the readout says "out of reach: 1.1 left, measured".
  - Sky, skin, product and feathered-layer targets do not enter the search. On the app's own block they export 0.10, 0.17, 0.07 and 0.34, each within 0.05 of its readout.
  - A pick's sample of the color entering the color editor is kept while nothing it depends on changes: the recipe before the ranges, the app settings and the photo's source. Before, every target sampled it again at full resolution (two renders, ~370 ms).
  - The target's measurements now take the Develop view's turn at the GPU. Before, the first one waited out the 250 ms pause after the edit's own frame.
  - **Latency (app log, from the click or Return to the frame presented; 10 repeats each, laptop shared with other jobs):** for target picks that needed no visible correction, p50 54 / p90 59 ms, and their measured check was done at p50 84 / p90 100 ms. Before, that check alone took 280–320 ms. Typed hex + Return: p50 ~39 / p90 51 ms, including the correction's frame in the 5 that needed one.
  - With AI Denoise on, the readout's prediction (0.1) matched the export (0.14) on one skin target.
- **Importing a Lightroom catalog names what it leaves out of its albums.** A smart collection whose rules have no Rawtonic counterpart (camera, lens, file name, edited, virtual copy, ISO …) was left out with only a count in the preview; the import now lists each one with the criterion that stopped it. An album that held a virtual copy (Rawtonic albums hold photographs) now lists those copies, so the added photograph is not a surprise. Measured on a real Lightroom Classic 15.6 catalog of 46 smart collections: 19 were imported, 17 of them with the same photographs as in Lightroom (nested AND/OR groups, ratings, labels, flags, keywords, dates, file formats); 2 differ where a virtual copy has a rating of its own, and 27 are not imported.
- **Importing a Lightroom catalog keeps smart collections by camera, lens, file name and ISO.** These criteria were left out; they now become smart albums on the catalog's own camera, lens, file name and ISO. Lightroom's camera name is the maker and the model ("Canon EOS 600D"), so a camera word is looked for in either.
