Holding a tool's reset button with ⌥/Alt (or holding it for a moment) shows the photograph without that tool — on the active layer when one is selected — until you let go; nothing is reset and no history step is made. On a Linux test PC the toggle showed its frame after about 42 ms and the held reset after about 85 ms (24 and 45 MP, app-internal timing), and slider drags in side by side kept their preview rate.
- **Session folders anywhere.** The session menu › *Session Folders…* sets where the session's captures and imports go (Capture), where *Move to Selects* moves photos (Selects) and where exports go by default (Output): any folder inside the session, which moves with it, or outside it, such as a capture drive or a shared folder (Capture One's session folders). Changing a folder moves no files unless *Move the contents to the new folder* is ticked; then they move with the journaled file operations (copied and verified to another drive) and Edit ▸ Undo puts them back. Photos in a folder outside the session belong to it like those inside and keep their edits while the drive is away. A folder whose drive is not connected is shown as not available, and capture, import, *Move to Selects* and export into it stop with its name instead of writing elsewhere. The folder tree's context menu sets a folder as the Capture, Selects or Output folder directly (nothing moves). Masks of every kind stay with a closed session copied or moved to another place or drive: a copy renders byte-identical.
- **Stacks and Auto-Stack.** Photo › Stacking (and the right-click menu) groups photos and virtual copies under one top photo: *Group into Stack* ⌘/Ctrl+G (the photo the Navigator shows is the top), *Unstack* ⇧⌘/Ctrl+G, *Remove from Stack*, *Move to Top of Stack* ⇧S, and S collapses or expands the selected stack (*Collapse/Expand All* in the menu); each is one Undo step. In a folder or the whole project or catalog a collapsed stack shows its top with the count (click it to expand); expanded members sit together, joined by a bar. Ratings, flags, labels, edits, export and moves on a collapsed stack act on its top only. Albums, smart albums and any filter or search list stacked photos singly, so a filter never hides a matching photo inside a collapsed stack. Moving a top to the Trash shows the next photo as the top (Undo puts it back). *Auto-Stack by Capture Time…* groups the selection (or the shown folder) by the time between photos (1 s – 60 min) with a live count, and first by the camera's own bracket (EXIF auto-bracket) and burst (Fujifilm and Sony frame numbers; a burst with frames deleted in the camera stays one) data; photos already in a stack stay as they are. Import from Lightroom brings Lightroom's folder stacks over with their top, order, collapsed state and the virtual copies in them, and Import from Catalog brings the other catalog's stacks with its photos; importing again makes no second stack and never takes a photo out of a stack you made here. Sony photos already in a library have their burst numbers read again in the background. *Split Stack* divides an expanded stack at the active photo: it and the photos after it become a stack of their own (the active photo its top) and the photos before it stay a stack (one side left there with a single photo is no stack); *Move Up in Stack* ⇧[ and *Move Down in Stack* ⇧] swap the active photo with its neighbour, so moving the top down shows the next photo, and neither does anything at the ends. Each is one Undo step that puts the stack back exactly. Stacks are kept in the library only; no file or XMP sidecar is changed. Auto-Stack plans 10,000 photos in about 20 ms on a ThinkPad X13 laptop.
- **Import from Catalog.** The catalog menu › *Import from Catalog…* brings back the work done in another Rawtonic catalog, such as one made with Export as Catalog and edited on a laptop. Photos are matched by their identity, else by the content of their original (SHA-256), never by file name. The review lists new photos (added where the other catalog has them, or copied and verified to a folder you choose) and changed photos with what changed; for changed photos one choice for all and one per photo: *Replace edits and metadata* (the default; the replaced edit is kept as a snapshot named “Before import from …”, so it is one click away), *Keep current* (the default for photos changed here, or on both sides, since the export; each changed photo says which catalog changed it) or *Import as virtual copy*. Unchanged photos are skipped and counted. Everything is applied in one step, which Edit ▸ Undo takes back: if anything fails, nothing is applied and copied originals are taken back. The other catalog is never changed; projects stay self-contained, so the import goes into catalogs only. Importing 1,000 changed photos took about 1.2 s on a ThinkPad X13 laptop. A stack changed alone in the other catalog (its photos the same in both) is offered as a change in *stacks* and comes back with its top, order, members and collapsed state when you Replace; a stack you changed here since is kept.
- **Export as Catalog.** The project or catalog menu › *Export as Catalog…* makes a new catalog of the selected photos (or all photos the folder or album shows) to take a job to another computer: their edits with history, virtual copies, snapshots, ratings, labels, flags, keywords, IPTC, capture-time corrections, eye checks, masks, the albums holding them and the LUTs and profiles their edits use. *Include original files* copies and verifies the originals into the new catalog's folder (stored relative, so the folder can move); without it the catalog refers to the originals where they are, and photos whose drive is not connected edit on their previews (*Include previews*, on by default). Photos inside the project or catalog's own folder (all of a project's) belong to it and are always copied; photos that another project owns (added to a catalog where they are) have their latest edits there and are left out, after you confirm, or the export is refused. Progress and Cancel are in the activity centre; a cancelled or failed export leaves no catalog behind, an existing folder with anything in it is never used, and the library you export from is never changed. 1,000 photos by reference took 2.8 s on a ThinkPad X13 laptop.
- **Highlights keeps bright skies and snow bright.** Lowering Highlights on a bright photo (snow, an overcast or bright sky, a white room) no longer greys its near-white areas: Rawtonic now lowers less the brighter the frame's darker part and median are, as Camera Raw does, and still brings back detail there. A snowy sky under an overcast light at −40 darkens by 2 L* instead of 7 (Camera Raw 2.2); on 25 test photos not used for tuning, the difference of the bright areas' change from Camera Raw's at −100 fell from 7.5 to 2.3 (Oklab ΔE×100) and at −40 from 1.6 to 0.8. Darker photos render as before. Photos imported from a Lightroom Classic or Capture One catalog now get this Highlights response too (they had the first one, which darkened bright areas furthest): on the 40-photo Lightroom test catalog the median colour difference to Lightroom's own exports fell from 1.74 to 1.33.
- **Truer colour in large over-exposed skies and walls.** Where one or two colour channels of a RAW clip over a large area, the rebuilt colour now follows the colour ratios measured a few pixels away from the clip instead of the samples right at its edge, and spreads smoothly instead of cell by cell. On 30 photographs clipped 1.5–3 stops below their original exposure (the unclipped original as the truth), the median colour error of the rebuilt areas fell by a fifth (Oklab ΔE×100 0.93 → 0.74) and the hue error from 3.7° to 2.9°: a sunset sky no longer turns purple (2.96 → 0.73), a tungsten-lit table no longer redder (4.43 → 1.60), and a dusk sky loses its brighter blotch with a hard edge. Skies where green and blue clip over faint red get somewhat worse (four frames, 0.93–1.91 → 1.63–2.50). Saved edits look exactly as before (`renderVersion` 9).
- **Relight: aim the light with a ball, start from classic setups, and every slider shows at 100 %.** A light-direction ball in the panel (and a ring on the photo around the face) aims a sun: the centre lights from the camera, the edge from the side; drag it, or use the arrow keys (Shift for bigger steps), Esc cancels a drag. The panel keeps Strength, Softness, Warmth and Original light in front; the rest is under Advanced. Nine one-click setups — side, loop, split, butterfly, edge light, window light, warm evening light, soft fill and blinds at sunset — are placed on the detected face (it is keyed from the side the face turns to), else on the subject or the frame's centre; Undo takes them back. Controls that cannot act on the light now are greyed out with the reason (face reflections without a face, diffusion with a light from the front, a spot's direction). New lights use measured ranges (`rangeVersion` 2): on 4 portraits and 3 scenes, Softness, Diffusion and Shadow depth did not visibly change people at 100 % before (mean ΔL* at most 0.01) and Face reflections stayed below one L* step; now Softness and Diffusion reach the shadow side (mean ΔL* 3–5, p95 up to 15) and Face reflections give a visible sheen (p95 2.3–6.6 on the face), and Strength 100 % is a clear key light (gain 4 instead of 3, finer at the low end). Saved lights keep their ranges and export exactly as before; *Use the current ranges* moves a light over at the same brightness.
- **Find Duplicates suggests less and never touches another project's photos.** In each group the review now keeps the photo with the most work (edits, rating, flag, label, keywords, albums, title or caption, virtual copies), on a tie the oldest import, and suggests the others for the Trash only when they are inside the project's or catalog's own folder; photos in your own folders that a catalog refers to are listed but never marked unless you mark them. A photo inside another project's or catalog's folder is greyed with "Belongs to <name>: remove it there" and cannot be moved to the Trash from here, as Export as Catalog leaves such photos out.
- **Import from Lightroom brings real Lightroom Classic 15 catalogs over, edits included.** Tested with a catalog Lightroom Classic 15.6 (German interface) built for 40 scratch photos in nested folders. Such catalogs were refused before (Lightroom 15 compresses the metadata it keeps per photo), and their edits would not have come along: Lightroom keeps develop settings in its own settings table, not in that metadata. Now ratings, labels, picks and rejects, keyword hierarchies, title, caption, creator, copyright and IPTC location fields come over for all 40 photos, and the edits of all 36 edited ones: tone, white balance, point, parametric and RGB curves, HSL, colour grading, black and white, sharpening, noise reduction, vignette, grain, dehaze, texture, transform, lens distortion and chromatic aberration, linear, radial and brush masks (Lightroom 15 groups a brush's strokes, which were left out before also from sidecars), Select Subject and Select Sky masks (computed when the photo is opened or exported) and heal and clone spots. Photos Lightroom marks as unedited no longer get Lightroom's defaults as an edit. Smart collections become smart albums when every rule has a counterpart (rating, pick, label, keywords, capture date, file type; 11 of the 14 in the test catalog, including Lightroom's own); the others are named in the import's summary as before. White balance follows Lightroom's absolute Kelvin and tint, and *As Shot* keeps the camera's balance. Crop and straighten come over (a crop reaching past Rawtonic's frame, such as an Olympus aspect crop, keeps its centre and aspect ratio and shrinks to fit, and the summary says so); the lens profile switch and its amounts are applied with Rawtonic's own lens data, and Lightroom's manual lens vignetting comes over. Virtual copies arrive as virtual copies with their own edit, rating, label, keywords and snapshots; snapshots come over too, and importing again keeps a copy as it is instead of doubling it. Stacks come over (see *Stacks and Auto-Stack*). Still left out: Lightroom's Remove spots, history steps (the import is one step) and virtual copies' names and album membership. On the test catalog the median colour difference of the imported edits to Lightroom's own exports is now 1.26 (3.44 with the first version of this import in this release). Reading and importing the 40 photos took 0.2 s on a Mac mini.
- **XMP sidecars from Lightroom Classic and Capture One read and written as those programs see them.** Tested with sidecars that Lightroom Classic 15.6 (German interface) and Capture One 16.6.6 wrote for 12 scratch photos, both ways. Lightroom's labels in its interface language (*Rot*, *Gelb* …) now show as Red, Yellow …, its picks and rejects (`xmpDM:pick`) come in as flags, and a keyword hierarchy such as *Places › Europe › Berlin* is one keyword, not four (Lightroom and Capture One also list every level as a keyword of its own). Ratings, labels, flags and keywords set in Rawtonic now show the same in both programs: the label in the language the sidecar already uses (*Blau* for a German Lightroom), picks and rejects for Lightroom, flat keyword terms in `dc:subject` with the hierarchy only in `lr:hierarchicalSubject` (Lightroom no longer gains stray *Places* and *Places › Europe* keywords), and a cleared label stays cleared in Capture One, which reads its colour back from the IPTC urgency it keeps beside the label. Several creators (Capture One adds the camera's artist) are kept instead of all but the first. Lightroom's develop settings, GPS, IPTC Extension and everything else Rawtonic does not edit stay byte for byte as they were. Capture One's orange and pink tags are kept and shown by name in Metadata; Rawtonic's five labels stay the ones you assign.
- **A fresh catalog from Export as Catalog has nothing waiting.** Photos whose only metadata was their camera's own rating no longer open in the exported catalog as "XMP not updated" until *Try again*; the export no longer writes them an editor sidecar that opening the catalog took for new metadata. When photos belong to another project or catalog, the message names it as you know it (its name, not its folder). The eyes filter reads *Eyes to check* and fits in every language.
- **Find Dust.** Spot removal (Heal or Clone) has *Find dust*: dashed amber circles mark the sensor dust found on flat areas, and Visualize Spots' sensitivity slider filters them live (its upper half also finds fainter dust, with more false spots). *Apply* turns them into ordinary heal spots you can edit, as one Undo step. Running it again never adds a spot twice. It leaves out faces (when the face models are installed; it never downloads them), edges, lines, text, fine texture, dark or clipped areas and in-focus specks. *Sync dust to selected photos* adds the open photo's dust spots, as you have moved or resized them, to the selected photos of the same camera, file kind and frame. Each spot lands on the same place on the sensor through every photo's own orientation, crop and lens geometry, and exports render the spots before a photo is opened. Other cameras and frames are skipped and named, and the whole sync is one Undo step. It is not yet as thorough as Lightroom's Dust ▸ Apply. On 24 test photos not used for tuning, at the default sensitivity it found 40 % of the judged dust with 65 % of its marks real, against Lightroom's 81 % and 61 %. At sensitivity 100 it found 60 % (33 % real). Its heal left a residual of 0.036 % on 10 Sony dust spots (Lightroom 0.04 %). Syncing 9 photos took 0.7 s against about 20 s in Lightroom.
- **Faster culling.** Stepping through photos in Develop, the Library loupe and full screen now decodes the next photo right after the open one and keeps the next two decodes. A step then shows the camera JPEG at once and develops its frame from the kept decode, without decoding the RAW itself. On a MacBook Pro (M5 Pro) stepping through 200 card RAWs every 0.6 s (measured from key press to the screen captured at 60 fps), the developed frame came after 0.053 s at the median and 0.066 s at p90, instead of 0.153 and 0.298 s. Capture One 16.6 measured 0.099 and 0.116 s on the same laptop. At one photo every 2.25 s: 0.063 / 0.068 s, instead of 0.128 / 0.181 s. The app uses about 0.3–0.4 GB more memory while stepping, within its existing limit of an eighth of the RAM.
- **Recovery after a forced quit or crash.** The app was killed at random points in every write path, and the catalog, originals, exports, resume and Undo held in every run. What a kill leaves behind is now cleaned up: partial copies from an interrupted import (in the library and in the backup folder) and the hidden folders of an interrupted Export as Catalog or Import from Catalog, which can hold gigabytes of copied originals. They are removed only when they are clearly Rawtonic's own, and the activity centre says how much was removed. Other programs' temporary files are never touched. In 20 of 20 import kills nothing was left behind (7 of 20 before). XMP sidecars left unwritten are written in the background when the library opens. A batch edit cut off by a kill (Paste Settings, a preset or style, rating, label, keyword) is offered at the next open as "Interrupted: … applied to N of M photos", with *Apply to the rest*, *Undo the applied part* and *Dismiss*. A sidecar the library wrote just before a kill is no longer flagged as "changed by another program".
- **Profile browser.** A new Profile tool replaces the camera-profile list. It shows the open photo rendered with every camera profile, look and black-and-white mix, as a grid or a list with search, grouped with Favourites first. Hovering over a profile or giving it keyboard focus shows it in the main view, Esc goes back, and a click or Enter applies it as one history step. Looks have an Amount (0–200 %). On a Linux PC with an RTX 3080, all visible tiles showed 159 ms after opening the browser (median), and a hovered profile appeared in the main view after 21 ms (median).
- **Picker loupe and pointer RGB readout.** While the white balance, colour editor, luma range or Levels picker is over the photo, an 11 × 11 magnified pixel grid follows the pointer, with the R/G/B values of the pixel it samples. *View › Pointer RGB Readout* (off by default) shows the values under the pointer in the viewer's footer, in 0–255 or %, the same values the pickers sample. On a Mac mini a reading took 2–3 ms at the median, and slider drags with the readout on kept their speed.
- **Measured scopes.** The histogram, waveform, RGB parade and vectorscope now count every pixel. Before, a one-pixel clipped column could vanish, and smoothing spread clipped spikes over the top codes. They use the weights of the colour space shown, and the vectorscope has 75 %/100 % targets and a skin line. The clipping triangles take the colour of the clipping channels, and Levels' histogram counts every pixel too. Scopes read the whole shown frame, as in Lightroom and Capture One.
- **Develop controls.** The Develop controls were reviewed side by side with Lightroom Classic 15.6 and Capture One 16.6.6 on the same 10 photos:
  - Sliders are finer by default (the tool column starts at 340 px). An Option-drag on a slider's track moves a tenth as far and no longer jumps to the pointer.
  - Sliders light up on hover and while dragged.
  - The white balance picker sits in the white balance row and ends after one pick. Shift-click keeps it on.
  - *One Tool Open at a Time* is in the tool header menu.
  - The tone curve shows the photo's histogram behind it and a crosshair while you drag. The region curve's splits are handles under the curve instead of three sliders.
  - The clipping triangles are centred in their hover boxes and framed while their warning is on.
  - Exposure comes first on a layer.
  - Layers are named by kind ("Brush 1", "Sky 2").
  - Presets can be starred into a Favorites group.
  - The preset Amount row stays in place.
  - The spot under the pointer is highlighted.
  - ⌘' makes a new virtual copy.
  - Camera settings under the histogram show their units.
- **One naming tool for import, rename and export.** Import, tethering, batch rename and export now share one naming editor. Tokens are chips you add by double-click, Enter or drag, with free text between them, a live example from a real photo and presets shared by all three. Export subfolders can use tokens (`{job}/{date}/{format}`). A job name is remembered per library and defaults to the library's name. `{recipe}` and `{format}` name exports, and a token left empty never stops a batch. The Export form is remembered per library, and its footer shows the destination folder as it will be made. Saved recipes and presets name files exactly as before.
- **Add Folder… adds the photos to a catalog where they are.** In a catalog, Folders › *Add folder…* now adds the folder's photos (subfolders included by default) without copying them or writing anything beside them. RAW+JPEG pairs, XMP metadata and the duplicate check work as on import, with progress and Cancel in the activity centre. Photos already in the catalog are skipped with a count, and photos in another library's folder are refused with its name. For folders added before, a banner offers to add their photos. In a project, the folder is only shown, and the dialog offers to copy the photos into the project.
- **Library and import.** After an import the grid shows the new photos (Previous Import). *Include subfolders* is a grid toolbar toggle, remembered per folder and on by default for a catalog's Photos root. Auto-advance has an on/off toggle in the loupe bar. The import dialog's Copy button stays in reach.
- **Face box on the photo.** Hovering over a face in the Loupe face strip draws its box on the photo, and a click pins it (← / → move between faces). The box carries the face's eyes-open call in colour, icon and words, and follows zoom, crop, straightening and lens corrections.
- **Sky and Subject selections work in exports of never-opened photos, and Subject keeps to what is in focus.** Sky and Subject selections that were never computed (from a Lightroom or Capture One import, or pasted or synced settings) are now computed at export, as when the photo is opened. A missing model stops that photo and names the layer. Before, such photos exported without the mask, with no warning. New Subject selections leave out a separate object that is clearly out of focus beside the sharpest one, while a single subject is never cut. The masking tools now start loading Subject when you unfold Layers & masks.
- **Faster export sharpening, with the same output.** Output sharpening is rewritten for speed: on a Mac mini M4 Pro it took 18.9 ms instead of 69.1 ms on a full-size 16-bit TIFF (−72.6 %). A batch of 38 files exported 4.9 % faster (median 13.68 → 13.01 s). All 864 test exports were byte-identical to before.

### Fixes

- **Session folders stay where they were set.** After a session's Output folder moved, the former one — also when it lay under a folder the session scans, such as an outside Capture folder — was scanned as photographs and its exports showed up in the library: the session now records every folder it ever had as its Output folder (in `session.json`) and no scan or re-index takes exports in them for photographs, wherever they are; nothing is moved or deleted. A configured folder inside the session whose parent was renamed is shown as not available in Session Folders and in the session's opening view and is never made again at its old path, where it would take the work meant for the renamed one; choosing the folder where it is now fixes the setting.
- **Crop mode keeps room for its handles.** On entering crop, the uncropped photo now leaves at least the rotate band's width around it, so the handles and the rotate band are no longer cut off at the viewer edge.
- **Import presets no longer undo edits made during an import, and too many keywords no longer stop one.** A metadata preset now applies its changes in turn with ratings, labels and keywords you set while the import runs, so those edits are kept. If a preset would give a photo more than 10,000 keywords, the photo is still imported with its own keywords and a warning, instead of the whole import stopping at "Too many keywords" on every resume.
- **More of Lightroom's and Capture One's metadata.** Upper-case `.XMP` sidecars are found and updated in place on case-sensitive drives. The IPTC sections edit 15 more Core and Extension fields that Lightroom 15.6 and Capture One 16.6.6 write: people and organisations shown, event, model age, genre, caption writer, creation date, rights URL and more. Exports embed these fields.
- **Smaller fixes.** The export job name no longer carries a catalog's `.catalog` extension. A filter turned on no longer shifts the sort control and grid. Naming keeps the separator before the counter. The viewer footer drops lower-priority items when narrow instead of overlapping them. The red "Last library could not open" notice clears once a library opens.

### Changes to existing edits

- **Saved edits export exactly as in alpha.10,** with one exception: never-opened photos whose Sky or Subject selections had never been computed (from a Lightroom or Capture One import, pasted or synced settings) used to export without that selection and now export as they look in Develop. Every other change below applies to new edits and to photos never edited; recipes you saved keep their render settings (`renderVersion` 8, alpha.10's, or earlier) and were compared byte for byte against the previous code on each change (for example 23 relight, 25 lens-vignetting and 44 Lightroom-catalog exports, 864 for output sharpening).
- **Nikon Z lens corrections from the camera's data now run about the shot's own optical centre** (recipe `cameraGeometryVersion` 8, new edits and never-edited RAWs): Z-mount NEFs record per shot where the lens axis falls on the sensor, which moves with the in-body stabilisation by up to tens of pixels; the distortion correction is now centred there instead of on the frame's centre. Against the cameras' own JPEGs of 57 raw.pixls.us Z NEFs the Z f + 35-150mm at 150 mm (axis 66 px off-centre) now matches to 0.2 px median instead of 2.4, the Z 6 + 24-70mm f/4 (26 px off) to 0.3 instead of 1.3. Saved edits keep their look.
- **Lowered Highlights on bright photos (`toneVersion` 4, new edits).** See *Highlights keeps bright skies and snow bright*; saved edits keep their response and export exactly as before. Edits imported from Lightroom Classic or Capture One catalogs start with the current tone response, as a new edit does.
- **Truer deep shadows on some Canon RAWs (`renderVersion` 11, new edits and never-edited RAWs).** Canon CR2 files whose maker notes give the four photosites of a colour quad black levels that the sensor's covered border does not show (EOS 500D and 7D at ISO 3200, EOS M and 550D at ISO 1600) now take the border's levels: lifted deep shadows of those files lose a green or magenta tint (colour difference ΔE×100 of the darkest flat areas against a clean frame of the scene, EOS 500D at ISO 3200: 1.31 → 0.68, Camera Raw 0.54; median of 4 pairs at ISO 3200–5000: 0.79 → 0.51, Camera Raw 0.51). Other files render as before.
- **Rebuilt highlights of RAWs (`renderVersion` 9, new edits and never-edited RAWs).** Clipped colours inside larger partly clipped areas are rebuilt from colour ratios (see *Truer colour in large over-exposed skies and walls*); small clipped spots and fully clipped areas render as before. The decode takes 60–110 ms longer on 24–47 MP photos with clipping (RTX 3080 host, CPU stage).
- **Deep shadows keep their tone when you change Exposure (`renderVersion` 10, new edits and never-edited RAWs).** The black point of the default RAW look scaled with Exposure, so raising Exposure crushed the deepest tones instead of lifting them: at Exposure +1 they rendered up to 3 stops and at +2 up to 8.5 stops darker than Camera Raw (median over 41 RAWs of 9 makes). The black point now stays at a scene level (it follows a quarter of the exposure change) under a soft toe, as Camera Raw renders: from Exposure −1 to +2 the deepest tones are within 0.2–0.5 stops of Camera Raw's and the median colour difference fell at +1 from 1.52 to 1.05 and at +2 from 2.17 to 1.05 (Oklab ΔE×100; 1.14 → 1.07 at Exposure 0). Extended low ISOs (ISO 50, L, Lo) no longer darken their deepest shadows by 2–4 stops more than Camera Raw does: on 8 photos of 4 makes the step from base ISO now matches Camera Raw's to within 0.17 stops in every tone band. The tone sliders respond as before. On noisy high-ISO photos lifted by 2 stops the colour of the very darkest flat areas varies slightly more against a clean frame (ΔE×100 0.55 → 0.70 at ISO 12800 and above; Camera Raw 0.49).
- **Extended low ISOs open as dark as in Camera Raw (`renderVersion` 9, new edits and never-edited RAWs).** Sony bodies read the sensor at base ISO at their extended ISO 50 (or 64, 80) and record how much they over-expose (the capture gain); Rawtonic now pulls such photos down by it, as the camera's JPEG and Camera Raw do: an α7C at ISO 50 opened a stop brighter than the same scene at other ISOs. The Fujifilm X-T2 at L 100, the Nikon D800/D800E at Lo 0.3 and 0.7, the Sony SLT-A99 and ZV-1 below their base ISO get their missing extended-ISO step, and the Sony α7R IV at normal ISOs opens a stop brighter (it opened a stop darker than Camera Raw). On extended-ISO photos of 7 bodies the step from base ISO to the extended ISO now matches Camera Raw's to within 0.09 EV in mean brightness (up to 1.14 EV off before); each camera's own base-ISO calibration is unchanged. Clipped raw highlights at those ISOs render light grey instead of white, as in Camera Raw (a little darker: 0.86 of white against 0.90 on an α7C file); the clipping warnings follow the rendered image, as in Lightroom.
- **Subject selections leave out objects that are out of focus (`subjectVersion` 2, new Subject selections and Lightroom imports).** See *Sky and Subject selections…*. Stored selections render as stored. Selections saved without a bitmap before this change (never-opened imports, pastes, presets) are still computed the old way. The 44 recipes imported from Lightroom before this change render byte-identical. Re-imported with this build, the 40-photo Lightroom test catalog's median colour difference to Lightroom's exports fell from 1.293 to 1.263.
- **Lightroom imports start with every version a new edit gets, and Lightroom's manual lens vignetting comes over (new keys `lensManualVignetteAmount`, `lensManualVignetteMidpoint`; no new `renderVersion`).** Before, imported edits rendered as `renderVersion` 1 with the first versions of Clarity, noise reduction, Defringe, grain and AI Denoise. Lens Corrections ▸ Manual ▸ Lens Vignetting now has its own slider pair in the Lens panel. It uses our own model, fitted to Lightroom 15.6's measured response. Edits imported before keep their look: 22 of 22 and 25 of 25 old recipes, and 44 of 44 catalog JPEGs, exported byte-identical.

### Known gaps

These are measured shortfalls or open work; they stay open until they are closed by measurement.

- **Find Dust** finds less than Lightroom's Dust: on 24 test photos at the default sensitivity it found 40 % of the judged dust (Lightroom 81 %).
- **Import from Lightroom** leaves out Remove spots, history steps and virtual copies' names and album membership; Select Subject can take a larger subject than Lightroom's.
- **Deep shadows at ISO 12800 and above** still keep more colour cast than Camera Raw after lifting; the remaining cause is known and not fixed in this release.
- **Windows** is experimental: unsigned, tested on one NVIDIA graphics card and on a build machine without a graphics card. It does not run without a Vulkan or DirectX 12 driver (Microsoft's software renderer is refused).
- **Open With and Edit With** are macOS only for now.
- **Find Duplicates** finds identical image data only, not visually similar photos. **Eyes** are suggestions; there is no eye-focus check.
- **Keyboard focus:** some dialogs do not yet return the keyboard focus or show a focus ring where they should.
- **Auto-advance with the physical Caps Lock key** is not yet verified on real hardware; the auto-advance toggle and Photo > Auto-advance work.
- **Object removal on large holes:** the AI fill is still poor on some objects (a kitten in foliage, a pot on a table).
- **Canon tethering** is experimental and untested on a real Canon body; leave it off unless you want to help test.
- The known gaps of alpha.10 and earlier not named here remain.

## 0.1.0-alpha.10 — 2026-10-31

The tenth alpha. An activity centre in the top bar keeps every running task, result and failure in one place, and one Undo now takes back a batch edit on every photo. New for culling and library work: Find Duplicates, eyes open or closed per face, Open With / Edit With (macOS), Shift Capture Time, moving a whole session and a Rename preview. In Develop: two-sided band gradients, Levels with handles on the histogram, separate shadow and highlight clipping warnings, the camera's JPEG at Shift+\\ and cleaner lifted shadows at high ISO. Canon photos shot at another aspect ratio open cropped to it. A first, experimental and unsigned Windows installer is available. Saved edits export exactly as before; new edits and never-edited photos change in a few cases (see *Changes to existing edits*).

### Highlights

- **Find Duplicates.** File › *Find Duplicates…* (or the command palette) finds the photos of a project or catalog whose files hold identical image data, even under other names, in other folders, with other metadata or edits: RAWs are compared by their sensor data (DNG, NEF, CR2, ARW, ORF, RW2, PEF, RAF, CR3 and more), JPEG and PNG by their image data, other files as a whole. The comparison runs in the background (progress and Stop in the activity centre) and is remembered until a file changes; 1,000 RAWs took 6.5 s on a ThinkPad X13 laptop. The review lists each group with thumbnails, folders, dates and edits; photos with edits, ratings, labels, flags, keywords or virtual copies of their own are kept by default, otherwise the oldest import. Move to Trash moves only the photos you marked, as one step that Undo reverses; nothing is merged or deleted. Smart albums have a new rule, *Duplicate*. Visually similar photos are not found yet.
- **Photos shot at another aspect ratio set in the camera open cropped to it.** Canon RAWs taken at 1:1, 4:3, 16:9 or 4:5 and the Samsung EX2F at 16:9 keep the whole sensor in the file; they now open with the camera's frame as their crop, as in Lightroom: a normal crop you can widen to the whole sensor, change or reset (Reset and the crop tool's reset return to the camera's frame). Photos you have already edited keep their own crop (also none); never-edited photos, their thumbnails and exports show the camera's frame. Panasonic, Olympus / OM System and Sony aspect ratios were already shown as shot.
- **Move the whole session.** Moving photos or a folder out of their session (Move to Folder, cut and paste, dragging to another folder or drive) now offers *Move the Whole Session to …* instead, saying why: a session stays one self-contained folder that moves as a whole (photos that must live anywhere belong to a catalog). On the same drive the session folder is renamed in one step; to another drive it is copied and checked byte for byte, and only then does the original go to the Trash. The open session opens again at its new place and its entry in File > Open Recent follows; Undo moves it back. Nothing moves when the session is open in another program, a folder of its name is already there, it would go inside itself or another session or catalog, or the other drive has too little space.
- **Canon EOS R5 Mark II in APS-C crop mode: the whole frame the camera records.** New edits of these CR3 files develop 5088 × 3392 pixels, centred as in the camera's JPEG, instead of 4970 × 3392 cut short on the right. Photos you have already edited keep the frame they were edited in, also after Reset, undo, paste and presets, so no edit changes its size or crop; never-edited photos show the full frame (their previews are made again when the photo is next decoded).
- **Cleaner lifted shadows at high ISO.** New RAW edits keep the true level and colour of noisy shadows: the black clip before demosaicing no longer lifts them and tints them per colour, and the demosaic no longer darkens noisy colours. On 18 high-ISO frames with a clean reference of the same scene, the colour cast of lifted shadows fell on 9, rose slightly on 2 (ISO 3200) and stayed on 7; at ISO 12800 and above it fell by about a third (worst frames 7–8 → 1–3). Cameras whose maker-note black level contradicts the sensor's masked border (EOS 7D at high ISO, two CHDK PowerShots) use the border's. Saved edits look exactly as before (`renderVersion` 8).
- **Camera preview in Develop.** Shift+\\ (View > Camera Preview, or the camera button beside the viewer) shows the JPEG your camera wrote into the RAW in place of your edit, whole and to fit, labelled *Camera preview*; Shift+\\ again or Escape returns to the edit (Escape does nothing else then). Stepping to another photo, leaving Develop, before/after, the multi view or a tool that edits end it. While it shows, clicks, drags and the wheel do nothing to the edit underneath. It never changes the recipe, the history or the file. A RAW without a camera JPEG says so.
- **Resolve XMP files changed by another program from the session bar.** The "XMP not updated" status now says why for each photo as soon as it opens, and lists the XMP files another program changed (also thousands of them) with **Keep library** (the library's rating, label, keywords and IPTC are written into the XMP file; what other programs stored there is kept) and **Use XMP** (the XMP file's are read into the library), per photo or for all. Use XMP is one step of Edit > Undo. Neither ever changes how a photo looks: an edit saved only beside a photo (`.rrdata`) is taken into the library first, also before a flag or a title is set. Only XMP files are written, never the photos. An XMP file the library wrote that was deleted elsewhere now counts as changed elsewhere (Keep library writes it again).
- **Shift Capture Time.** Library context menu, Photo menu or the Metadata tool → *Shift Capture Time…* corrects a camera clock that was set wrong for the selected photos of a project or catalog: shift all of them by one signed amount (days to fractions of a second), or set one reference photo's correct time and move the others by the same difference. The dialog shows the cameras, the photos left out and why (not in the library, offline, no capture time), and the reference's and the selection's times before and after; nothing is written before Apply, and Apply is one undo step. Fractions of a second and the UTC offset stay as the camera wrote them, so bursts keep their order. The library keeps the correction apart from what the file says (rereading the file never undoes it; *Back to the time in the file* removes it); sorting by capture date, search and smart albums by date, the Metadata tool, export names and the exported EXIF (DateTimeOriginal, SubSecTimeOriginal, OffsetTimeOriginal) use the corrected time. Your photo files and their file dates are never changed. Photos outside a project or catalog have to be added first.
- **Rename shows the final names before it renames.** The Rename dialog lists each photo's new name (with the number a batch adds), the sidecars, XMP and paired JPEG renamed with it, and any name that is taken, and Save runs exactly that list (if the files changed meanwhile, nothing is renamed). A RAW+JPEG imported as one photo now renames its JPEG too; a RAW and a JPEG of one name that share an XMP are renamed as a pair instead of refusing the whole rename (renaming only one of them takes the XMP with the RAW, as the dialog says); renaming `A.NEF` no longer takes the edit of a separate `A.NEF.jpg`, also when moving or deleting it.
- Queued exports keep to the library they were started from. Each photo's recipe, metadata and EXIF are read once, when the export is added to the queue, from the session or catalog it was started in. Opening another session or catalog while exports wait no longer changes what they render or embed. Virtual copies now embed their own rating, colour label and keywords. A photo moved or deleted before its turn fails on its own and says why. An export whose photos have edits that could not be saved is not started. Exports that an earlier version left in the queue are not run: they show as failed with "export it again".
- **Activity centre.** One place in the top bar for what Rawtonic is doing: running tasks with progress and Cancel (exports, imports, AI masks, model downloads, AI Denoise, preview builds, copies to another drive, batch Auto and face retouch), results of the last 15 minutes, and everything that needs your attention. Failures and warnings stay listed until they are resolved or dismissed, with Try again, Show photos or Copy details, instead of a toast that disappears; only confirmations of your own actions still show as a short toast. View > Activity… or ⌥⌘A (Ctrl+Alt+A on Linux and Windows).
- **Eyes open or closed, per face.** *Check eyes…* in the Library’s Eyes filter reads each face of the photos and suggests *eyes open*, *closed or partly closed*, *not visible* or *uncertain*; the grid and compare view show a badge, the Loupe lists each face with its call, and you can overrule any face (your call is kept when the photo is read again). The Library filters *Eyes closed* and *Eyes: needs check* and the smart-album rule *Eyes* find them. It is a suggestion only: nothing is rated, flagged, rejected or removed. It runs in the background (progress and Stop in the activity centre) only after your first *Check eyes…*, which may download the face models. In a test on 82 photos with 328 faces where each shoot was left out of the tuning in turn, about 7 of 10 faces it called closed were closed, and it found about 3 of 4 closed ones.
- **Open With and Edit With (macOS).** The photo context menu opens the selected originals in another program (their XMP sidecars are written first), or renders them as 16-bit TIFF (Adobe RGB or ProPhoto RGB) exactly as they are edited at the click, adds the TIFF to the library stacked with its photo and with its rating, label and keywords, and opens it in the program you choose. Photos with generated areas are not sent to Edit With yet.
- **Two-sided band gradients.** A linear gradient can be a band: full effect over a width centred on its line, fading out on both sides (Band width and Feather in the Layers panel; on the photo the solid lines set the width and the dashed lines the feather). Gradients without the band are unchanged.
- **Levels with handles on the histogram.** Levels shows the channel's histogram (RGB, R, G or B) with black, midtone and white handles, output handles, a field for each value and black and white point pickers; on the RGB tab the pickers make the clicked pixel neutral black or white. Saved Levels look as before.
- **Clipping warnings for shadows and highlights apart.** Two indicators above the histogram turn the shadow (blue) and highlight (red) warnings on the photo on and off separately and light up when that side clips; J toggles both. Clipping is now judged in the colour space the picture is shown or soft-proofed in, so saturated colours that fit a Display P3 screen are no longer marked clipped.
- **Targeted tone curve in every channel.** The targeted tool on the point curve sets or moves a point in the channel the curve shows (RGB, Luminance, R, G or B), and marks the clicked tone on the curve.
- **Undo of a batch edit takes it back on every photo.** One Undo reverses Sync, paste, presets, styles, Library Reset or Auto lens on all photos it changed, and Redo applies it again; each photo keeps its own History. All writes of a photo (edits, ratings, labels, keywords, XMP) now go through one queue per photo, so a later change never loses to an earlier one, and quitting waits for every write instead of a timer: Rawtonic shows what is still being saved and asks before quitting with unsaved changes.
- **Windows (experimental, unsigned).** A Windows x64 installer is built for the first time. It needs a graphics card with a Vulkan or DirectX 12 driver: PCs with only Microsoft's software renderer (WARP) get a clear message instead of a crash. With Vulkan the installer compiles the graphics pipelines once, so the first start does not wait for them, and the app falls back to another graphics interface when the first does not start.
- **Faster first photo in Develop.** The graphics shader the first photo needs is compiled from the moment the app starts, before the window exists; on a MacBook Pro (M5 Pro) the median cold click to first frame fell from 2.7 s to 1.3 s.
- **Fixes:** saved edits keep their lens profile after a lens-database update (one ulp of rounding made them look it up again at the next save); the export dialog's folder picker cancelled by you cancels the whole export, and a subfolder name of spaces only means no subfolder; keyword changes that fail on some photos update exactly the photos that saved and show the real message; no false "N XMP pending" after a clean import; smart albums with the AI-edit rule "No AI edits" open as saved; Remove shows its graphics preparation on first use; without a graphics card, thumbnails of reset photos show the camera's crop; on Windows the window keeps its size across restarts and photo names no longer show their folder; buttons keep their border and focus ring in high-contrast themes.

### Changes to existing edits

- **Saved edits export exactly as in alpha.9.1.** Every change below applies to new edits and to photos never edited; recipes you saved keep their render settings (for example `renderVersion` 7 or earlier) and were compared byte for byte, full size, against the previous code on each change (78 exports of 26 files for the shadow fix; 103 legacy edits for the camera crop; 186 for the R5 Mark II frame; 9 gradient edits for bands; 108 for Levels).
- **Noisy deep shadows of RAWs (`renderVersion` 8, new edits and never-edited RAWs).** Lifted shadows at high ISO keep their level and lose most of their per-colour tint (see *Cleaner lifted shadows at high ISO*); at low ISO the change stays below what the eye sees (8 × 8-pixel block means: 99.9th percentile 0.64 Oklab ΔE×100 on 15 frames; the largest single block 2.7). On the EOS 7D at ISO 12800 and two CHDK PowerShots the black level comes from the sensor's masked border, which changes their overall black. Bayer drafts, fast developments and DNGs with gain maps keep the old blacks.
- **Canon photos shot at 1:1, 4:3, 16:9 or 4:5 and the Samsung EX2F at 16:9, never edited:** their thumbnails, Develop view and exports are now cropped to the camera's frame (in our 113-file test, exactly the 13 files with such a frame changed). Photos you edited keep their crop.
- **Canon EOS R5 Mark II APS-C crop-mode CR3, never edited:** 5088 × 3392 pixels instead of 4970 × 3392.
- Thumbnails and previews of never-edited RAWs are made again once after the update.

### Known gaps

These are measured shortfalls or open work; they stay open until they are closed by measurement.

- **Deep shadows at ISO 12800 and above** still keep more colour cast than Camera Raw after lifting (about 1.0 against 0.5–0.8 on our flat-shadow measure); the remaining cause is known and not fixed in this release.
- **Windows** is experimental: unsigned, tested on one NVIDIA graphics card and on a build machine without a graphics card. It does not run without a Vulkan or DirectX 12 driver (Microsoft's software renderer is refused).
- **Open With and Edit With** are macOS only for now.
- **Find Duplicates** finds identical image data only, not visually similar photos. **Eyes** are suggestions; there is no eye-focus check.
- **Keyboard focus:** some dialogs do not yet return the keyboard focus or show a focus ring where they should.
- **Auto-advance with the physical Caps Lock key** is not yet verified on real hardware; Photo > Auto-advance works.
- **Object removal on large holes:** the AI fill is still poor on some objects (a kitten in foliage, a pot on a table).
- **Canon tethering** is experimental and untested on a real Canon body; leave it off unless you want to help test.
- The known gaps of alpha.9 and earlier not named here remain; the alpha.9 gaps *Undo after Sync or paste on several photos* and the *XMP pending* indicator are fixed.

## 0.1.0-alpha.9.1 — 2026-10-28

A small update to the spot tool: it is chosen as soon as you pick what it does, and what you healed, cloned or removed can now be found, hidden and deleted without knowing a keyboard shortcut. Existing edits and exports are unchanged.

### Highlights

- **Picking a spot mode chooses the tool.** In the Retouch tab's *Spot removal* group, clicking Remove, Heal, Clone or Red eye (or Red eye / Pet eye, or Detect objects) turns the spot tool on, as in Lightroom; opening the group or moving a slider does not, so a click on the photo still moves it while you look at the settings. The button shows the tool's key — *Start spot removal (Q)*, and *End spot removal (Q)* while it is on. Q or the tool bar opens the group if it was folded. Escape first deselects a spot, then ends the tool, and only then goes back to the Library.
- **A list of your spots.** Below the settings, *Corrections (n)* lists every heal and clone spot and every removal (in Red eye mode, every eye correction). Click a row to select it on the photo; its eye hides it in the photo and its exports (it stays drawn faintly on the photo so you can select it again); its bin deletes it. The header shows or hides all of them and deletes all of them (one step to undo).
- **Deleting on the photo.** Alt-click a spot, removal or eye correction to delete it; Alt-drag a box to delete every spot and removal it touches. With an object removal selected, Alt still takes away from its selection. A right-click on a spot selects it, and the settings that open with it have *Delete selected*; Delete or Backspace still deletes the selected one.

### Changes to existing edits

- None: saved edits render and export as in alpha.9.

## 0.1.0-alpha.9 — 2026-10-28

The ninth alpha. Object removal fills large holes with a new, larger AI model on macOS and Linux, and inpainting now always runs on your computer. Match Light gives a series shot in changing light consistent exposure and white balance, the culling keys follow the common professional layout (X rejects; the proof margin moved to Alt+X), and point curves put a point exactly where you click. Canon owners can help test tethered shooting behind an opt-in that is off by default. Two decoder and lens fixes change exports of some existing edits: photos from older Panasonic and Leica bodies, and JPEG, TIFF and other non-RAW photos with lens vignetting correction (see *Changes to existing edits*). Many fixes from a review of the whole interface make the dialogs, Preferences and the Develop column more consistent.

### Highlights

- **Remove fills large holes with a larger AI model.** Large removals (holes about 205 px thick or more) are now filled by a distilled diffusion inpainting model that sees the photo around the hole, on macOS through Core ML on the graphics card and on Linux on a GPU (CUDA when installed, else WebGPU); small holes keep the smaller, faster model. It is downloaded once (about 620 MB, with one progress bar from 0 to 100 % and Cancel) when you first remove a large object; without it, offline or when it does not run on this computer, the smaller model fills as before and a notice says so and why. A hole longer than the photo's shorter side uses the smaller model without the download. On a Mac mini (M4 Pro, 27 removals) a large fill took 2.7–4.8 s. Overlapping removals keep their own progress and Cancel; Escape cancels all of them. Fixes: the fill no longer copies dark blocks or leaves a light halo where no patch of the right shade exists; closing the spot tool's settings no longer removes anything at the click; Show removals hides and shows all removals of a photo (also in its exports) without deleting them; History names a removal "Remove".
- **Inpainting always runs on your computer.** The hidden, inherited setting that could send the photo and its mask to an external generative AI server is gone; Remove and generative fill always use the local models, and no saved setting can make them reach the network.
- **Match Light.** Library or filmstrip context menu → *Match Light…*: the open photo (or several, blended by capture time) is the reference, and every other selected photo gets the Exposure and white balance that make it look lit like the reference, so one look can then be synced across a wedding or event series shot in window light, tungsten, flash and evening light. Faces can lead (skin tones; the face models are downloaded only when you ask). Each photo shows a rendered preview, the change, a confidence and a reason; brackets, silhouettes and stage light are kept, uncertain ones are proposed unticked; Apply is one undo step for the whole batch. On our own test series, the skin colour difference to the reference fell from ΔE 26 to 7.7.
- **Culling keys and commands.** X rejects a photo (⇧X still works), 6–9 set red through blue labels, and **Alt+X toggles the proof margin** (it was X). Caps Lock or Photo > Auto-advance changes the existing preference; a single selection in the grid now advances too. N opens the selection in the Library's survey view. Colour labels on grid and filmstrip tiles are larger and show their name, so colour is not the only cue. Match Light and selection-aware merge, lens and negative commands are in the command palette.
- **Point curves are precise and work with the keyboard.** A click or drag on the curve puts the point under the pointer in every channel, also when the curve's box is not square. Focus a point and move it with the arrow keys, A inserts a point on the curve, Delete removes an inner point; each keyboard edit is one undo step.
- **Canon owners can help test tethered shooting.** Canon cameras are still not connected by default, as no Canon body has been tested yet. Preferences > Tethering now has **Experimental: Canon cameras (help test)**, off by default, with a plain warning. With it on, a Canon camera connects only with a memory card in it: Rawtonic sets it to save every photo on the card, never frees or deletes anything on the camera, and tells you about every shot fired from Rawtonic that did not arrive within a minute ("Shot 4 was not received. If the camera took it, it is on the memory card."), because the camera library can lose a Canon camera's notice of a new photo (a shot taken with the camera's own shutter button whose notice is lost, or a lost shot whose place a photo taken on the camera meanwhile takes, is not noticed: it is on the card, so compare the card with the library). The tether panel marks such a camera as experimental, and the camera test guides you step by step, counts the shots not received and marks its report as coming from the experimental path, with its photo steps as "pass (not certain)". The compatibility list shows Canon EOS as "experimental — testers wanted" ([how to help](docs/TETHER_CAMERAS.md#help-test-a-canon-camera)).
- **Protect freckles from the face retouch.** The Retouch tab has a protection brush: paint over freckles, moles and beauty marks the retouch should leave as they are (Protect / Erase, size and soft edge; Alt erases; [ and ] change the size; "Show protected areas" tints them). Protected pixels keep their values from before the retouch exactly, with a soft edge into the retouched skin. Edits without protection render as before.
- **Spot removal settings in the Retouch tab.** The Retouch tab has a *Spot removal* group (Remove, Heal, Clone, Red eye, size, feather, opacity) with a *Start spot removal* button; picking the spot tool opens the tab and ending it returns to where you came from. The right-click panel stays as a shortcut.
- **Link tonal wheels** in Color grading moves Shadows, Midtones and Highlights together by one hue rotation and saturation offset.
- **Filter by AI edits.** Library filters and smart albums can find photos with AI edits, missing or pending AI results, the model used for generated fills, and a file's declared digital source type.
- **Export queue and results.** With several recipes of the same format, each queued job shows its recipe, size and destination in your language; finished exports offer *Show in Finder* (or your file manager) for each output folder. *Choose a folder…* opens the folder dialog at once.
- **Zooming the window no longer makes the interface jump (macOS).** A double click on the title bar, Window > Zoom or ⌥-clicking the green button now resizes the layout with the window through the whole animation, as in a native Mac app.
- **One look for the whole interface.** The inherited dialogs (confirmations, Report a problem, Review what will be sent, Camera help, Rename, Create folder, Copy / Sync settings) and Preferences use the studio's buttons, panels and type; the top bar is the same in every view and at every width; text fields show keyboard focus; Preferences' segmented choices are one Tab stop with arrow keys.
- **Develop column on laptops.** *Layers & masks* starts folded while a photo has no layers, *Snapshots* starts folded and the Snapshots/History dock takes at most 30 % of the column, so white balance and exposure are in view. A layer's mask overlay shows only where masks are edited (not in Color, Detail, Crop or Retouch unless you draw a mask there); masks show while their range, colour, depth, refine-edge and feather sliders are dragged. The white balance picker has an eyedropper cursor; the tool column and the LUT row no longer scroll sideways in a narrow panel; the tool header's drag grip no longer covers the eye.
- **Library and import.** The import form's content stays readable at small window sizes, capture information no longer crowds the viewer footer, long folder-rule paths are shortened at rest, failed preview builds stay listed with their reason until you dismiss them, and an empty library offers *Import photos* (or *Clear filters* when filters hide everything).
- **Wording.** About describes the optional statistics, reviewed reports, model downloads, map access and update checks; the interface says "photo" for photographs throughout; Copy / Sync uses the Develop tools' names; the German interface was reworked.

### Changes to existing edits

- **Panasonic and Leica RW2 from older bodies (Lumix G/GH/GX/GF/GM, FZ, LX, TZ and others): exports change, also for existing edits.** These files decoded a few pixels wrong where the RAW file's data blocks wrap (about 0.02 % of their sensor samples), which showed as short bright or dark streaks of four pixels. They now decode correctly: the streaks are gone, and because noise reduction measures the photo's noise, which those samples had inflated, the rest of the photo moves by a few levels in 16 bits (a median of 10 of 65535). Previews and thumbnails of these photos are made again once.
- **JPEG, TIFF and other non-RAW photos with lens vignetting correction: exports change, also for existing edits.** A lens profile's vignetting describes lost light; on non-RAW photos it was applied to the file's encoded values, so the corners were lifted about twice as much as the profile says (measured on six camera JPEGs: 0.65–2.1 EV instead of 0.30–0.97 EV at the corners). It now lifts the light by exactly the profile's amount, in the view, masks, thumbnails and exports, so their corners come out darker than before (0.35 EV at the corner for an 18–55 mm zoom at f/6.3, about 0.55 EV for a 50 mm lens at f/1.8, 1.1 EV for a 16–35 mm at f/4.5); their thumbnails are made again once. A camera JPEG with its own tone curve still shows its corners up to 0.3 EV darker than the corrected RAW of the same frame, and camera JPEGs the camera already corrected are corrected twice when a profile is applied.
- All other saved edits, RAW photos and non-RAW photos without lens vignetting correction export as before. Removals you already made keep their fill; only new large removals use the new model.

### Known gaps

These are measured shortfalls or open work; they stay open until they are closed by measurement.

- **Undo after Sync or paste on several photos** may not restore every photo; check the photos after undoing a batch (a fix is in progress, not in this release).
- **XMP sidecar status:** the "XMP pending" indicator can stay on for photos whose sidecar was written, and failed sidecar writes from batch paste or styles are not always reported (fix in progress, not in this release).
- **Keyboard focus:** some dialogs do not yet return the keyboard focus or show a focus ring where they should (accessibility fixes in progress, not in this release).
- **Auto-advance with the physical Caps Lock key** is not yet verified on real hardware; Photo > Auto-advance works.
- **Object removal on large holes:** the AI fill is still poor on some objects (a kitten in foliage, a pot on a table); on holes with known content the error is a little higher than before the halo fix (LPIPS 0.0705 → 0.0715), mostly fine texture such as water coming out smoother.
- **Canon tethering** is experimental and untested on a real Canon body; leave it off unless you want to help test.
- The known gaps of alpha.8 and earlier not named here remain (see below).

## 0.1.0-alpha.8 — 2026-10-27

The eighth alpha. Film grain is new: built like the grain of photographic film, with four presets measured on film scans, and the Develop view now shows it as the export will look. The lens correction warp runs on the graphics card, so the Develop view and the export develop exactly the same pixels. Tethering fetches the photos a Fujifilm camera kept while Rawtonic was not connected, styles replace each other instead of piling up, AI tools need about 3 GB less memory, and object removal no longer paints new objects into large holes. The update notice now downloads the right installer, and after an update Rawtonic shows what changed.

### Highlights

- **Film grain.** Grain in new edits is now built like the grain of photographic film: grains of random size in each dye layer of a negative, strongest in the midtones and printed back, with a fine, irregular texture at every Size and Roughness instead of smooth blobs. **New grain looks different from before**; photos already edited with grain keep their grain exactly (old recipes export byte-identically). A new **Colour** slider replaces the structure choice for film grain: 0 (the default) is neutral grain like black-and-white film, higher values give each colour layer its own grain like colour negative film. Amount 50 is as strong as Lightroom's Amount 50 on a grey chart. On a laptop (M5 Pro) neutral grain costs about 3.6 ms per 4 MP preview frame, Colour about 9 ms.
- **Grain looks in Develop as it will in the export.** While a photo has grain, Develop renders its preview at the screen's own resolution and shows it pixel for pixel, whatever the High-DPI setting. At Fit the grain now looks like an export at the displayed size (95 % of its strength on a grey chart; it was 58 %, and coarser); at 100 % the view shows the export's grain pixel for pixel. A smaller export (for example a 2048 px JPEG) shows the full-size grain scaled down.
- **Grain presets measured on film.** The grain tool's built-in presets are new: *B&W 400 classic*, *B&W 100 fine*, *Colour negative 400* and *Colour negative 100 subtle*, each fitted to our own measurements of openly licensed film scans (grain strength, texture spectrum and, for the colour ones, how much the colour layers' grain goes together) for the photo enlarged from a 35 mm frame. The earlier Fine, Medium, Coarse, Heavy and Speckled presets are gone; photos they were applied to keep their grain.
- **Grain from Lightroom and Capture One** imports as film grain: Lightroom's Amount, Size and Roughness carry over 1:1; Capture One's grain types and Granularity are mapped onto Size and Roughness and Impact onto Amount by measurement (texture spectrum and strength on a grey chart).
- **One lens warp for the view and the export.** For new edits and never-edited photos the lens and geometry correction (lens profiles, the camera's own correction data, Transform, manual distortion, lens vignetting) is computed on the graphics card, the same way for the Develop view, 1:1 zoom, masks, thumbnails and exports, so what you see is the export's geometry to the pixel. Transform and Upright corrections on wide frames no longer drift by up to 1.3 px across the frame. Headless exports of 27 RAWs took 5 % less time on a Mac mini (M4 Pro).
- **Faster automatic chromatic aberration estimate.** The automatic lateral CA correction measures the photo about 60 % faster with exactly the same result; a headless export of 58 RAWs took 8 % less time (Linux PC, Ryzen 5 3600X).
- **Tethering fetches the photos waiting on the camera.** A camera that keeps tethered photos until the computer takes them (Fujifilm in USB tether mode) may hold photos taken while Rawtonic was not connected; on such a camera that is their only copy. They are now downloaded and added to the library when the camera is connected or reconnected, as Capture One does when it starts, while you can keep shooting. Each is freed on the camera only once it is safely in the library, and only with "Free photos on the camera once they are in the library" on; one that cannot be downloaded stays on the camera and is reported. Photos already in the library are recognised and not added twice. Tested on a Fujifilm GFX 50R: three waiting photos were listed at connect, downloaded, added to the library and only then freed on the camera, while new shots kept arriving. Note that the GFX 50R's queue does not survive switching the camera off or pulling the cable: photos still waiting then are lost on the camera's side, so connect before switching off.
- **Styles replace each other.** Applying a style while another one is applied takes the previous one back first; clicking the applied style again takes it back; the applied style is marked in the list and named by its Amount slider. Settings you changed yourself after applying a style keep your value when the style is replaced or removed (Capture One loses them). This also works on several selected photos at once and survives restarts; each click is one undo step.
- **AI tools use less memory.** AI models now free their working memory after each run, and on macOS freed large memory blocks go back to the system. In a session with Relight, Subject and Sky masks and full-size exports on three photos (24 GB MacBook), Develop needed 6.8 GB instead of 9.9 GB and the peak fell from 12.7 to 9.7 GB; the Library afterwards 2.7 instead of 4.9 GB. AI runs on the processor (not the Core ML ones) take 4–10 % longer for it.
- **Object removal no longer invents objects.** Remove with Detect objects could fill a large hole with a new, smeared object (a yellow blot where a kiwi was taken out of a fruit bowl): the AI fill model saw too little around the hole. It now sees 2.5 times the hole's size. On 17 objects in 13 RAWs the fill's error fell (LPIPS 0.075 → 0.071, colour error ΔE 13.2 → 11.6); on 10 held-out objects one invented object is gone, one fill is a little better and one worse, the rest alike. See Known gaps.
- **The update notice downloads the right file.** "Download" now opens the installer for your computer in the browser (the Apple Silicon or Intel DMG on a Mac, the AppImage on Linux) instead of the release page with all its files; when a release has no file for your computer, it opens the release page. "What's new" shows the release's notes in the app. Both come from the same once-a-day check as before; Rawtonic still downloads nothing itself.
- **What's new after an update.** The first start of a new version shows what changed in it, from the notes that come with the app (no network access); not after a first install. "Don't show after updates" or Preferences › General turns it off.
- **Notifications look like the rest of Rawtonic** and no longer cover the update notice: they stack above it in the lower right corner, use the app's type, colours and buttons, fade instead of sliding in, close only with their close button (a click elsewhere on one used to close it, also on one with a button to act on) and are announced politely to screen readers.
- **Fixes:** exports sized by Width, Height, Long or Short Edge (Fast (Web) among them) now have exactly the requested size (a 6000 × 3376 photo at 2048 px wide came out 2047 px wide); wide-gamut exports (Display P3, Adobe RGB, ProPhoto) no longer declare sRGB in their EXIF colour space tag, which their ICC profile contradicted; double-clicking a Relight slider resets it to the value a new light starts with (it reset to unrelated values); copying only an untouched grain Colour now copies it; the Styles list says so when you have no styles of your own yet; the mask overlay button shows a dot and names the mode when the mask display is not *Always*, and M / ⌥M (Alt+M) say which display they switched to.

### Changes to existing edits

- **Saved edits render as before.** New edits and never-edited RAWs get `renderVersion` 7 (the lens warp on the graphics card) and film grain version 3; photos edited with alpha.7.1 or earlier keep their versions and export byte-identical: 513 of 513 exports of 19 recipe kinds (`renderVersion` 1–6) on 27 RAWs, and old grain recipes 16 of 16, checked byte for byte against the build before each change.
- **Never-edited RAWs** can look a hair different, from the warp's new arithmetic: more than one level of 255 on at most 0.05 % of the values of a full-size 16-bit export; the largest single differences are a few edge-fringe pixels whose correction tips the other way.
- **Resized exports of existing edits** can be one pixel larger on a side (the requested size; see Fixes). Unresized exports are unchanged.
- **PNG exports** carry their EXIF in the standard `eXIf` chunk instead of a compressed text chunk, which ImageIO, browsers and ExifTool read; readers that only knew the old text chunk no longer see it.

### Known gaps

These are measured shortfalls against the better of the leading professional RAW editors; they stay open until they are closed by measurement.

- **Object removal on large holes:** the AI fill is still poor on some objects (a kitten in foliage, a pot on a table, a removed lemon), and on synthetic holes with known content the wider view is slightly worse (6 of 10 held-out holes); Removal was checked offline with the app's own fill code, not yet in the app's interface.
- **Memory in long mixed sessions:** a long session with many AI tools (Relight with face refinement, Subject, Sky, Depth, AI Denoise, repeated full-size exports, multi-photo TIFF and AVIF batches, the multi view, clone/heal and object removal) still reached 17.5 GB on a 24 GB Mac, and macOS twice asked Rawtonic to unload models (no crash; measured before the object removal change). The Core ML Subject and Sky models stay loaded in Develop (about 3 GB).
- **Exporting while you edit:** with the warp on the graphics card, the app's peak memory during an export is about 1 GB higher, and slider drags during an export are a little less even in their slowest frames; app exports are not faster (headless exports are).
- **Grain:** at Fit the view shows 95 % of the export's grain strength (the renderer at reduced size); the multi view and compare cells were not re-measured. Lightroom grain imported at Size 0 is not yet matched in strength.
- **Styles:** lens choice, layers (AI masks, gradients) and Relight lights a style sets are applied but not taken back when the style is removed or replaced.
- **Tethering:** after allowing camera access while the library changes during the connect, the tether panel can stay at "Connecting…"; disconnect and connect again. Fujifilm live view still does not work.
- **Export queue:** cancelling a batch whose photos were all already being exported says "Cancelled (3 of 3 exported)" and offers Resume, although everything was exported.
- The known gaps of alpha.7 and earlier not named here remain (see below).

### Details

- **GPU lens warp** (recipe `renderVersion` 6 and 7): version 6 computes the lens and geometry warp in 32-bit floating point with each output pixel placed from its own position instead of along a running sum per row; version 7 runs that arithmetic on the graphics card for every use of the warp (preview bases, the full-resolution frame, 1:1 regions, mask sources, the edge-fringe plan, thumbnails, merges and exports), so the display's full-resolution frame and the export's input are bit-identical (GPU tests on 6 recipes covering lens models, lateral CA, vignetting, perspective with manual distortion and a crop). Positions on 351 photos with camera or lensfun lens data stay within 0.0024 px of exact (the previous 64-bit warp: 0.0020 px). The processor warp remains for frames larger than the graphics card can hold; a failing graphics card warp fails the render instead of returning different pixels. Headless exports on a Mac mini M4 Pro: 27 RAWs × 2 recipes 20.9–21.5 → 19.7–20.1 s.
- **Automatic lateral CA estimate:** the edge check reads green first (99 % of the patches fail it), red and blue are averaged only for patches that pass, and each window's values are read once for all candidate models. On 187 RAWs the estimates of versions 1, 2 and 3 are bit-identical to before, and 299 of 299 16-bit exports are byte-identical; criterion benchmark 109 → 43.6 ms per estimate.
- **AI memory:** ONNX Runtime sessions run without their CPU memory arena (all systems), and the macOS app starts with `MallocLargeCache=0` (Info.plist), so freed large blocks leave the footprint. Exports with freshly computed Relight, Subject and Sky masks and an AI Denoise export are byte-identical to before. Not measured on Linux or before macOS 27.
- **Object removal:** the fill model's pipeline crops tightly around the hole before scaling it to 512 px, so the 1.5 × context Rawtonic passed never reached it; four 2 × 2 px marks at the corners of a box 2.5 × the hole now widen its view. Only the hole is taken from the model's result; kept pixels and the AI marking of the patch (IPTC Digital Source Type) are unchanged. The factor was chosen on 17 objects (2 ×, 2.5 ×, 3 × compared) and frozen before the held-out check.
- **EXIF colour space:** sRGB exports keep ColorSpace 1; Display P3, Adobe RGB and ProPhoto exports write 65535 (Uncalibrated), as EXIF prescribes for other spaces, and a retained Interop index becomes R98 (sRGB), R03 (Adobe RGB) or is removed. Checked by reading back 84 format, depth and source combinations with ExifTool.
- **Styles:** the applied style is stored in the recipe (`appliedStyle`, see `docs/RECIPE_SCHEMA.md`) with every setting it set and the value before; the renderer ignores it, so exports do not change.
- **Tethering diagnostics** for the Fujifilm live view work: `RAWTONIC_TETHER_TRACE=FILE` logs every camera request, `rawtonic-tether liveview` measures live view around shots; without them the helper behaves as before.

## 0.1.0-alpha.7.1 — 2026-10-26

A fix release for alpha.7 with one fix; edits and libraries are unchanged.

- **AI masks show on the first open of a photo.** When a photo with a stored preview was opened for the first time in a session, its AI masks (Subject, Sky, Depth, People, Object), AI patches and Relight rendered empty: a black mask overlay and layer thumbnail, and the layer had no effect until the photo was opened again. They now show and work right away.

## 0.1.0-alpha.7 — 2026-10-26

The seventh alpha, and the first one signed with an Apple Developer ID and notarized by Apple: on a Mac it now opens like any other app, without the Terminal step. Saturated colours keep their hue, the lens corrections Rawtonic reads from the camera's own data now frame and straighten the photo as the camera does on Sony, Nikon Z, Canon EOS R5 Mark II / R6 Mark III, Samsung NX, Panasonic and Fujifilm, and batch exports take about a third less time. There is also a first, experimental build for Intel Macs.

### Highlights

- **Signed and notarized for macOS.** The DMG and the app are signed with Rawtonic's Developer ID and notarized by Apple: open the DMG, drag Rawtonic to Applications and double-click it. macOS no longer calls it damaged, and `xattr -cr` or "Open Anyway" are not needed any more. Checked on the release files as downloaded from a browser (with the quarantine flag macOS gives downloads): Gatekeeper accepts the DMG and the app as notarized Developer ID software. In the signed app, every AI mask (Subject and Sky on Core ML; Depth, People, Object on the bundled AI runtime) and tethering (Sony A7 III over USB: settings, capture, the RAW in the library 2.5 s after the click) work as before.
- **Saturated colours keep their hue.** Colours a photo holds that the export's colour space (sRGB, Display P3, Adobe RGB), the screen or the soft-proofed space cannot show used to be clipped channel by channel, which bent their hue: blue LEDs and stage lights went purple or cyan, red-orange flowers red, orange stage light yellow. They are now brought into the space at their own hue; colours inside the space are not touched. On 24 public photos with neon, stage light, LEDs, flowers and sunsets, the hue error of the sRGB export against the photo's own wide-gamut rendering fell from 5.3° to 2.4° (IPT hue; Capture One 4.0°, Camera Raw 5.8° against their own wide-gamut renderings), better than before on 24 of 24 photos and better than Capture One on 19; Display P3 exports 4.5° → 2.4°. The Develop view, soft proofing and exports agree.
- **Lens corrections from the camera's data, as the camera applies them.** For never-edited RAWs and new edits with the camera's own correction data (details below):
  - **Nikon Z:** framed as the camera frames it (Rawtonic showed 0.3–1.5 % more of the frame than the camera): on 57 public Z NEFs of 13 bodies the geometry against the camera's own JPEG is now within 0.15 px (median; was 18.9 px).
  - **Canon EOS R5 Mark II and R6 Mark III:** their distortion is now corrected from the camera's data (it was left uncorrected): against Camera Raw's default rendering the R6 Mark III is within 0.2 px median (was 12.6), the R5 Mark II within 0.8 px (was 31.9).
  - **Sony:** the camera's correction table is followed exactly, including the strong barrel of the RX100 III–V, ZV-1 and RX0; on 15 Sony RAWs against Camera Raw the error fell from up to 31 px to 0.1–1.9 px median.
  - **Fujifilm:** the camera's own correction table is followed exactly (X-S20 at 16 mm within 0.7–0.8 px of the camera's JPEG, was 2.4–2.6; 129 RAFs measured), and the photo is framed as the camera frames it, without an extra 0.2 % crop.
  - **Panasonic** bodies zoom in by the scale they store, as the camera does (framing within 0.08 % of the camera's JPEG on 214 frames, was up to 1.2 %); the G7 and G70 correct their 3:2, 16:9 and 1:1 frames at the right radius.
