  - **Samsung NX** (NX200 and later, NX mini, Galaxy NX, EX1 / EX2F / WB2000): distortion corrected from the camera's data (within 0.05–0.09 px of Camera Raw where it uses the same data; was up to 20 px median).
- **Faster batch exports.** On a Mac mini M4 Pro, exporting 27 public RAWs from the Library as a 2048 px JPEG and a full-size 16-bit TIFF takes about 25 s instead of 37–38 s with alpha.6, and from the command line 19–20 s instead of 31–33 s. Develop stays fluid during the export (54–56 previews per second while dragging a slider, as before). This was measured step by step, each change against the build before it (A/B/B/A runs), not in one run against alpha.6. The colour noise reduction and the edge-fringe correction now run on the graphics card, and exports use the same specialized, faster renderer as the Develop view.
- **Intel Macs (experimental).** A separate `Rawtonic_0.1.0-alpha.7_x86_64.dmg`, signed and notarized like the Apple Silicon one, with the same AI runtime and tethering parts. It has run only under Rosetta 2 on Apple Silicon Macs, not on an Intel Mac yet; see Known gaps.
- **Linux AppImage:** a headless export with relative paths (`./Rawtonic.AppImage export photo.ARW --output out.jpg`) and files passed to an already running Rawtonic now refer to the folder you ran the command in (they ended up inside the AppImage's read-only mount and failed); a plain export to a folder that cannot be written stops at once with a clear message.
- **Storage:** preview size limits and clearing previews live in one place, Preferences › Storage. Clearing only the 1:1 previews keeps the standard previews, and Rawtonic asks first, because photos on disconnected drives cannot be viewed without them. Thumbnails you looked at recently are now kept longer when the cache is trimmed, and many thumbnails missing at once are rebuilt a few at a time instead of all together.

### Changes to existing edits

- **Saved edits render as before.** New edits and never-edited RAWs get the new versions (`renderVersion` 5 with the hue-keeping colour mapping, `cameraGeometryVersion` 7, `colorNoiseVersion` 11, `fringeVersion` 5); photos edited with alpha.6.1 or earlier keep theirs and export byte-identical: 112 of 112 exports of alpha.6.1-style edits on 28 RAWs of all makers, and 432 of 432 exports of 16 recipe kinds on 27 RAWs, checked byte for byte.
- **Never-edited RAWs** can look a little different where the new versions apply: framing and straight lines on Nikon Z, Sony, Fujifilm, Panasonic, Samsung NX and Canon R5 Mark II / R6 Mark III bodies (28 of 70 public RAWs of all makers in our pre-release check), and very saturated colours beyond the output space (a blue lamp, yellow flowers, saturated foliage). Everything else differs by less than half a level of 255 on average, mostly in single pixels.
- New edits export with the Develop view's specialized renderer: the result differs from the old general renderer by at most one level of 255 in about 0.002 % of the values of a full-size 8-bit export (more in a JPEG, from its compression).
- Recipe files written by hand or by scripts without a `lensCorrectionMode` are treated as never-edited and get the current camera corrections; every recipe the editor saves carries one.

### Known gaps

These are measured shortfalls against the better of the leading professional RAW editors; they stay open until they are closed by measurement.

- **Batch export** is faster but not yet re-measured against Lightroom and Capture One (alpha.4 on a laptop: 20 RAWs to a web JPEG and a 16-bit TIFF in about 17 s, Lightroom 8 s, Capture One 9 s). It is now limited by the processor; the lens correction warp is the largest part of that work.
- **Very saturated colours:** the hue now stays, but in areas far outside sRGB, texture and flat patches stay at alpha.6's level, below Capture One's, because Rawtonic's rendering pushes saturated colours further beyond sRGB than the other editors do; the mapping keeps somewhat less colourfulness than the old clipping.
- **Lens corrections from camera data, not yet as the camera:** Canon R5 Mark II full frame 0.8 px median against Camera Raw (one constant of Canon's data reading was fitted on two files and checked on a third, as no more files carry that data); R5 Mark II APS-C crop files are corrected but developed in an off-centre frame; the Samsung EX2F's 16:9 frame and Sony 16:9 frames whose correction reaches beyond the crop (HX95, ZV-1 II, RX0 II, ZV-E10) keep the previous correction; a Nikon Z 6 with the 24-70 mm f/4 is shifted by 1.4 px; near the corners of wide Fujifilm lenses the cameras correct a little less than their tables say (X-S20 at 16 mm up to 11 px); Olympus / OM System and Nikon Z lateral chromatic aberration data are not used, as the automatic CA correction did as well or better.
- **Intel Macs:** not yet run on Intel hardware. Not qualified: Intel processors and Intel or AMD graphics and their drivers, speed on Intel Macs, macOS 13–14 on Intel, tethering a real camera. Fujifilm X-Trans RAWs export very slightly differently from the Apple Silicon build (at most 6 of 255 levels in 0.004 % of a JPEG's pixels); other RAWs export identically.
- The Linux AppImage is not signed (as is usual for AppImages).
- The known gaps of alpha.6 and alpha.4 not named here remain (see below).

### Details

- **Hue-keeping colour mapping** (recipe `renderVersion` 4, part of 5): a colour outside the output space is clipped to the space as before and then given back its own CAM16 hue at the clipped colour's lightness and chroma, lowering the chroma only where that hue holds less (standard CAM16 viewing conditions, nothing fitted). Display, export and soft proof share the step. Measured on 24–25 public PlayRaw photos from discuss.pixls.us, each editor's sRGB render against its own ProPhoto render over the pixels outside sRGB; hue also better in CIELAB (3.65° → 2.16°; Capture One 3.35°) and Oklab (7.35° → 4.19°; Capture One 3.78°), and in both halves of a held-out split. Smooth gradients outside sRGB stay smooth (no neighbouring pixels more than one 8-bit level apart in the GPU test).
- **Nikon Z framing** (`cameraGeometryVersion` 2): the camera stores the scale its JPEG shows the corrected frame at; Rawtonic now reads it per file (nothing fitted) and frames the photo with it (zoom against the camera's JPEG 0.9996–1.0003, was 0.9983–1.0154; per-file median within 0.5 px on 44 of 57 NEFs, was 7). The Z 6 with the 24-70 mm f/4 and the Z 7's crop image sizes keep a shift of 0.6–1.4 px that Camera Raw shows as well.
- **Canon R5 Mark II / R6 Mark III distortion** (`cameraGeometryVersion` 3): the distortion table in the camera's lens record is now read and applied (pincushion keeps the corners on the sensor's corners as Camera Raw does, barrel keeps the camera's framing). Canon's table is applied at a share of 0.8, fitted on Camera Raw's correction of an R5 Mark II and an R6 Mark III and held out on the camera's own JPEG of an EOS R50 V with 8 % barrel distortion (0.15 px median); one constant for every body and lens. Before/after against Camera Raw (median / largest, px): R6 Mark III full frame 12.6 / 15.4 → 0.2 / 0.75, crop 4.9 / 6.0 → 0.08 / 0.9, R5 Mark II 31.9 / 37.6 → 0.75–0.78 / 1.9–2.3. Other Canon bodies are unchanged.
- **Faster exports, step by step** (Mac mini M4 Pro, 27 RAWs, 2048 px JPEG + full-size 16-bit TIFF, A/B/B/A): colour noise on the graphics card (`colorNoiseVersion` 11; command line 30.8–31.9 → 26.6–27.4 s, app 37.3–38.9 → 34.2–34.7 s), edge fringe on the graphics card (`fringeVersion` 5; 25.5–28.3 → 25.2–26.0 s, processor load −30 %, quality equal to version 4 on the 61-file fringe test set), exports with the specialized renderer (`renderVersion` 5; 24.3–25.5 → 21.0–21.1 s, app 31.6–33.7 → 25.0–27.5 s, graphics card work per photo 705 → 458 ms), and the fringe result going straight into the renderer instead of through the processor (19.2–20.0 s from 20.8–22.4, app 24.5–25.0 s from 27.0–27.5, byte-identical). 200 copies of the same RAWs: 220 s with colour noise version 10 → about 145 s. Every single export now renders the way one render for several recipes does, so both give the same bytes on every graphics card (they differed by up to one level on NVIDIA).
- The edge-fringe correction of new edits (`fringeVersion` 5) needs the graphics card; without one the photo reports an error instead of falling back to the processor (saved edits with versions 1–4 keep the processor path).
- **Fujifilm lens corrections from the camera's data follow the camera's own correction table exactly** (recipe `cameraGeometryVersion` 7, new edits and never-edited RAWs): the warp now runs through the knots Fujifilm stores, as the camera reads them, instead of a three-coefficient formula fitted to them, which could not follow wide lenses. Against the cameras' own JPEGs of 129 raw.pixls.us RAFs the X-S20 at 16 mm is now within 0.7–0.8 px median (was 2.4–2.6), the X-T4 at 16 mm 1.2–1.35 (was 2.1–2.2), the GFX 100S II 0.74–0.84 (was 1.1–1.2); the root-mean-square error fell on 20 files and rose by more than 0.1 px on 4, none by more than 0.15 px except the FinePix S1, which the camera crops far differently anyway. Near the corners of wide lenses the cameras correct a little less than their tables say, which the formula partly hid (X-S20 at 16 mm: largest error 7.4 → 11.3 px). Saved edits keep their look.
- **Sony lens corrections from the camera's data follow the camera's own correction table exactly** (recipe `cameraGeometryVersion` 6, new edits and never-edited RAWs): the warp now runs through the knots Sony stores instead of a three-coefficient formula fitted to them, which could not follow the strong barrel of the RX100 III / IV / V and ZV-1 or the wavy table of the RX0. Against the cameras' own JPEGs the RX0 is now within 0.17 preview pixels (was 0.54), the RX100 III / IV / V and ZV-1 within 0.26–0.30 (was 0.38–0.49); the shape against Camera Raw improves on those bodies (ZV-1 0.60 → 0.12 px, RX100 III 0.87 → 0.50). Camera Raw frames them 0.03–0.13 % wider than the cameras do; Rawtonic keeps the camera's framing. Saved edits keep their look.
- **Samsung NX lens distortion is corrected from the camera's own data** (recipe `cameraGeometryVersion` 4, new edits and never-edited RAWs): SRW files of the NX200 and later, NX mini, Galaxy NX and the EX1 / EX2F / WB2000 compacts get the distortion model the camera applied to its own JPEG, exactly as stored (no refit). On the six raw.pixls.us files where Camera Raw corrects from the same data the geometry now matches its rendering to 0.05–0.09 px median, at most 0.66 px (before 0.9–20 px median, up to 77 px at the corners); lenses Camera Raw corrects with its own profiles instead (NX 30mm, 18-55mm, 50-200mm, the first NX bodies, EX1, WB2000) now follow the camera's JPEG (0.05–0.23 px on five, 1–2.5 px on three). Files whose camera did not apply its data (NX11 + 18-55mm at 55 mm, NX210 + 60mm) stay uncorrected, as their camera JPEGs are; the EX2F's 16:9 frame is still developed as the full 4:3 sensor. Samsung's lateral CA and vignetting data are not used (the automatic CA correction measures less CA left than the data's own values would). Saved edits keep their look.
- **Sony lens corrections from the camera's data are much closer to the camera's and Camera Raw's** (recipe `cameraGeometryVersion` 1, new edits and never-edited RAWs): Sony's distortion table now runs from the image centre to the corner of the sensor's full active area, compacts crop inside the sensor corners as the camera does, and bodies with interchangeable lenses keep the largest frame without empty edges. On 15 Sony RAWs against Camera Raw the geometry error as rendered fell from up to 31 px (median) and 78 px (corners) to 0.1–1.9 px median and 0.3–5.2 px at the corners: 9 of them (RX100 VI in 3:2, 16:9, 4:3 and 1:1, RX100 VII, RX10, RX10 III and IV, A6400) within 0.3 / 1.1 px, the RX100, RX100 III/IV, ZV-1 and RX0 still 1.0–1.9 px median (from 21–31 px for all but the RX0). On 26 further Sony RAWs with interchangeable lenses or compacts the error against the camera's own JPEG fell on most bodies. 16:9 frames whose correction reaches beyond the crop (HX95, ZV-1 II, RX0 II, ZV-E10) keep the previous correction for now. Saved edits keep their correction.
- **Panasonic and Fujifilm lens corrections from the camera's data now frame the photo as the cameras do** (recipe `cameraGeometryVersion` 5, new edits and never-edited RAWs): newer Panasonic bodies (G9, GH5, GH5S, GH6, S5, G85, GX85, GM1, GF7 and others) zoom in by the scale they store with their lens data, the G7 and G70 correct their 3:2, 16:9 and 1:1 frames at the right radius, and Fujifilm's distortion table now starts at the image centre and keeps the largest frame without empty edges, without the extra 0.2 % crop. Against the cameras' own JPEGs of raw.pixls.us RAWs, the framing difference fell from up to 1.2 % to at most 0.08 % on 214 Panasonic frames (geometry error with the JPEGs' own 1–3 px offset removed 0.54 px median, the measurement's floor), and the geometry error as rendered on 129 Fujifilm RAWs from 3.1 to 1.3 px median (X-E4 3.5 → 0.3 px, X-T5 with XF18mm 5.1 → 0.6 px, GFX 100S II 6.8 → 1.1 px). Not yet as the camera: wide Fujifilm lenses whose correction the lens model cannot follow exactly (X-S20 and X-T4 at 16 mm, GFX100RF: 2–2.6 px; the X-T4 got 0.9 px worse), bodies that crop barrel distortion further than their data say (XF1, XQ1, XQ2, X-A5, X-A10, X-T100 and older FinePix compacts), and the X-T5's Sports Finder crop. Saved edits keep their correction.
- Batch exports are faster: the colour noise reduction now builds, measures and rebuilds its multi-scale pyramid on the graphics card instead of the processor, where it was the largest single stage of an export (recipe `colorNoiseVersion` 11, new edits and never-edited RAWs). It is version 10's filter, not a new look: on 54 high-ISO RAWs at Color 25, 50 and 100 the colour edges, colour detail, colour noise, isolated specks and the error against clean ground-truth frames (RawNIND) are the same as version 10's in every ISO band (edges at least 0.9999 of version 10, specks per million pixels 11.6 → 11.5, 9.1 → 9.1 and 7.8 → 7.8); pixels differ only by about 1 of 65 535 on average, from reading the 16-bit input the renderer holds and the graphics card's order of summing. On a Mac mini M4 Pro, exporting 27 public RAWs as a 2048 px JPEG and a full-size 16-bit TIFF took 26.9 instead of 31.1 seconds (median of 4 runs each) and 200 copies 184 instead of 220 seconds; in the app, the same export from the Library took 34.2–34.7 instead of 37.3–38.9 seconds with Develop drags meanwhile as smooth as before (54 against 53.5 previews per second, median of 24 drags) and up to 2 GB less memory. A preview and its export now take the same path for this filter. Saved edits render exactly as before (versions 1 to 10 byte-identical on 27 RAWs with 13 recipes); photos beyond the graphics card's texture size limit keep the processor's pyramid.

## 0.1.0-alpha.6.1 — 2026-10-25

A fix release for alpha.6 with only the fixes from the first alpha.6 tests; edits and libraries are unchanged.

- **A double click on a photo in the Library opens Develop** (as D or Return); E and Space stay the loupe for looking through. The loupe's bar has a Develop button, and the Library button in Develop returns to the loupe when Develop was opened from it (G still goes to the grid).
- **F full screen on macOS:** after F into full screen and F back, the next F did nothing until a click. F, F11 and ⌃⌘F now toggle every time and keep the keyboard focus, also when pressed quickly again during macOS's full-screen animation.
- **Shortcuts after a slider drag:** while the slider just dragged kept the focus, only Undo and Redo reached the app, so M did not show the mask overlay again. A focused slider now keeps only the keys that move it (arrows, Home/End, Page Up/Down); M, F, ratings, the tool keys and [ ] for the brush size work right after a drag.
- **No grid flicker** when the Library comes back from Develop (the grid drew three large columns for a frame before its real width was known).

## 0.1.0-alpha.6 — 2026-10-25

The sixth alpha: the changes since alpha.5, many of them from the first use tests of alpha.5. A native menu bar on the Mac and a command palette, faster imports and culling, undo for ratings and flags, built-in styles and presets per tool, a range wheel and skin tones in the Colour Editor, a photograph-only full screen, Highlights that go further in night scenes, and a Storage page in Preferences.

### Highlights

- **Menus and commands.** On macOS a full menu bar (File, Edit, Photo, View, Window, Help) with Open Recent and Export with Recipe; its key equivalents follow the shortcuts you set in Preferences. A command palette (⇧⌘P / Ctrl+Shift+P, rebindable) finds and runs any command; on Linux the same menus sit in the top bar's … menu. The view keys G/E/D and the flag keys P/U/⇧X can now be rebound.
- **Import.** Importing 200 RAW+JPEG pairs from an exFAT card with a backup copy now takes about 10 s until the files are in place instead of 27 s (Lightroom 11.5 s, Capture One 23.7 s on the same laptop): the library copy and the backup are made from one read of each source, then each copy and the source read again are compared byte for byte before anything is published. The import dialog has metadata presets (creator, copyright with the capture year, description, usage terms, credit, keywords; contact details only when you tick them) that fill empty fields, or overwrite them when you say so.
- **Culling.** The next photo shows at once: Develop, the Library loupe and the full screen develop the neighbours ahead in the direction you step, show the camera's embedded JPEG (colour-managed, framed like the developed photo) until the developed frame is in, and Auto-advance after a rating or flag key can be turned on in Preferences › General (off by default). Ratings, flags and colour labels can be undone (⌘Z / Ctrl+Z), also for many photos at once.
- **Full screen and loupe.** F shows only the photograph, full screen, from the Library and Develop (the window's own full screen moved to F11 / ⌃⌘F); I shows the photograph's facts over it. The Library has a loupe (E, Space or a double click; G back to the grid). Ratings and flags there apply to the photo shown.
- **Presets.** Grain, Vignette, Color grading, Black & white, Sharpening and Noise reduction have a preset button in their header: hover to preview, click to apply, save your own. 39 built-in presets and styles with descriptive names (Preferences › General hides them). Presets with AI masks now find the subject, sky or person again on each photo they are applied to, instead of reusing the first photo's mask.
- **Colour Editor.** A range wheel to see and drag every colour range, and a Skin tone mode with Uniformity for hue, saturation and lightness. On four public portraits it evens skin out at least as much as Capture One's Skin Tone at uniformity 50 and 100, while changing less outside the skin. Capture One styles with skin tone entries import into it; the conversion is approximate and the import report says so.
- **Masks.** Every mask kind is one click away in the row above the layer list. An Object mask takes several clicks or boxes: Shift adds another object, Alt (⌥) takes a part away, ⌘Z / Ctrl+Z takes the last one back.
- **Highlights in night scenes.** New edits lower Highlights further in dark frames (lamps, candles, lit windows): on 7 of 7 dark test frames −50 and −100 now reach at least as far as Camera Raw's; brighter frames render as before (see Changes to existing edits).
- **Before/After** can split top and bottom as well as left and right (the Compare button's menu, or ⇧Y to cycle); each session remembers its choice.
- **Storage** in Preferences shows how much space caches, downloaded AI models, logs and the open library's cache take, with limits and buttons to clear each. Clearing never touches photos, sidecars, catalogs, saved masks, Output or Trash; removed thumbnails are made again when needed.
- **Faster AI masks after a fresh install.** On a Mac the first Subject mask took 13.7 s; Rawtonic now prepares the Subject and Sky models in the background while you are in Develop, so a click 25 s after opening the first photo took 1.5–3.3 s (four runs). A click within about 15 s of opening Develop still waits for part of it.
- **Library header.** Search, filters and sorting share the session bar in wide windows. Grain moved to the Detail tab after Noise reduction; Vignette, Light effects and LUT are separate tools in Adjust.
- **First start** asks on which side the Develop tools should be (also in Preferences).
- **Fixes:** unticking a People part (e.g. Lips) left its mask on the photo; the tone curve followed a drag only about 4 times a second (now every frame); targeted adjustment missed the first press on the photo; deleted photos stayed in Previous Import and albums; the session's Trash folder had no trash icon.

### Changes to existing edits

- **Highlights:** new edits get `toneVersion` 3. Photos edited before keep their version and render as before, and so do recipes that lower Highlights or raise Shadows above +50; the editor moves other recipes to 3 when it opens them, where nothing renders differently. Never-edited RAWs render as before (Highlights at 0 is unchanged).
- Exports of existing recipes are otherwise unchanged: the faster export resize and colour-noise steps and the faster lens correction produce identical files (checked byte for byte on 27 public RAWs, 54 files).
- **Photos moved to the system Trash** leave albums and Previous Import at once (catalog schema 16; libraries are upgraded when opened). Photos trashed with an earlier alpha cannot be told apart from photos whose drive is disconnected, so they stay listed as offline.
- **Keys:** F is the photograph-only full screen (window full screen F11 / ⌃⌘F); D in Develop no longer has a second meaning for the panels.

### Known gaps

These are measured shortfalls against the better of the leading professional RAW editors; they stay open until they are closed by measurement.

- **Batch export** is still about 2× slower than Lightroom and Capture One (alpha.4: 20 RAWs to a web JPEG and a 16-bit TIFF in about 17 s, Lightroom 8 s, Capture One 9 s). This round made it about 4–11 % faster on a Mac mini (27 RAWs, two recipes: 41 → 37–38 s in the app); the rest is develop work on the CPU. Not re-measured against Lightroom and Capture One yet.
- **Culling** has not been re-measured with real key presses against Lightroom and Capture One yet. Scripted on a Mac mini: the next photo's first pixels after about 90 ms, its full development after about 0.2 s (was 0.62 s) at a steady pace; when you step faster than about every 0.6 s, the camera JPEG shows within about 20 ms and the development follows after about 0.43 s.
- **Import with a metadata preset** takes about 1–2 s longer for 200 photos than without.
- **Highlights −100 in dark scenes** now goes further than Camera Raw's on some frames (candles, snow) and looks darker and flatter there; about −50 to −60 matches Camera Raw's −100. On an overexposed bulb Camera Raw still lowers the bright area much further.
- **Skin tone** import from Capture One styles is approximate, and the Colour Editor as a whole is not yet at the better editor's level.
- **Before/After side by side** (two panes) is not available yet; the split shows one photo.
- **Storage**: removing AI models takes effect at the next start; most languages other than English and German show the Storage page in English.
- **Menus**: single-key shortcuts are not shown in the macOS menu items (only those with ⌘).
- The known gaps of alpha.4 not named here remain (see below).

### Details

- Import metadata presets add creator, copyright (with a capture-year token), description, usage terms, credit and keywords. They fill empty fields unless explicitly set to overwrite; contact details are opt-in. Copy writes XMP after verified publication, while Add keeps metadata in the catalog pending the usual later XMP sync. Independent RAW/JPEG photographs sharing a stem retain the existing XMP ownership limit and report a warning.
  Selecting a preset also adopts readable embedded descriptions, keywords and IPTC fields so the new sidecar preserves them. Unreadable embedded XMP is ignored, as in capture-fact reading, and never blocks the photo's import.

## 0.1.0-alpha.5 — 2026-10-24

A fix release for alpha.4: its downloads were built without the address of Rawtonic's report relay, so **Report a problem**, crash reports, camera test results and usage statistics could not be sent from the app (only **Copy report** and **Open on GitHub** worked). alpha.5 sends them as alpha.4's notes describe. Nothing else changes; edits render exactly as in alpha.4.

### Details

- Optimized builds send contributions to `https://relay.rawtonic.cc` unless `RAWTONIC_RELAY_URL` names another relay at build time (empty: none); debug builds and tests have no relay unless it is set. Before, every build without the variable, including the alpha.4 packages, had none.

## 0.1.0-alpha.4 — 2026-10-24

The fourth alpha: about 130 changes since alpha.3. Red eye correction, library search with smart albums, RAW+JPEG pairs and imports that resume, moves to other drives, a copy/paste picker, named snapshots and an export queue panel arrive. Sharpening, colour noise reduction, fringes, curves and Clarity were reworked and measured against the leading RAW editors, and you can now report a problem or a crash from inside the app, without an account.

### Highlights

- **Red eye and pet eye correction.** A new mode of the spot tool: click an eye and the pupil is found and corrected (drag to size the search circle), with Pupil Size and Darken; pet eyes keep a catchlight. The correction stays on the eye through crops, turns and lens corrections. Lightroom's red eye corrections come over with catalog imports.
- **Help without an account.** *Report a problem* (Help menu on macOS, the … menu in Library and Develop) sends your description with optional system details, log, a screenshot of the window and the photo file; after a crash Rawtonic offers a crash report at the next start; the tether camera test can send its result; a RAW that does not open offers help to get its camera supported. Nothing is sent before you have seen the exact text and clicked Send, and every dialog can instead copy the report or open it on GitHub. See [Privacy](PRIVACY.md).
- **Update check and opt-in usage statistics.** Rawtonic looks for a new release at startup at most once a day (can be turned off; it never downloads or installs anything itself). Anonymous weekly usage statistics — which camera models were opened and which tools were used, without any id — are sent only if you turn them on in Preferences (off by default).
- **Library search and smart albums.** Find photos by part of any word, filter by camera, lens, focal length, aperture, ISO, shutter speed, capture year and keywords (Shift-click picks a range), and save the filter as a smart album with nested all/any/none rules and live counts.
- **Import.** A RAW and its JPEG come in as one photo; an import cut off by a quit, a crash or a card taken out finishes when the library opens again, without duplicates; CR3 files and merged DNGs get their capture details, and the rating the camera wrote is taken over. Imports and exports onto exFAT drives on macOS work (they failed with "os error 45").
- **Move photos and whole folders to another drive**, verified byte for byte before the originals go to the Trash, undoable, keeping Finder tags and other extended attributes; photos can also move to exFAT drives on macOS.
- **Copy, paste and sync** with a picker for tools and single layers, *Previous* (paste the settings of the photo open before) and Lens Correction.
- **Snapshots** have names and thumbnails, sit with History in a dock in every Develop tab, and can be compared with the current edit in Before/After (F12).
- **Export.** Photos exported with several recipes are developed and rendered once for all of them (20 RAWs to a web JPEG and a 16-bit print TIFF: about 17 s instead of 31–35 s, every file byte-identical); a queue panel shows each photo's progress and the time left, with pause and cancel per job; Develop stays fluid while a batch exports; stripped exports keep creator and copyright.
- **Curves.** A Luminance channel (contrast without extra saturation or hue shifts), curves with more than 16 points, curves in a wide working space, and targeted adjustment for the region curve; Lightroom tone curves import as Camera Raw draws them.
- **Detail.** Sharpening follows the photo's brightness and follows Camera Raw's Radius and Masking more closely, its Amount goes to 150, and JPEGs get stronger sharpening at high amounts. Colour noise reduction leaves fewer colour specks (on 55 high-ISO RAWs now fewer than Camera Raw at Color 25 and 50 and Capture One at its default), luminance noise reduction fewer single-pixel specks. Purple and green fringes from axial chromatic aberration are corrected automatically, edges between two saturated colours are cleaner, and thin dark lines no longer get coloured stripes.
- **Looks.** Clarity leaves fewer halos beside narrow bright edges; film grain looks the same on every graphics card; LUTs go to 200 % amount, import as packs from folders, and 1D and shaper .cube files are read.
- **Speed.** Multi view zooms sooner (the selected photo at 100 % 0.98 → 0.67 s, a 12-photo zoom 4.6 → 2.5 s, with a first view of each region within about 0.5–0.7 s); the first frame of a never-opened RAW on Linux comes after 0.75 s instead of 1.2 s (on a cold NVIDIA driver cache 2.9 s instead of 4.4 s); the first slider drag after opening a photo is no longer slowed by previews built in the background.
- Many fixes: Lightroom catalog imports render their gradients, brushes, colour ranges, spots and red eyes in exports of photos never opened in Develop; lens profiles from the lens database are looked up at the photo's aperture and its sensor's crop factor (Canon, Fujifilm, Olympus and Panasonic RAWs got wrong vignetting and distortion corrections); Save & Relaunch restarts the app again; Return in a new export recipe's name saves the recipe instead of starting an export.

### Changes to existing edits

New behaviour is tied to recipe version keys (`renderVersion` 2 and 3, `sharpenVersion` 3–6, `colorNoiseVersion` 8–10, `lumaNoiseVersion` 2, `fringeVersion` 3 and 4, `clarityVersion` 2, `grainVersion` 2, `maskVersion` 4 for new layers, `curves.version` 2 and 3), so photos edited with alpha.3 render as before; never-edited RAWs develop with the new defaults. Exceptions:

- **Lens profiles from the lens database** for photos without saved lens parameters (never-edited RAWs and their previews) are looked up at the photo's f-number and its body's crop factor and keep their whole lateral chromatic aberration correction: 31 of 62 public test RAWs get a different vignetting, distortion or CA correction. Saved edits keep the lens parameters they were saved with.
- **Photos imported from a Lightroom catalog and never opened** now export with their gradients, brushes, colour ranges, spots and red eye corrections (they were left out until the photo was opened in Develop; opening it changes no pixel). Lightroom AI masks still need the photo opened (see Known gaps).
- **Export metadata** (no version key): recipes that strip metadata, including Fast (Web) and your own recipes, now keep creator, copyright and usage terms. Pixels are unchanged.
- **Ratings** (no version key): photos whose metadata you have not changed take the rating the camera wrote into the file (read once when the library is opened).

### Known gaps

These are measured shortfalls against the better of the leading professional RAW editors; they stay open until they are closed by measurement.

- **Batch export** is about 2× slower: 20 RAWs to a web JPEG and a 16-bit TIFF (38 files) take about 17 s, in Lightroom 8 s and in Capture One 9 s on the same Mac; while you keep editing during a batch, Capture One exports 3–4× as many files in the same time. The GPU render is the slowest stage.
- **Import** of 200 RAW+JPEG pairs from an exFAT card with backup takes 27 s (Lightroom 11.5 s, Capture One 23.7 s), and the import dialog has no metadata preset for creator and copyright yet (set them after the import).
- **Culling**: the next photo after a rating key shows after 0.51 s (median; Capture One 0.10 s, Lightroom 0.35 s); there is no auto-advance after rating yet.
- **AI masks**: the first Subject mask after a fresh install takes 13.7 s (the model loads; Lightroom 7.9 s), later ones are fast. AI masks pasted onto other photos or imported from a Lightroom catalog are computed when each photo is opened in Develop: a photo with pasted AI selections cannot be exported before that, and imported Lightroom AI masks have no effect in its exports until then.
- **Highlights −100 in dark scenes** (lamps, candles, lit windows at night): Camera Raw darkens broad bright areas 1.3–3× further on 6 of 7 dark test frames; Rawtonic reaches at least Capture One's depth on every frame.
- **Moving files**: moves to network drives (SMB and other network shares) are refused; whole folders cannot be moved to exFAT drives on macOS (their photos can); a session's photos cannot be moved out of their session (move the whole session folder, or use a catalog).
- **Pen pressure** is built into the brush, but the macOS and Linux webviews report a tablet pen as a mouse, so a pen paints at constant pressure for now.
- **Contribution channels are new**: problem reports, crash reports, camera test results and usage statistics have had little use outside testing. The removal of paths, names, serial numbers and positions from report texts is best effort — check the exact text the review dialog shows before you send.
- **AI Denoise** is about 1.5× slower than Camera Raw's Denoise (about 10 s vs 6.5 s for 24 MP on the same Mac) and leaves a higher lightness error at the same noise level (2.21 vs 1.69); on Linux it takes about 35 s per 24 MP photo on the GPU.
- **Tethering** on the GFX 50R: the new photo is on screen 2.4 s after the shot, in Capture One 2.1 s (on the a7 III, a7 IV and X-H2S Rawtonic takes 1.1–1.5 s, Capture One 1.2–1.6 s). Tethering as a whole is experimental; Fujifilm live view does not work yet and Canon cameras are not offered.
- **Auto straighten**: on 51 test photos its median error is lower than Lightroom's Auto (0.06° vs 0.18°), but its 90th percentile is slightly behind (0.46° vs 0.42°). Straightening several selected photos at once is not available yet.
- **Layers**: at most 32 visible layers take effect; adding or showing a 33rd is refused with a message.
- **Colour noise reduction** still leaves more colour specks than the better editor in each ISO band from 1600 to 6400 and more than Capture One at Color 100, and fine colour detail at ISO 3200 and above is below the best of them.
- **Chromatic aberration and fringes** remain above the best reference on a few cameras (e.g. Lumix GX850, TZ202, Canon EOS M50 II); the automatic axial CA correction adds 5–18 % to export time on the photos measured.
- **Sharpening**: Masking sharpens low-contrast photos less than Camera Raw; JPEGs at Amount 120–150 get 12–19 % less fine detail than in Camera Raw; Camera Raw's strongest settings (150) are out of reach on some photos.
- **Clarity**: halos 6–15 px beside narrow bright edges remain above the better editor's on 6 of 10 comparable photos.
- Nikon High Efficiency NEFs don't open yet; imports from Lightroom and Capture One don't carry every adjustment over.
- **Linux**: tested on an NVIDIA graphics card under KDE Wayland, on an AMD laptop, and with exports on Ubuntu, Debian and Fedora; other graphics cards and desktops may show problems. The very first Develop frame after an install or driver update takes about 3 s on NVIDIA.

### Details

- **Report a problem** (Help menu on macOS, the … menu in Library and Develop): your description plus, each switchable, system details (graphics card), the last 200 lines of the log (editable), the camera make, model and file type of the open photo, a screenshot of the window (macOS, off unless ticked) and the photo's file itself (off unless ticked; the dialog warns that it keeps all its metadata and may show people). **Crash report on the next start**: when Rawtonic stops on an error in its own code or a fatal signal, it keeps a crash record in its settings folder and asks once at the next start whether to send it ("Never" deletes the records and stops recording; Preferences turns it back on). Every send first shows the exact text ("Review what will be sent"), goes through Rawtonic's own relay without an account, and gives a report id to delete it; reports are deleted after 90 days. Every dialog can instead copy the report or open GitHub's public issue form with the text shown first (no screenshot or file). Paths, user and computer names, serial numbers, e-mail and IP addresses and GPS positions are removed from the text on a best-effort basis ([Privacy](PRIVACY.md), ADR 0017).
- Anonymous weekly usage statistics, opt-in (Preferences › General, off by default): while on, Rawtonic counts per ISO week which camera models were opened (names from the decoder's camera list only), which tools from a fixed list were used (yes/no), and the app version, system and graphics card vendor; each finished week is sent once at a later start, without any id. *Show what is sent* lists every pending week in plain words or as the exact text; switching off deletes what was kept.
- Moving a session's photos to a folder outside the session (Move to Folder, paste after cut, on the same drive or to another) is refused before anything moves, with a message saying so: the session keeps only photos inside its folder, so they went offline there while their files showed in the new folder as loose photos without their ratings and edits. Move the whole session folder to the drive, or add the drive's folder to a catalog, whose photos may live anywhere. Photos still move within their session, into a session, and anywhere from a catalog.
- The tether panel's **Camera test** runs a guided test with your body (settings read and changed back, autofocus, single shots and a burst with timings, a shot on the body, cable out and back in, release from the camera's transfer queue) and builds an anonymous report (model, firmware, system, app version, results; no serial number, file names or folders), which *Send result…* sends through the review dialog, or copies, or opens as a GitHub camera report. The panel shows each camera's support level from the compatibility list, also before connecting. A RAW that fails to decode, or shows only its embedded preview, offers a guide to sharing a sample on raw.pixls.us (opened in the browser; Rawtonic uploads nothing there) or a problem report with that file; formats no decoder reads are not offered camera help.
- Export recipes that strip metadata, including Fast (Web) and existing user recipes, now retain creator, copyright and usage terms. Contact details (email, phone, website and postal address) remain omitted unless explicitly enabled in Export > Metadata (CLI: `--keep-creator-contact`). GPS, camera and serial details remain stripped. Recipe files are not migrated or rewritten; export pixels are unchanged. Collages keep their previous policy: no source metadata, with AI marking when needed.
- Built-in LUT labels use descriptive names in all interface languages; saved LUT paths and colour data are unchanged. On macOS, plain Delete/Backspace opens the Library delete dialog, and choosing a crop ratio returns focus to the photograph.
- Multi view zooms sooner: the selected photo's 100 % view 0.98 → 0.67 s and a locked 100 % zoom of 12 photos 4.6 → 2.5 s on a Mac mini M4 Pro (decodes it already holds are reused while they match the current settings). A zoom right after entering shows each cell's region from the reduced copy it was developed from within about 0.5–0.7 s, until the full-resolution region replaces it (still decode-bound, about 5 s); the zoom's own full decodes are kept for the background previews instead of being decoded again.
- Export: Return in *New recipe name* saves the recipe (it started an export of the selection), and the Subfolder field no longer capitalises what you type.
- Photos imported from a Lightroom catalog render their gradients, brushes and colour ranges, their spots and their red eye corrections without being opened: the import records each photo's orientation, and exports, the CLI and thumbnails place them as the editor does (opening the photo changes no pixel). Lightroom's Red Eye and Pet Eye corrections are imported. AI masks still wait for the photo to be opened, and the import says how many photos have them.
- Photos can be moved to exFAT drives on macOS (card readers, many external SSDs), which were refused: macOS cannot rename a file there without risking replacing another, so each file is created under its final name only if that name is free, its creation recorded before a byte is written, then copied, synced and read back there. The originals go to the Trash only after every copy is verified. Such a file is visible while it is copied, so Rawtonic never deletes it again: if the move fails, is stopped or is interrupted by a crash, the copy stays, complete or not, beside the untouched original, and the message and the operation's note name it; a notice lists such copies with Show in Finder and Move to Trash (drag it out of the Trash to get it back; offered only for the copies that move made, and not when the original is gone). Remove it before moving that photo there again. Another program writing into that new file during the copy can lose what it wrote: a write into a part already copied makes the check fail (the copy stays, the original is untouched), while a write ahead of the copy is overwritten without trace and the move completes normally (ADR 0016). Whole folders cannot be moved to such a drive (their photos can).
- Stronger sharpening of JPEGs and other non-RAW photos above Amount 40 (recipe `sharpenVersion` 6, new edits): measured against Camera Raw on the same JPEG pixels, fine detail on held-out frames went from 10 / 22 / 31 % below Camera Raw's to 5 / 12 / 19 % below at Amount 80 / 120 / 150, with halos still below Camera Raw's. RAW photos and amounts up to 40 render as with version 5; 450 of 450 full-resolution exports of older recipes byte-identical.
- The Sharpening Amount slider goes to 150 for sharpening responses 2 and up (Camera Raw's range; layers keep −100…100), and Lightroom's Sharpness imports 1:1 up to 150.
- Sharpening Masking follows Camera Raw's ramp (recipe `sharpenVersion` 5, new edits): less sharpening on flat noise, more on moderate texture; the texture error against Camera Raw fell from 0.123 to 0.098 on held-out frames. Without Masking it renders as version 4; 272 of 272 full-resolution exports of older recipes byte-identical.
- **Red eye and pet eye correction**: a mode of the spot tool (Remove, Heal, Clone, Red eye). A click searches the circle around it for the pupil, a drag sizes the circle; the pupil is found by classic image processing (no AI model) and fitted as an ellipse, and slivers and red surfaces are refused. Pupil Size and Darken per eye; pet eyes keep a catchlight. Corrections are stored per photo (`adjustments.redEyes`) in the photo as decoded, so turns, flips, straightening, lens and transform corrections keep them on the eye, and preview and export share them. They are not copied with settings (like spots).
- Rawtonic checks GitHub for a new release at startup at most once per day, including pre-releases on alpha/beta builds. Turn this off in Preferences, choose whether to include pre-releases, or use **Check now**. A small notice links to the release page; Rawtonic never downloads or installs updates itself. See [Privacy](PRIVACY.md) for what the check sends.
- Copy, paste and sync offer Lens Correction again: a lens choice pasted onto the open photo applies at once, and its lens data are looked up for that photo (also an offline one, from its recorded camera facts) as their own part of the paste's undo step; an export started before the lookup finishes resolves it itself.
- Clarity leaves fewer halos beside narrow bright edges such as slats, balusters, window frames and branches against the sky (recipe `clarityVersion` 2, new edits): the detail band is measured against an edge-aware base. At Clarity +100 the dark ring 1–6 px from such edges is 19–68 % of version 1's on 6 held-out photos and lower on all 11 measured, at or below the better of Camera Raw and Capture One on 3 of the 6; texture gain stays within a few percent of version 1. The Clarity version travels with Clarity through presets, paste and *Edit all selected*. Saved edits render as before.
- LUTs: Amount goes to 200 % (above 100 % the LUT's change is extended, kept inside the colour cube); importing a folder or several files makes a pack, grouped in the LUT picker; .cube files with 1D tables, a 1D shaper before the 3D table, or other input ranges are read (a .cube the earlier reader took is read exactly as before). Files that cannot be imported are listed with the reason in your interface language. Imports run off the main thread.
- The first frames after opening a photo come sooner on integrated graphics: background previews of the neighbouring photos wait while the open photo is decoded or rendered and give way to slider and brush drags within one tile of their work. On an AMD Radeon 680M laptop (Linux), the first frame after a first start 4.06–4.70 → 3.42–3.50 s, and input to preview on the first drag after opening (median) 33–41 → 11–14 ms. Exports byte-identical.
- A batch export no longer freezes the Develop view: exports render on a GPU processor of their own, wait between tiles while a Develop frame runs, and their CPU work runs at lower priority. Measured during a batch export on a laptop: 42–58 screen updates per Exposure drag, the last 10–52 ms after release (Lightroom 40–47 and up to 310 ms, Capture One 11–18 and up to 2.1 s). The export processor is released when memory runs short. Slider drags with several photos selected in multi view commit at intervals instead of on every move. Exports byte-identical.
- Moves to another drive keep extended attributes: on macOS Finder tags and comments, resource forks and the other attributes a program may set, on Linux the user attributes (`user.*`), for photos and for moved folders and everything in them. They are copied with each file, read back from the copy and compared, and the original goes to the Trash only while its attributes still match its copy's. A drive that cannot keep them (FAT or exFAT on Linux) refuses the move before anything is copied, naming the file and its attributes; the original stays where it is (ADR 0016).
- Colour noise reduction leaves fewer colour specks at its default (recipe `colorNoiseVersion` 10, new edits and never-edited RAWs): each pixel's filtered colour is now checked against its neighbours' plain filtered colours only, no longer against the side of a colour edge its own noisy colour picked, with a margin of 1.25 instead of 1.5 times the noise. At Color 25 the coarser scales keep a little colour noise, and in flat areas the filter kept single pixels' noisy colour within a range that included their own choice; those pixels no longer stand out. On 55 high-ISO RAWs, colour specks fell from 7.5 to 5.0 per million pixels at Color 25 (Capture One's default and Camera Raw 5.4), from 4.2 to 3.5 at Color 50 (Camera Raw 4.7) and from 4.1 to 3.1 at Color 100 (Camera Raw 4.6, Capture One at 100 1.4), with more specks on at most 2 of the 55 files; colour edges stayed within 0.12 % of version 9 in every ISO band, the error against clean ground-truth frames (RawNIND) fell or stayed in every cell, and fine colour detail fell by about 1 % at ISO 1600–2500 and 6400. Per ISO band still above the better editor from ISO 1600 to 6400 at every Color (below both from 12800 up), and above Capture One at Color 100 overall. Saved edits render exactly as before; at Color 0 version 10 renders as version 9.
- Folders can be moved with everything in them: **Move Folder…** in a folder's menu in Folders picks where to. On the same drive it is a rename; to another drive the Library asks first, naming the folder, its photos, files and size, the drive and its free space. The whole folder, files the library does not know included, is copied next to its new place, every file synced, read back from the drive and compared byte for byte, and then the folder appears under its name at once, never replacing anything. The original folder goes to the Trash of its drive only while it still holds exactly what was copied; one that changed meanwhile, or whose drive has no Trash, stays where it is and the Library says so. The catalog, previews and albums follow, as for a renamed folder. A crash, a failure or Stop before the folder appears changes nothing; Undo copies the folder back as it is then (edits and files added since included) and Redo moves it again. Folders with links or other drives in them, folders holding a session or catalog, and moves of a folder into or out of a session are refused (ADR 0016).
- Library: catalog folders are grouped by drive (network shares labelled as such), and drive classifications are kept and refreshed. Failures of file operations are translated, and file sizes in export estimates follow your locale.
- Film grain looks the same on every graphics card (recipe `grainVersion` 2: new grain and Lightroom and Capture One imports): version 1's pattern differed in 89–93 % of pixels between two graphics cards. Saved grain keeps version 1.
- Compare a snapshot with the current edit in Before/After (F12, split and held peek): the snapshot renders as its export would, with its own layers, masks and AI bitmaps. Snapshots framed or developed differently (crop, geometry, lens, AI Denoise) are not offered for comparison yet and say why.
- Sharpening Radius below 1 and Masking work as in Camera Raw on low-contrast photos (recipe `sharpenVersion` 4, new edits and never-edited RAWs; from Radius 1 up without Masking it renders exactly as before). Below Radius 1 the Threshold now judges the finer kernel's detail as if it were Radius 1's, so low-contrast texture is no longer shut out: at Radius 0.5 and Amount 40 the fine-detail gain against Camera Raw went from 15 % low (median of 8 public sample RAWs; Canon R5 −42 %, R6 −57 %) to −3…+3 % on every file, also on the 4 files held out from tuning (R6 −57 % → 0 %), with halos still at or below Camera Raw's and flat-area noise below it on 7 of 8 files (Nikon Z6III 1.07 against 1.06); the medium band is now 7–15 % stronger than Camera Raw's at Radius 0.5–0.75 (median). Masking reads a wider neighbourhood that tells texture from noise: at Masking 50 it keeps 82–94 % of the texture sharpening on contrasty photos (before 77–88 %, Camera Raw 84–92 %) with flat-area noise sharpened less than Camera Raw does, and on the low-contrast R5 frame it keeps 7 % instead of removing all sharpening (Camera Raw 21–23 %). On Radius and Masking settings within the slider range, the settings outside ±10 % of Camera Raw's fine detail fell from 26 to 10 of 48. Masking costs about 1.2–1.7 ms more per 2304×1728 preview frame on an M4 Pro (+16–23 %; Detail 100 with Masking 80: 9.6–10.5 ms); other settings cost the same within measurement noise. Saved edits render exactly as before.
- Save & Relaunch in Preferences (after changing a setting that needs a restart) restarts the app again: it quit and did not start again on every platform, because the app ends its process when asked to exit, before Tauri would start the new one. The new instance now starts once the old one has given up its single-instance claim (checked on Linux: before, the app was gone; now it comes back with the setting applied).
- Keyboard: tool fields take focus correctly, the viewer's menus open from the keyboard, and the studio's import controls and focus order were fixed.
- Undo and redo of copy-moves restore files with safe permissions: a file's recorded permissions are kept, never widened, also for older journals.
- Capture details for more files: CR3 and CRM files of any size (before, those over 16 MiB got none), and DNGs whose tags lie after the image data, as merges write them (also past 1 GiB). The rating the camera wrote into a file becomes the photo's while its metadata is untouched. Photos read without a capture date are read again once (library schema 13 and 14).
- Photos can be moved to a folder on another drive (Move to Folder, paste after cut), with their XMP, editor sidecars and virtual copies. The Library asks first, naming the drive, how much is copied and how much is free there. Each file is copied next to its new place, synced, read back from the drive and compared byte for byte with the original, then given its name without ever replacing a file; only then do the originals go to the Trash of their drive. An original that changed meanwhile, or whose drive has no Trash, stays where it is and the Library says so. A progress notice shows how far the copy is, with Stop. A crash at any point loses nothing: before the copies are all in place the move is rolled back at the next start; after, it is finished and the originals not yet in the Trash are kept. Undo copies the photos back the same way (edits made since included) and Redo moves them again. Refused for now: network drives (ADR 0016). exFAT/FAT drives on macOS, refused at first because they cannot rename without risking replacing a file, take photos since the in-place copy described above; whole folders still cannot move there (on Linux both work).
- Thin dark lines (slats, mesh, wires, twigs) are no longer taken for dead photosites (recipe `renderVersion` 3, new edits and never-edited RAWs): their red and blue samples were lifted to the samples beside the line, which drew magenta and green stripes on X-Trans photos. Saved edits keep the old test; previews of the older variant are not shown for a new recipe.
- One GPU render for all recipes of a photo: recipes of one photo share the main render pass and each gets only a small final encoding pass for its colour space and precision. The 20-RAW set with a web JPEG and a 16-bit TIFF recipe (38 files) exports in 16.5–18.5 s instead of 22.0–22.8 s on an M5 Pro (Lightroom 8 s, Capture One 9 s), every file byte-identical; renders a photo keeps are bounded to 768 MiB of pixels.
- **Export queue panel**, reachable from the top bar at any time: jobs newest first with state, progress and time left; pause, cancel, resume, retry, remove and reveal per job; hold or start the queue; per-photo rows with the stage (reading, AI Denoise with its share of tiles, rendering, masks), duration, failure reason and Show.
- Snapshots and History sit in a dock under the scrolling tools in every Develop tab (up to 40 % of the column), Snapshots open from the start; before, the Snapshots tool sat folded at the end of each long tab.
- Library metadata lists: Shift-click chooses every value from the last one clicked (a range of ISO, focal lengths or dates), and capture dates are listed by year, a year chosen in one click with its months on demand (ISO 800 to 3200 in 2024: 3 actions instead of 15). Smart album counts follow a rating at once (were 0.42 s late).
- The filmstrip scrolls horizontally with a vertical wheel or trackpad gesture.
- Luminance curve: the tone curve has a Luminance channel next to RGB, R, G and B (recipe `curves.luminance`, for the photo and in layers). It changes the brightness of each colour by the colour's luminance and keeps its saturation and hue, so an S-curve adds contrast without the extra saturation and hue shifts an RGB curve brings (Capture One renders, S-curve on saturated colours: its RGB curve raises saturation ×1.14–1.24 and turns hues by 2.4–2.7°, its Luma curve keeps saturation within ×0.95–1.01 and hue within 1°; Lightroom has no such curve). It acts after the RGB and channel curves; lifting its black end lifts black to grey. Capture One styles bring their Luma curve into it instead of onto the RGB curve; the import report says that Capture One curves come over approximately (it applies them to γ 1.8 values: a few levels off its renders). The RGB curve's tab is now labelled RGB. Recipes without the channel render byte-identically.
- Point curves act in the wide working space, as Camera Raw's do (curve `version` 3 inside the recipe's `curves`, written by Lightroom imports and by edits of neutral curves): the RGB and the red, green and blue curves are applied to the colour in ProPhoto primaries with the sRGB tone curve instead of to display values clipped to sRGB. Measured on two photos rendered by Camera Raw, that space predicts its curves pixel by pixel within 0.06 levels for a red curve (display values: 9.6 levels; a red curve 128 → 160 turns grey 128 into R 186 / G 119, which display values cannot give) and within 0.66 instead of 1.46 levels for the RGB curve on saturated colours, so Lightroom's `ToneCurvePV2012Red/Green/Blue` import 1:1. Colours beyond sRGB now go through the curves themselves instead of being carried past them. Curves saved before keep their look, also when a point of them is moved; neutral curves render as before (exports byte-identical). No measurable GPU cost (6 MP display frame 6.7 against 7.0 ms on an M4 Pro).
- Tone targets (the targeted tone and region curve) work with layer curves, LUTs, clipping warnings, soft proofing, colour-range and layer solo views, and with curves that fall or turn back: the level under the pointer is now rendered for the photo's curves (the photo without them and without what follows them, 4–10 ms per press) instead of traced back from the displayed colour, where these were declined.
- Exporting photos with several ticked recipes is faster: the recipes are queued together and each photo is loaded, lens-corrected and uploaded to the GPU once for all of them, then each recipe renders, sizes, sharpens, watermarks and encodes its own file (before, every recipe decoded and developed the photo again). Up to four photos are worked on at once (before, at most one per 4 GB of free memory), and no further photo starts while less than 2 GB of memory is free. 20 Nikon Z RAWs with a web JPEG and a 16-bit Adobe RGB print TIFF recipe (38 files) take 23.6–24.9 s instead of 31.3–34.9 s on an M5 Pro (two A/B/B/A rounds); every file is byte-identical to before, and a single photo exports in the same time (1.63–1.67 s). Lightroom Classic and Capture One take 8–9 s for this set; the GPU render, once per recipe, is now the slowest stage (about 17 s of the 24).
- Point curves with more than 16 points render whole (curve `version` 2 inside the recipe's `curves`, written by new edits, Lightroom imports and every curve edit, so it travels with the curves through copy, paste, presets and sync): curves imported from Lightroom with many points were cut after their 16th point (every tone above it held at that point's level, up to 34 levels off on a 20-point curve); the GPU now reads such curves from a table that holds the exact curve every quarter input level (on 20-point test curves within 0.01–0.11 of an output level, always under a quarter level; a jump within one input level is spread over a quarter input level). Saved edits render exactly as before, including those with more than 16-point curves (measured on an M4 Pro against the previous main: 208 of 208 full-resolution 16- and 8-bit exports of 13 public RAWs with 8 saved recipes byte-identical, among them a 20-point curve without the key; GPU preview equivalence tests pass); re-importing the Lightroom settings or touching the curve renders them whole. A 20-point curve exports in the time of a 16-point one (24 MP, 16-bit: 2.69 s against 2.83 s, median of 4).
- Lightroom tone curves import as Camera Raw draws them: Camera Raw interpolates `ToneCurvePV2012` points with a natural cubic spline on display values (measured 2026-10-21 on two photos), which overshoots between steep points where Rawtonic's curve stays flat; imports add points (on whole levels) on Lightroom's spline until Rawtonic's curve is within a quarter level of it, except next to steep transitions (a rise of many levels within a few input levels), which whole-level points cannot follow closer (2–12 levels off there in the tests; the import logs it) (an overshooting curve: 128 → 135 at input 96, Camera Raw 135). The import no longer lifts shadow points of the curve on its own.
- Targeted adjustment for the region curve: drag up or down on the photo to move the region (Shadows, Darks, Lights or Highlights) of the tone under the pointer, as Lightroom's parametric curve does; one drag is one undo step. The tone curve graph now draws the curve the renderer applies instead of straight lines between the points.
- **Copy, paste and sync** with one picker for Copy and Sync: tool groups by section, the photo's changed ones marked, local layers one by one; the choice is remembered (Option skips the picker), the clipboard holds exactly what was chosen, and *Previous* pastes the settings of the photo open before. Pasted layers join each photo's own (the same layer is replaced); AI selections never take another photo's bitmap: they wait for their own detection, which runs when the photo is opened, and its export fails until then.
- Sharpening follows the photo's brightness, as Camera Raw's does (recipe `sharpenVersion` 3, new edits, reset photos and never-edited RAWs): it fades out towards the shadows and is up to twice as strong in the highlights, at every Amount including the default 40. Before, it sharpened all tones alike, so shadow noise was sharpened up to five times as strongly as in Camera Raw and bright detail in dark areas got dark rims. On 8 public sample RAWs measured against Camera Raw at Amount 40–150 and Detail 0–100, the settings whose halos exceeded Camera Raw's fell from 24 of 112 to 1 (at Amount up to 100, the slider's range: 9 of 64 to 1, at most 0.3 Oklab L ×100 above), and the sharpening of dark flat areas came down to Camera Raw's level (Canon R6 at Amount 80 / Detail 100: noise ×6.0 → ×2.6, Camera Raw ×3.8). On 6 further high-ISO RAWs held out from fitting, every halo is at or below Camera Raw's (before up to +2.8), and flat-area noise at Amount 80 / Detail 100 is at or below Camera Raw's on all 14 files; it is higher than before in bright flat areas of some noisy photos (ISO 12800 A7C without noise reduction ×1.6), where Camera Raw sharpens more still, and with the default noise reduction it changes by ×0.75–1.04 at the default sharpening. Texture at the default sharpening stays at Camera Raw's or above (×1.20 of it, before ×1.21); at Amount 120–150 the strongest fine detail Rawtonic reaches is lower on some photos. Same GPU cost (preview frame and full-resolution export within ±1.5 % on an M4 Pro). Saved edits render exactly as before (136 of 136 full-resolution exports byte-identical).
- Masks and layers: a new linear or radial gradient is drawn with one drag on the photo (a click keeps the default shape); clicking into a value field selects its value; the layer list shows about six rows and scrolls, so the sliders below stay in reach with many layers.
- **Named snapshots** kept by the catalog: take one of the current edit or of any history step, rename, update with the current edit or delete it, see thumbnails developed from it, and restore it as one named, undoable history step. A photo in no library keeps them in its sidecar; XMP sidecars are unchanged (ADR 0015).
- **Catalog search and smart albums**: the Library's search box finds part of any word in file names and folders, the photo's searchable metadata, camera and lens (`0123` finds `DSC00123.ARW`), and new metadata lists filter by camera, lens, focal length, aperture, ISO, shutter speed and capture date, answered by the catalog. Smart albums hold nested all/any/none rule groups with a live match count; *Save as smart album* from the filter bar; they refresh after catalog writes. Capture facts are recorded at import, and a background pass reads them once for photos already in the library. CLI: `rawtonic search`, `rawtonic facts` (ADR 0014).
- Automatic correction of axial chromatic aberration for new RAW edits (recipe `fringeVersion` 4, new RAW edits and never-edited RAWs; noisy photos included): purple and blue fringes on dark branches and letters against a bright sky, and green or yellow rims on the dark side of bright edges, up to about 8 pixels wide, now take the colour of the edge's two sides mixed at the pixel's own brightness, as a blur the same in every colour would give. Real colour is kept where a channel lies beyond both sides (a coloured stroke along an edge), where the brightness rises in two steps between the sides (a band of another colour, a light leaf before the sky), and where the colour differs in a hue other than the purple-to-blue and yellow-to-green families by more than about twice the photo's noise (an orange glow round a lamp). On 41 public sample RAWs the share of bright-edge pixels with unexplained colour fell or stayed equal on every file (Canon EOS M50 II 2.10 → 0.80 %, Panasonic S1R II 5.39 → 0.48 %, Fujifilm X-H1 1.86 → 1.01 %, Nikon Z6 II 1.31 → 0.45 %), and on 21 further held-out RAWs it fell on 14 (Sony A9 2.59 → 1.01 %), stayed equal on 4 and rose on 3 (Panasonic S5 0.11 → 0.12 %, Sony A7C 3.59 → 3.61 %, and a Nikon D7200 frame with only 270 bright-edge pixels, 21 → 30 of them); purple fringe left in hand-checked fringe regions fell on all 10 (X-H1 1.57 → 0.77, S1R II 1.46 → 0.49, Panasonic G7 0.60 → 0.14), now at or under the better of Capture One and Camera Raw at their defaults on 7 of 10 (before 4). Legitimate purple and green detail at edges kept 98–100 % of its colour except where its edges were fringed (a test chart's blue patch edges 85–91 %, a crane's purple-fringed red lattice 87 %). The pass runs once per photo geometry; on an M4 Pro it adds about 0.07 s (24 MP, few edges) to 0.15–0.3 s (24–80 MP, many edges) to a whole export (5–18 % on the photos measured), about 40 % less than when it was first built, with identical results. Saved edits render exactly as before.
- Exports, imports and tethered photos onto exFAT and FAT drives on macOS no longer fail with "Operation not supported (os error 45)": where a drive has no exclusive rename, files are copied into a newly created file that never replaces another; ingest checks the copy's SHA-256 again, and a copy that fails is kept and named, never removed by name.
- Tethering: on Sony bodies the next shot waits for the last one's files (five shots fired within 0.2 s gave one photo and four timeouts on an a7 III), the camera test picks a plain ISO from 100 up, and repeated camera library messages are logged at most once a minute.
- **RAW+JPEG pairs**: a RAW and a JPEG or HEIF of the same name come in as one photo, the JPEG copied verified beside the RAW under its new name and moved and trashed with it (an option imports such JPEGs as separate photos); duplicate hints know these companions. Card sidecars match in any letter case. **Every copy import is a job** in the library: a quit, crash or card taken out is finished when the library opens again, without duplicates, also after the closed session was moved (ADR 0013).
- The edge-fringe pass of saved edits (`fringeVersion` 1 and 2) is about a third faster with exactly the same pixels (version 2 on a 24 MP photo 0.07 s instead of 0.10 s on an M4 Pro; version 1 runs the same code).
- Pen pressure for the brush: with *Pen pressure* Size (on by default) or Flow, each point of a pen stroke keeps its pressure and scales the dab's size and/or flow; Auto Mask weighs the stroke by its flow. Mouse and touch strokes carry no pressure and render as before. The macOS and Linux webviews currently report tablet pens as a mouse, so pressure from a real pen is not picked up there yet.
- Faster first frames: the first frame of a never-opened RAW on Linux 1.2 → 0.75 s (the quick draft is developed before the full development, which follows 0–110 ms later); a cold NVIDIA driver cache compiles the first display shader in 2.9 s instead of 4.4 s (exports keep their code); frames built from a reduced copy no longer measure chromatic aberration on that copy; 16-bit exports no longer wait for the 8-bit pipeline; a first frame drawn before the photo's shader variant is ready keeps the noise reduction passes.
- Colour noise reduction leaves fewer colour specks along edges (recipe `colorNoiseVersion` 9, new edits and never-edited RAWs): each pixel's filtered colour is now kept within the range of its neighbourhood's filtered colours plus about the noise, so a pixel whose noisy colour the filter kept next to a brightness or colour edge (a dark pixel by a bright border, the rim of a colour edge) no longer stands out as a coloured speck; a coloured point far beyond its surroundings (a small light, real fine detail) keeps its colour. On 55 high-ISO RAWs, colour specks fell from 10.3 to 7.5 per million pixels at Color 25, from 5.8 to 4.2 at Color 50 (Camera Raw 4.7, Capture One's default 5.4) and from 5.3 to 4.1 at Color 100 (Camera Raw 4.6); colour edges stayed within 0.08 % of version 8 in every ISO band, the error against clean ground-truth frames (RawNIND) and the colour texture kept rose or fell in no cell by more than 0.5 %, and fine colour detail fell by at most 0.6 % (ISO 1600–2500 and 6400). Still above both editors at Color 25 (5.4) and Capture One at Color 100 (1.4); wrong-side pixels along colour edges and 2-pixel colour blocks remain. Saved edits render exactly as before; at Color 0 version 9 renders as version 8.
- Cleaner edges between two saturated colours (recipe `fringeVersion` 3, new RAW edits and never-edited RAWs): where the demosaic leaves red and blue a pixel off green, the edge between a yellow object and grey got a tinted line and the edge between cyan and magenta chart patches a light seam neither colour has. The edge-fringe pass now also moves such pixels onto the line between the two colours a blur would follow (in linear light), only in small windows that hold a strong edge and two colours, and only for colours a mix of the two could make, so a third colour (a thin stripe, a small patch on the edge, a pink flower on a green leaf) keeps its colour. On 34 low-noise public sample RAWs, Bayer and X-Trans, the share of bright-edge pixels with unexplained colour fell on every file (Nikon D100 1.97 → 0.54 %, Olympus E-400 1.74 → 0.50 %, Panasonic TZ202 0.36 → 0.09 %, Fujifilm X-H1 4.72 → 2.07 %, X-T5 2.46 → 0.94 %, Sony ZV-1 4.75 → 1.80 %); colour lost on clearly coloured pixels stayed under 0.2 % beyond 0.04 Oklab chroma, legitimate purple and green detail at edges kept 98–100 % of its colour. Noisy photos (about ISO 3200 and up) render as before (the stage found colour lines in the noise there). The pass runs once per photo geometry, not per slider change; on an M4 Pro it takes about 0.24 s for a 24–36 MP photo (whole exports 3–9 % longer than with the previous version). Saved edits render exactly as before.
- Colour noise reduction removes more lone coloured specks (recipe `colorNoiseVersion` 8, new edits and never-edited RAWs): whether such a pixel is a coloured point that stands out in brightness is now judged after the luminance noise reduction, so a pixel that is darker or brighter than its neighbours only by noise loses its stray colour, while a point the luminance noise reduction keeps (a small light, real fine detail) keeps it. On 55 high-ISO RAWs, isolated colour specks fell from 14.3 to 10.3 per million pixels at Color 25 and from 8.0 to 5.8 at Color 50 (5.6 → 5.3 at 100; Capture One's default 5.4, Camera Raw at Color 50 4.7), with more specks on at most 1 of the 55 files; colour edges stayed within 0.04 % and colour detail within 0.3 % of version 7 in every ISO band, and the error against clean ground-truth frames (RawNIND) rose in no cell by more than 0.5 % (largest +0.007 %). Saved edits render exactly as before; at Color 0 or Luminance 0 version 8 renders as version 7.
- Three render fixes for new edits (recipe `renderVersion` 2; saved edits render as before): Clarity, Dehaze and Glow no longer leave faint seams every 2048 pixels on photos with a short side above 3456 pixels (their large blur stopped short of its radius at render-tile edges: up to 1,341 of 65,535 levels on a striped 5400-pixel test frame, now none); lens corrections and straightening keep colours beyond sRGB instead of clipping their negative channels (only where the photo has such colours); and with the Classic and AgX looks, switching Color Grading or Calibration off now turns them off.
- AI fixes: Subject, Sky, object and depth masks and face analysis are recomputed when AI Denoise or another decode option develops the photo again (they kept the earlier pixels' results); an object-mask encoding computed ahead stops when you switch photos or memory runs short; AI Denoise rejects tiles with invalid values from a GPU fault instead of rendering and caching them; clearing AI tags of session photos lasts; models download without deleting the old file first.
- Lens profiles from the lens database are looked up at the photo's f-number and scaled to its body's crop factor, and keep their whole lateral chromatic aberration correction. Canon and Fujifilm RAWs were looked up at the APEX aperture value (f/8 read as f/6, so the wrong vignetting row), CR2, CR3, RAF, ORF and RW2 files were taken for full frame (their EXIF had the focal length copied into the 35 mm equivalent), and the profiles' radius-dependent CA terms were dropped. On 62 public sample RAWs, 31 newly looked-up profiles change: on APS-C and Micro Four Thirds bodies the corner vignetting correction had been evaluated far outside the lens's measured radius (up to a brightness of 0, now −0.3 to −1.6 EV), and the distortion correction was scaled for the wrong frame (E-M1X with 12–40 mm at 12 mm: −23.9 % instead of +5.1 % radial scale at 60 % of the half-diagonal); 4,026 channel profiles had CA terms worth a median 0.5 px (up to 13 px) in a 24 MP corner. The Lens tool shows the same profile a save looks up. Saved edits keep the lens parameters they were saved with; the 35 mm equivalent in exports is the camera's own (or absent) for newly read EXIF.
- AI masks of new layers grow and feather from beyond the crop (recipe `maskVersion` 4): a cropped layer shows the uncropped photo's mask edge instead of one cut at the crop. Mask thumbnails are drawn with their layer's mask version.
- Adding, duplicating or showing a 33rd rendered layer is refused with a message (previews and exports apply at most 32); layers beyond the limit in older recipes are marked *Not applied*.
- Smaller fixes: a photo with AI-generated areas always keeps its AI marking in exports (the export fails if even that cannot be written), and a photo whose file was written before a cancel is recorded as done; face retouch is no longer dropped by vignetting, CA or fringe changes (new retouches; saved ones keep their rule); turning the photo in crop mode moves spots and patches with it; virtual-copy numbers in export names come from the file name only; a replaced XMP keeps its file permissions; a LUT file replaced under the same name is read again; Clear All Sidecar Files moves them to the Trash; Sky, Subject and Depth masks are only ever applied to the photo they were made for; in Develop the delete undo hint names the Library; hot folders and tethering finish their last photos when you switch libraries; slower work moved off the main thread.
- Fewer isolated dark and bright single-pixel specks in noisy photos (recipe `lumaNoiseVersion` 2, new edits): the luminance noise reduction keeps a pixel within the range of its eight neighbours plus half its noise, and the sharpening no longer lifts such noise-like extremes; a point that stands far out of the noise (a light, a star) is left alone. On 55 high-ISO RAWs (paired, same settings otherwise), isolated specks fell from 20.3 to 16.4 per million pixels at Color 25 (11.4 → 10.6 at 50, 12.5 → 8.2 at 100) and isolated lightness outliers by 28 %; colour edges are unchanged, and against clean ground-truth frames (RawNIND) the error is lower at 0.7–2 px (−0.8 %) and in flat areas (−3.4 %). The cost: about 1 % of the finest lightness texture (texture kept 0.7–2 px −1.3 %, 2–4 px error +0.5 %). Saved edits render exactly as before, and so do photos whose measured noise is at most 0.002 (most base-ISO photos; two ISO 50/100 test frames measured just above it and changed by at most 60/65535, below one 8-bit step); a preview frame takes about 0.2 ms more on the GPU.

## 0.1.0-alpha.3 — 2026-10-20

The third alpha: about a hundred changes since alpha.2. Tethered shooting arrives (experimental), local adjustments get finer masks and a faster brush, AI Denoise gets a new model, and colour noise reduction, fringe and chromatic aberration removal, highlight recovery, Auto white balance and sharpening were reworked and measured against the leading RAW editors.

### Highlights

- **Tethered shooting (experimental).** Connect a camera on USB, set its exposure, fire it from the app (button or ⌘K / Ctrl+K), focus, and see its live view; every shot lands in the session, verified and named by your template, with your next-capture adjustments, rating and keywords. A camera fault cannot crash the app (the camera library runs in its own helper process), and nothing on a memory card is deleted. Tested on Sony a7 III and a7 IV and Fujifilm X-H2S and GFX 50R on macOS. Other Sony and Fujifilm bodies may work but are untested; **Canon cameras are not offered yet**; Fujifilm live view does not work yet; on Linux the tether tool is included but has not been tried with a real camera.
- **Local adjustments.** 16-bit layer masks (smooth gradients without banding steps), a **Refine edge** slider for hair, fur and foliage, a better Auto Mask for brush strokes, and **Solo** (H or Option/Alt-click an eye) to see one layer or mask alone. Brush strokes follow the pointer more closely and stay quick on layers with many strokes; photos with 20–32 layers stay fluid while dragging sliders, and exports with many layers need about half the memory.
- **AI Denoise** uses a new, faster model for new edits (downloaded once, on first use; photos denoised before keep their model): about 10 s instead of 27–31 s for a 24 MP photo on our test Mac, and its amount now has the effect of the same number in Camera Raw's Denoise (50 = its default).
- **Auto straighten**: one click (crop tool *Auto*, Shift+R or the photo's context menu) levels the horizon or the verticals and crops so no empty corners show. It gives no answer rather than a wrong one when a photo has no clear lines.
- **Auto white balance** rebuilt: neutral in daylight and cool light, while sunsets, golden hour and lamplight keep their mood.
- **Cleaner colour**: colour noise reduction keeps colour edges and fine colour texture and removes lone coloured specks; Defringe removes only colour the edge does not explain; Remove chromatic aberration is on by default for RAWs whose camera or lens data has no correction of its own, and Panasonic cameras' own correction data are used.
- **Tones**: recovered highlights without pink, magenta or green casts; Shadows and Highlights act more like the leading editors; sharpening keeps halos in check at high amounts.
